# Changelog

All notable changes to Vesma (formerly Mnemos).

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [5.6.3] — 2026-10-06

### Fixed

- **`vesma service install` generated unit: `RuntimeDirectory=vesma` + writability probe (#509)** — `ProtectHome=read-only` makes `/run/user` read-only inside the unit namespace, so the canonical control-socket dir `${XDG_RUNTIME_DIR}/vesma` could not be bound (EROFS crash loop). systemd now creates it via `RuntimeDirectory=vesma` + `RuntimeDirectoryMode=0700` (works under `ProtectSystem=strict`); defense-in-depth: `resolve_runtime_dir()` probes writability (create+unlink — `os.access` cannot see RO mounts) and falls back to the sanctioned layout §3.6 dir `~/.local/state/vesma/run/` with a WARN when the candidate is unwritable.
- **`vesma service run` starts the mesh legs (unix + tcp) — serve() parity (#510)** — `ServiceApp` ran only the HTTP core, so the MnemosCore gRPC mesh transport never listened under the unit and federation degraded. The mesh wiring is now one shared code path with `serve()` (`backend.start_mesh_legs` / `stop_mesh_legs`): legs come up after the supervisor, stop on graceful + abnormal teardown with the control-socket file removed; a failed leg is SL §3.1 fail-fast. Side fix: the `lifespan` finally-clause nulls the `api.main` manager singleton on close, so a closed manager can never be handed out to a later lifespan cycle; the `meta_poller` page-timeout kill became best-effort — under service run the supervisor reaper owns `waitpid(-1)` and may reap the child first, so `ProcessLookupError` (ESRCH race) no longer surfaces as a bogus peer error.

### Added

- **ADR-0004 B0 — the decision-provider seam wired into the LIVE near-duplicate path (card `b0-provider-seam-wiring`; the B0 telemetry window prerequisite)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py` — a lazily built, once-per-manager provider seam `_decision_seam()` resolving the EXISTING `mnemos.decision_provider` config through `resolve_decision_provider` with the manager's LIVE embedder fingerprint (`_embedder_fingerprint` — the artifact pin-assert contract; off → None, any resolution failure → deterministic + `code=DECISION-E-SEAM class=provider-class` warn, ingest never blocked); the live call site is `_mint_relates_to_edges` — add-time auto-dedupe (ADR-0030 Decision 2), where the deterministic dup heuristic ran and NO provider call site existed before: the top-qualified pair gets ONE `is-duplicate` VERDICT probe (`IsDuplicateRequest`/`CanonState`, ADR-0037 R1 seam) — §8.3 verdict/action split: the provider answers verdicts only, the mint behavior (edges/weights/provenance) is byte-identical across configs (pinned by test); `MissingEvidenceError` skips quietly (unanswerable for every provider), any other provider exception degrades to the deterministic step rule with `code=DECISION-E-PROBE class=verdict-class` warn; the §3.9 DECISION line (`decision primitive=noul provider=… question=is-duplicate probability=…`) is emitted by the provider's own `evaluate` — the provider/confidence/is-duplicate fields the B0 collector joins (b0-telemetry-plan.md criteria 3/5); tests `tests/test_decision_seam_live.py` (new — 11: wiring pin, real-artifact probe over the REAL bundled embedder, mint-identity across configs, fail-open legs). Out of scope: Wave B arbiter (actionability), prod flag flip (TL+owner decision after merge).
- **ADR-0037 Wave A — `metadata.canon.lineage_marks` engine alignment: envelope allowlist, per-item validator, client strip** (`src/vesmaro/canon_validate.py` — `"lineage_marks"` joins all four per-type `ENVELOPE_ALLOWED_KEYS` sets (Д1: a canon-valid record carrying the marks array no longer fails the engine's own envelope gate with `CANON-E-ENVELOPE`) + `_lineage_violations` per-item pass enforcing the frozen lineage_marks.schema.json shape — non-empty array, `kind` ∈ {`same-message-other-envelope`, `merged-by-arbiter`, `split-from`} (`LINEAGE_MARK_KINDS` literal), `at` the ISO-8601 date-time pattern's shape, `ref` a non-empty string when present, `additionalProperties: false` per mark — failures surface as the new `CANON-E-LINEAGE` warn code through the EXISTING warn/strict gate, no new enforcement machinery (Д2) + `strip_client_lineage_marks` shared helper; `src/vesmaro/manager.py` — a client-supplied `lineage_marks` array strips on generic create/update (a separate pass AFTER the stamp strip; the minted checkpoint envelope stays merge-protected with its own marks); `src/vesmaro/cli/import_.py` — the untrusted JSON import strips marks inside a KEPT client-authored envelope (`--trusted-restore` keeps them — the migrate/backfill legit path). Rationale: marks are server-minted/arbitration-minted (W1–W3 only, ADR-0037 Д5) — a forged mark is input-forgery onto the future merge-arbiter's destructive fold; the same strip class as the checkpoint stamps. Integration pack warn-code tables updated (`integrations/instructions/vesma-canon-records.instructions.md`, `integrations/skills/vesma-canon-write.md`); tests `tests/test_lineage_marks_canon.py` (new — Д1/Д2/Д5 pins + regressions), `tests/test_canon_warn_validator.py` (code-catalog pin now seven codes). Wave B (the merge-arbiter itself: R1/R2 read sites, W1–W3 mint sites) stays out of scope.

### Documentation

- **Getting-started guides rewritten as the full user lifecycle (board card `svc-migration-docs-562`, docs wave A of the service migration)** — `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md` restructured around the utility-driven cycle (install → service → integration → doctor → updates → legacy cleanup): the `vesma service` verb set (`install|uninstall|status|health|start|stop|restart|logs|run`), the doctor family (`doctor fix|service|paths` — the `--fix` flag spelling demoted to a noted deprecated alias), `vesma update` subcommands (`check|apply|components|timer install|uninstall|status` replacing the deprecated flag forms), `vesma integration setup|update` as the only MCP path (manual MCP registration marked cancelled), `vesma completion` + the 5.6.2 `-h`-at-any-level feature, and two new sections — "Cleaning up old installations" (scenario A keeps the `~/.mnemos/` store and removes legacy pip dists / `mnemos-*` units / shell wrappers / old completion scripts; scenario B is the explicit irreversible full-removal path with an export-first warning) and "The legacy layer" (recognition signs + the legacy→canonical migration table from layout v1 spec §9). Every command verified against the live 5.6.2 `--help` surface.
- Manual procedures replaced with utility paths across the docs tree: `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/install.md` (the manual VS Code `mcp.json` block → `vesma integration setup`; `pip install --upgrade` → `vesma update check|apply`; container quick-start pinned to 5.6.2 with the `VESMA_API__` env spelling; a service-install section; `install.sh` dropped as a recommended channel), `docs/en|ru/index.md` (harness "fastest path" table → `vesma integration setup --target <name>`; the factually stale "bare `pip install vesma` is an unrelated project" warnings corrected), `docs/en|ru/user/integration-guide.md` (`vesma doctor --fix` → `vesma doctor fix`; the preset table → utility targets, OpenCode kept as the manual fallback), `integrations/mcp-presets.md` (prerequisite and Hermes `pip install mnemos-memory-server` → `pip install vesma`; the inverted PyPI-names warning fixed; the VS Code `mcp-setup.sh` scripted path replaced by `--target copilot`).
- README quick-start (EN/RU) restructured to install → service → integration → doctor, all through the utility: a new "Deploy the service" step (`vesma service install` + the systemd user unit), the podman quadlet block updated to the 5.6.2 image with the quadlet-derived unit name corrected, `--fix` → `vesma doctor fix`, and the extended-guide pointer updated to the lifecycle guide.

## [5.6.2] — 2026-10-06

### Added

- **`vesma ingest url URL` / `vesma ingest file PATH` — external-content ingest as canonical subcommands (board card `vesma-cli-w3-ingest`, wave W3 of `vesma-cli-architecture-rework`; standing design rule: subcommand = function (WHAT), flag = configuration (HOW))** (`src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`, `docs/en|ru/architecture/overview.md`, `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/backup-restore.md`, `docs/en|ru/admin/security.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`; tests `tests/test_cli.py` +15 new, 1 re-pinned) — `ingest url` fetches a web page, extracts the main text and saves it as a memory (the former `add --url` behavior; tags required per the tag contract); `ingest file` saves a local file's text content as a memory with `--title/--tags/--source` and supports `--dry-run` (context-filter preview without saving; bare `vesma ingest` shows the group help). Both canonical surfaces and the legacy flag forms share ONE save path in `main.py` — no logic duplication.

### Changed

- **`vesma add --url/--file` are hidden deprecated aliases (W3 alias cycle, design doc `docs/project/cli-architecture-rework.md` §2.5, §3; soft mode)** — the flag forms still work with identical behavior, but are hidden from `add --help` and print a one-line stderr hint (`[deprecated] \`vesma add --url URL\` is deprecated — use: vesma ingest url URL`; the W-C/W2 precedent, stdout stays clean for pipes/JSON). `vesma add <content>` remains the canonical quick-capture. Removal is not before 6.0. Historical edge semantics preserved: `add --url --dry-run` is still refused (exit 1), `add --file --dry-run` still previews; on the new `ingest file` surface a missing path is a clean exit-1 error instead of a traceback.

### Added

- **completion staleness detection — every generated completion script embeds the vesma version that wrote it, and `vesma doctor` warns when it goes stale** (`src/vesmaro/cli/completion.py` — `# vesma version: <v>` header line stamped into the bash/zsh/fish scripts, parsed by `_script_version`; `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py` — the Completion check now reads each existing script's stamp and returns WARN `stale completion script(s) … run \`vesma completion\` to refresh` when it differs from the running version; a pre-stamp (legacy) script counts as stale — the field case this closes: a stale installed script breaking at completion level 2 with no signal of why; the installer rewrites scripts on every run, so the fix is one command) — tests `tests/test_completion.py` (stamp present in all three scripts, parse helper round-trip), `tests/test_service_doctor.py` (synthetic stale script → WARN with fix command, fresh script → PASS).

### Changed

- **`vesma --install-completion` now installs Vesma's own description-aware completion (typer's builtin completion disabled on the root app)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/main.py` — `add_completion=False` + an explicit `--install-completion` root option delegating to the exact `vesma completion` path) — previously the most discoverable flag (root help) installed typer's generated scripts, which carry no candidate descriptions in any shell; the install output now also states the per-shell richness (`zsh`/`fish`: candidates show command and option descriptions; `bash`: values only — readline cannot render them). `vesma completion` behavior unchanged.

### Fixed

- **Client metadata stamp hardening: pipeline retry keys join the server-minted strip class (#432)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/models.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/import_.py`; tests `tests/test_b2b_semantics.py`, `tests/test_export_import.py`, `tests/test_sync.py`) — `pipeline_retry_count`/`pipeline_retry_at` (the refine lane's retry bookkeeping) are SERVER-MINTED: a client `add`/`update` can no longer MINT them on rows that lack them (the #251 forged-stamp class, CWE-346 — the merge-back only protected existing values) nor overwrite/reset them on rows that carry them; the JSON import strip covers the retry keys too (`--trusted-restore` keeps them, like checkpoint stamps — a trusted self-backup carries its own server retry state); the federation compact contract carries NO metadata field, pinned by test so a future mapper change that starts copying peer metadata reopens the question loudly instead of silently. (Entry restored on the release train — it was dropped from `[Unreleased]` by the `797281d` conflict resolution despite the note below.)
- **`-h` works on every command and group (was: exit 2 `No such option: -h` on all 30 top-level commands)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/*` — `context_settings={"help_option_names": ["-h", "--help"]}` on the root typer app and every sub-app instantiation, 26 sites; the `__complete` engine pins `help_option_names=[]` so `-h<TAB>`/`--help<TAB>` reach it as data instead of being intercepted) — `--help` worked everywhere while `-h` was rejected everywhere; both now work at every level, including through the legacy free-text parsers. Regression: a parametrized tree-sweep test asserts exit 0 + usage output for `-h` AND `--help` on the full command tree (95 nodes).
- **help depth sweep — every command and group carries a non-empty one-line short help plus a full body (what/how/why, flags described)** (58 thin nodes rewritten across `src/vesmaro/cli/` — service verbs, auth token/totp verbs, agent-token verbs, export/import, sync, scanner, logs, processor, workflow, migrate, ingest, recall, tags, doctor, memory, completion, update timer; group bodies moved into the Typer `help=` strings — callback docstrings were invisible to click) — audit tool: walk the click tree, assert `help` non-empty and body >= 2 sentences for every non-hidden node (tests/test_cli_help_depth.py).

### Documentation

- ADR-0038: PG-1 two-level graph walk ratified with 9 binding conditions — one BFS walker (`trace_path` byte-pinned), a separate `walk` section in `search_graph` behind `code_graph.search_walk` (default-off), quota `k(limit)` with work caps independent of it, epoch in the payload, `graph_audit` with token economics (`out_tokens`/`avoided_bytes`), baseline-before-implementation; slices M1–M3.
- New API surface-parity record (EN/RU): the CLI/REST/MCP matrix re-enumerated from code, the four ratified `wontfix` dispositions, and the versioning canon (`/api/v1` roots, legacy aliases, `Sunset` with 6.0); versioning notes synced into the HTTP API reference (EN/RU).
- Root `vesma --help` gains a `Tip: add -h to any command for help.` line (epilog under the Commands table); `docs/en|ru/user/metrics.md` states the vesma-eyes consumption contract explicitly (vesma serves the metrics endpoints via FastAPI/uvicorn; vesma-eyes is a separate frontend — no Node runtime ships with vesma).
- CHANGELOG: resolved the committed merge-conflict markers at `797281d` — the cortex key-token guard (PR #502) and #501/#499 entries moved back under `[5.6.1]` where they shipped; the #432 retry-keys entry, believed to stay in `[Unreleased]`, had in fact been dropped by that conflict resolution and is restored above (ships in `[5.6.2]`).

## [5.6.1] — 2026-10-06

### Added

- **cortex key-token guard v0 on the VesmaProvider duplicate verdict (PR #502; labeling-policy v1.1 §8)** (`src/vesmaro/decision_provider.py`; tests `tests/test_cortex_key_token_guard.py`) — hybrid «model+code» split: the model answers semantics, while distinguishing WHICH token changed inside the high-cosine razor band (cosine >= 0.95, `KEY_TOKEN_GUARD_COS`) is code. The guard fires ONLY on a duplicate verdict (p >= 0.5) whose pair sits in the band; the v0 KEY-class detector looks for a whitespace token carrying an ASCII digit in the symmetric diff of the cortex-normalized title+body texts (the SAME normalization the features see — zero second normalization) and flips the verdict to not-duplicate (`p -> 1-p`); a cosmetic or all-non-digit diff leaves the model verdict standing. Documented v0 gaps (numbers as words, alphabetic-only names/versions) stay with the policy, not smuggled as heuristics. Telemetry: the DECISION line gains `override=key-token diff=<tokens>` (truncated to 120 chars) as the audit trail; fail-open by construction — any guard exception returns the model verdict with machine-parseable `code=CORTEX-E-GUARD`, the guard never blocks the prod path; ADVISORY only — the provider answers verdicts, the merge-arbiter owns actionability (§8.3 verdict/system-action separation). Merge commit `408836f`.

### Fixed

- **`vesma doctor service`: DR-06 no longer false-FAILs healthy installations (#501)** — the check compared each component venv's `bin/python` resolution against the doctor's own interpreter (`sys.executable`); every venv of the same base CPython resolves to the same base binary, so any component venv "was" the running interpreter and DR-06 FAILED on a healthy install (live-tested on 5.6.0), while the prescribed `vesma service install` changed nothing. Identity is now judged against the LAYOUT engine venv (`~/.local/share/vesma/venv`, layout §3.2) by venv ROOTS and resolved site-packages; a symlinked `venvs/<name>` still FAILs (the actual supply-chain guard); when the engine venv is absent (dev/self-hosted) the identity leg is n/a and reserved-names enforcement stays. Fix commands are truthful per case; check retitled `venv != engine venv; reserved names`. Regression tests in `tests/test_service_doctor.py::TestDR06` (same-base-CPython repro, distinct engine venv, shared engine site-packages, DR-04 enforcer intact).
- **tests: `_find_vitals_master` no longer crashes suite collection in shallow checkouts (#499)** (`tests/test_usage_phase_c.py`) — in ring clones the checkout is shallow (`/work/repo`) and `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[4]` raised `IndexError` at candidate-list build time, interrupting the WHOLE suite at collection (ring convoy 2026-10-05: 0 tests ran, rc=2); the index access is now depth-guarded — the vitals-master pin stays reachable on full layouts and degrades to a graceful skip in short-depth containers (the #436 worktree-independence precedent, now container-independence too). Commit `39d4843`.

## [5.6.0] — 2026-10-06

### Added

- **`vesmaro.service` ratification closers — SL-06 true-container PID1 leg + LY-12 cache taint write-API (W7, final blockers before the service-track contracts go draft→stable)** (`src/vesmaro/service/pid1_probe.py` — the REAL Supervisor as the PID 1 of a container: minimal child manifest + sleeper written into a private XDG workdir, wait healthy, double-fork an orphaned grandchild that reparents into the `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` and must be REAPED (not zombie — absent from `/proc`) BEFORE the stop, then wait for the EXTERNAL SIGTERM — the mandatory PID1 handler turns it into the graceful stop — and print a JSON summary with the §3.4 journal (spawn/health/stop ordering evidence), the `/proc` zombie scan and the exit code; `scripts/pid1_conformance.sh` — the podman driver: in-box podman probed once (historically refused with `newuidmap EPERM` in distroboxes), fallback to the HOST podman via `distrobox-host-exec`, read-only repo bind-mount over `ghcr.io/vesmaro/vesma:latest` (carries python3.12 + jsonschema + pyyaml — no network needed at run time), READY-marker wait, SIGTERM to the container PID 1, `podman wait` exit code + parsed summary verdict, container always cleaned up; `tests/test_service_pid1_container.py` — the leg SKIPs with the EXACT probe failure where no podman works (CI sandboxes stay green) and produces the real evidence where one does: run 2026-10-05 on the owner host = `pid1: true`, SIGTERM → graceful `healthy→stopped` per journal, child gone, `zombies: []`, orphan reaped before the stop, container exit code 0; the `unshare` leg re-probed with a 30 s timeout, still an honest named skip in this sandbox); (`src/vesmaro/service/envfile.py` — `load_env_file` now returns `TaintedValues` of `TaintedValue` (transparent `dict`/`str` subclasses: env construction, comparisons and JSON dumps behave exactly as before, non-breaking for every existing consumer) — values originating from env files carry the secret taint AT LOAD TIME per specs/layout/v1 §3.9; `src/vesmaro/service/cache.py` — the sanctioned cache write API `put_under_cache(name, key, value, *, tainted=False)`: refuses tainted values with `CacheWriteRefusedError` (declared via the flag OR carried by the `TaintedValue` type; the message names component/key and NEVER echoes the value), validates the component name against the CM §3.1 grammar and the key as a safe single path segment, writes atomically (tmp + rename) into `~/.cache/vesma/<name>/` with explicit umask-independent `0750`, regenerable by construction — deleting the directory never breaks operation; honest scope: no engine feature writes env-file values into caches today — the guard exists BEFORE the first writer, and the taint is a per-value type marker that does not survive string transformation, documented; tests `tests/test_ly12_cache_taint.py` (taint-at-load + non-breaking semantics, refusal legs — declared/typed/every-mapping-value/bytes, untainted happy path with bad-umask 0750 verification, boundary validation of names/keys, rm -rf regeneration), `tests/test_service_pid1_container.py`, conformance report `docs/project/reports/service-conformance-2026-10-06.md` W7 update (SL-06 gap → pass with the container transcript; LY-12 partial → pass; full suite 5940 passed / 8 skipped / 0 failed, +23 vs the W6 baseline))

- **`vesmaro.service` W6 integration — the real Supervisor adapter, the in-process core, and conformance evidence closure (board card `svc-w6-integration-conformance`)** (`src/vesmaro/service/backend.py` — `SupervisorBackend`, the REAL `ControlBackend` over the W2 Supervisor: status/health from the FSM snapshot with the CS §4.5 health mapping, start/stop/restart with `already-running`/`already-stopped` idempotence, 102 from the §3.4 refusal token, 103 on a stop that never reached `stopped`, bounded settle windows below the 10 s response timeout, `ComponentLogBuffer` — per-component in-memory rings in front of the real logsink (structural lines routed from the §3.4 `component=` field, child forwards from the `vesma-<name>` identifier) serving the `logs`/`logs follow` verbs; `ServiceApp` — the full `vesma service run` composition: control socket serving first, then the embedded memory-server core (the same `vesmaro.api.main:app` import string as `serve`, uvicorn workers pinned to 1 with a loud warning), then the supervisor, then watchers — core thread exit without an ordered teardown IS core death: fail-fast exit 1, no core-restart mechanism by design (SL §3.1); teardown order socket → reverse-topological children + reap → core drain); `src/vesmaro/cli/service.py` — `run` rewritten over `ServiceApp` (`--config` passes the general config through; bind/port from settings; the W3 stub backend deleted); `src/vesmaro/service/supervisor.py` — integration seams: `request_start` revives a manually-stopped component (clears the stale T6/T13 `stop_requested` — manual start after manual stop was dead on arrival), `request_stop(force=)` (graceful phase skipped, SIGKILL after 0.5 s, same reaper-pause pgid boundary), `refusal_reason()`/`shutdown_requested()` read-only seams, `_restore_signals` degrades loudly off the main thread instead of aborting the teardown; `src/vesmaro/service/control.py` — PR #494 backlog hardening: connection cap 64 (newcomer closed unread), hello timeout 10 s (silent connections closed), follow-pump send deadline 10 s (a never-reading peer frees its subscription), 102 message channel is a short fixed text; tests `tests/test_service_e2e.py` (real Supervisor + real ControlServer + real adapter + real children over a LIVE socket: hello/status with real pids, idempotence markers incl. start-after-stop, logs tail + follow until source stop with a stop from a second connection, SIGTERM → reverse-topological stop order proven from the history journal + zero children/zombies at exit, kill -9 a child → socket accepts + §3.4 `signal=KILL` exit line + T8 backoff + respawn (SL-01), embedded `/health` answers while the supervisor lives → un-ordered core exit → fail-fast exit 1, deterministic core-startup failure refuses the start), W6 hardening tests in `tests/test_service_control.py` (CS-12 measured response burst, conn cap, hello timeout, follow send deadline, 102 message hygiene), SL-05 flake hardening in `tests/test_service_supervisor.py` (poll the orphan payload until it PARSES COMPLETE — a bare `exists()` could read a half-written file under full-suite load); conformance evidence `docs/project/reports/service-conformance-2026-10-06.md` (SL-01..18, CS-1..16, LY-01..12+14, CM-01..17 item→verdict→test, specs runner 24/24).
- **`vesmaro.service` control socket + `vesma service` CLI — supervisor control plane (board card `svc-w3-control-socket`, wave W3; contract `specs/control-socket/v1` @ `1.0.0-draft.2`)** (`src/vesmaro/service/control.py` — AF_UNIX/SOCK_STREAM JSONL server: §4.2 bind TOCTOU/SYMLINK procedure in normative order (connect-probe of EADDRINUSE with a settle re-probe so a concurrent winner inside its bind→serve window is detected live instead of losing its fresh socket, `lstat` before unlink — only own-uid socket nodes, symlink → refuse + alert, never followed, non-socket/foreign owner → refuse deleting nothing, ≤3 attempts, post-bind rights verification fatal on mismatch — fd via `fstat`, on-disk node via `stat`, fd↔path tie via `getsockname`, because Linux `fstat` on AF_UNIX fds reads the sockfs pseudo-inode whose mode is always 0777), `SO_PEERCRED` uid check on every accept before the first byte (fail-closed on getsockopt failure; a platform without SO_PEERCRED refuses to bind), hello-major-version gate (any method before `hello` → error 4 with `data.reason="hello_required"` + `data.supported`), methods `status/start/stop/restart/logs/health` with start/stop idempotence markers, `logs follow` streaming frames `{"id","stream"}` to a final answer (source stop → `{"state"}`; idle 60 s and the 3600 s hard cap close with a final answer; client disconnect frees the subscription; limits 2/peer + 8/installation → error 3), §4.6 error registry 1/2/3/4/100/101/102/103/200/500 with the 100-range data hygiene (names/states/exit codes only — no paths/env/argv), §4.7 limits (1 MiB line → error 1 + close, tail ≤ 10000 default 100, component-name regex `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,62}$` → error 3 vs registry membership → error 100), TCP forbidden by construction (no code path can open a network listener); `src/vesmaro/service/client.py` — thin client: §4.1 pre-flight refuses group-/world-writable or foreign-owned socket dirs and carries the ready chmod/chown command (printed, never auto-executed), hello-first session, 10 s response timeout outside follow, range-based interpretation of unknown error codes; `src/vesmaro/cli/service.py` — `vesma service status|health|start|stop|restart|logs [--follow] [--tail N]|run` (§4.8 1:1 mapping; `run` = supervisor entrypoint owning socket + component tree in one process, single-instance exit 0 «already running»; the backend wired to `vesmaro.service.Supervisor` is the W3 seam — status/health via `snapshot()` live, per-component verbs explicitly raise until the W6 adapter), registered in `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py` (W4 merges extend `cli/service.py` in place); server programmed against the `ControlBackend`/`LogStream` `typing.Protocol` seam, NOT the concrete Supervisor — the W6 integration wave lands the real adapter; tests `tests/test_service_control.py` (conformance CS-1..CS-16 + the SL-01 socket-accepts-after-child-death leg; CS-3 cross-uid and CS-5 foreign-uid live legs honest-skipped outside root — decision logic unit-tested), `tests/test_service_client.py` (pre-flight, session, timeouts, unknown-code ranges, follow, CLI wiring), `tests/control_fakes.py` (protocol-conformant fake backend with backend-side duties)).

- **`vesmaro.service` supervisor — component lifecycle engine (board card `svc-w2-supervisor`, wave W2; contract `specs/service-lifecycle/v1` @ `1.0.0-draft.2`)** (`src/vesmaro/service/fsm.py` — child FSM with the normative T1–T14 transition table as data (any (state, event) pair outside the table raises `TransitionError` — forbidden transitions impossible by construction, SL-07), `health.py` — CM §3.7 checkers (http/tcp/exec/liveness/callback) with the callback isolation boundary (ANY exception/timeout/malformed result → failed probe with `CALLBACK_FAILED`, nothing propagates) and the exec probe under **reaper wait-ownership** (the supervisor's reaper is the single `waitpid` owner per SL §3.1 — a probe that waited its own child would race the `waitpid(-1)` drain and CPython's ECHILD fallback fabricates `rc=0`, reporting a failing probe healthy; the checker polls the reaper's records instead, standalone callers keep the legacy self-wait), `supervisor.py` — process model (setsid children with `pgid == pid` verified at spawn, group-signal boundary: group signals only while the direct child is unreaped, reaper pause during stop grace, subreaper via `prctl(PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER)`, `waitpid(-1, WNOHANG)` drain to ECHILD, PID1 mode with mandatory handlers), isolation (`env -i` semantics: constructed PATH + `env.vars` + env_file + forced `PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1`, no fd/socket inheritance), restart policies (core: exponential 1s×2 cap 30s jitter ±20% counter reset after 300s uptime + crash-loop alert at 10 attempts; optional: 5-per-300s window → ONE exact `event=degraded` ERROR line → silent lazy-retry every 300s, manual start resets), topological start / reverse-topological stop with grace-then-SIGKILL per child and a stop budget under the unit's `TimeoutStopSec`, structural observability: spawn/exit/health/degraded §3.4 lines + append-only history journal (every FSM transition + global-health change, ISO-8601 prefixed, 0600/0700), `logsink.py` — journald/files EXACTLY ONE mode, `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER=vesma-<component>` marking, rotation 10MB×5; `src/vesmaro/service/board/__init__.py` — read-only status panel) — tests `tests/test_service_fsm.py` (exhaustive T1–T14 matrix + illegal-transition fuzz), `tests/test_service_health.py` (callback isolation, real-socket tcp/http, exec wait-ownership regression), `tests/test_service_logsink.py` (§3.4 grammar builders pinned to the contract's example lines, journal append-only + modes, rotation, journald datagram sink), `tests/test_service_supervisor.py` + `tests/service_children/` (real-process conformance: SL-01 kill -9 subset isolation, SL-02 exception boundary, SL-03 pgid==pid, SL-04 stubborn group SIGKILL, SL-05 subreaper orphan reap, SL-06 PID1 handler legs, SL-08 full-grammar regex over all emitted lines, SL-09 budget exhaustion single-ERROR, SL-10 lazy-retry silence + manual reset, SL-11 crash-loop alert + restarts continue, SL-12 backoff ladder + uptime reset, SL-13 exact env set, SL-14 fd/socket isolation, SL-15 cycle refusal + blocked gate + T3 unblock, SL-16 reverse-topo stop + zero leftovers). Honest gaps (deferred to W3/control-socket): SL-01 socket-accepts leg, SL-06 true-container leg (unshare refused in sandbox — handler/reap legs run for real), SL-08 journald live leg (sink unit-covered against a bound datagram socket).

- **`graph_audit` first-search marker (card `vesma-graph-audit-firstcall-marking`)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/{service,audit}.py`; test `tests/test_codegraph_tools.py` +1) — each task's FIRST `search_graph` call (actor+session scoped) now carries `details.first_search: true` on its `graph_audit` row (marker absent on later calls — shape policy), making the graph-first share computable from `graph_audit` rows alone; the computing query is documented in `GraphAudit.has_search`.
- **Memory-native awareness canon — the pack treats memory + surrounding sessions as the FIRST information source (epic `memory-native-awareness`, slice `mna-a-gate-canon`; owner directive 2026-10-05 «важно и срочно»)** (`integrations/agents_md/vesma-always-on.md`, `integrations/instructions/vesma-memory-ops.instructions.md`, `integrations/skills/vesma-session-init.md`, `integrations/skills/vesma-recall.md`; tests `tests/test_integration.py` +2 pins) — four canon additions, all ADDITIVE to the existing gates (safety/tag/cache contracts untouched): (a) **G4 mechanical trigger** — before asking the owner ANY findable fact and before writing "I don't know / no data / nobody did X", `vesma_search` is MANDATORY: own silo first, then a BOUNDED cross-silo sweep (≤2 queries, limit ≤5 — ops/infra facts live in OTHER project silos), with the query recipe (topic-project slug + topical keywords: path, service, timestamp, «deploy», «incident», «wall») and the stated-outcome rule (`Searched memory: 0 results for <query>`); (b) **G1 session-start environment pre-flight** — after the recall, `vesma_awareness(action="pre_flight", …)` (read-only presence + delta + conflict-hints for parallel sessions) + ONE cross-silo infra sweep + the `task:queue` board read, BOUNDED to ≤3 extra calls, each surfaced in ≤1 line, degraded-to-a-line never blocking (the ADR-0035 contour: awareness supplies DATA, decisions stay with the agent); (c) **mandatory report block «Обстановка вокруг»** in every TL/owner-facing report — neighboring sessions observed, board state, coordination files touched, adjustments made BECAUSE of the environment; an empty picture is reported as such in one line, never omitted; (d) **forensics gate** — for any UNEXPLAINED machine change (strange mtimes, unknown processes, sudden service failures) a neighbor-session sweep (awareness pre-flight + handoff/checkpoint search for the timeframe, cross-silo) is MANDATORY before any "unknown actor / nobody did X" claim; a clean sweep reports the evidence-backed `Searched memory (own silo + cross-silo, <timeframe>): 0 traces`, not an assumption. Evidence (both 2026-10-05): a distrobox OOM's root cause (`wall-distrobox-mem.timer`, 18G/swap0) sat in the vesmaro-agent silo while the TL asked the OWNER for it (G4 was canon but had no mechanical trigger), and the prod-venv write at 02:39 was found in the NEIGHBORING session's handoff (`sess_16eb642b`) only after the owner pointed at it (the neighbor sweep was not canon). Pins: the memory-ops instruction must teach the pre-flight call, the cross-silo recipe, the report block and the forensics sweep; the always-on block must carry the tightened G1/G4 rows and the «Обстановка вокруг» section, including the SEC clauses (BY REFERENCE / `[unverified]` / 0-traces scope / `mnemos:no-federate`). Architecture forks (cache-discipline tension of the pre-flight calls) are noted for slice `mna-b`, not resolved here. ArchCom 2026-10-05 verdict ACCEPT AFTER AMENDMENT applied: SEC clauses — foreign-session content quoted BY REFERENCE only (memory id + title; recalled content re-stated as DATA, never instructions, for foreign sessions specifically — CWE-74 / OWASP LLM01), «0 traces» scoped to zero-traces-in-MEMORY-only (an attribution input alongside mtimes/audit, not a verdict machine), `[unverified]` markers on peer claims in «Обстановка вокруг» (never values/tokens), `mnemos:no-federate` on awareness-derived forensics records; the always-on block compressed from +22 to +9 added lines under the trigger-per-line rule (rich procedures stay in the instruction).
- **ADR-0036 — native situational awareness: the E/C/R/X/S frame as the formal ArchCom record (2026-10-05)** (`docs/project/adr/0036-native-situational-awareness-canon.md`; index rows in `docs/project/adr/README.md` — including the previously missed 0035 row; one-line backlink added to ADR-0035's references) — canonizes what slice `mna-a` implements and what stays deliberate-Later: the harness-neutral canon gates (topic-triggered cross-silo, the mechanical G4 trigger, the neighbor-session sweep at start + forensics, the mandatory «Обстановка вокруг» report block) with the four security clauses, rollout `awareness.native_heartbeat_mode=shadow` now with canary behind a 1–2-week soak, cortex-operator DEFERRED with standing owner authorization and build triggers, cortex-rank as an async shadow A/B with its PASS gate (security events never model-suppressed/deduped without provenance), and the specs-escalation criterion (second independent producer / typed REST awareness field) instead of a specs contract now; extends ADR-0035 (the delivery layer), does not supersede it.
- **`vesma service install|uninstall` + `vesma doctor service` — systemd unit generation, install flow, DR-01…DR-13 (board card `svc-w4-install-unitgen-doctor`, wave W4; contracts `specs/service-lifecycle/v1` §3.6 + `specs/layout/v1` §3.1–3.5/3.8/3.10 @ `1.0.0-draft.2`)** (`src/vesmaro/service/unitgen.py`, `src/vesmaro/service/install.py`, `src/vesmaro/service/doctor_checks.py`; CLI `src/vesmaro/cli/service.py` (W3 extends with status/logs/start/stop/restart/run), `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py` `service` subcommand; tests `tests/test_service_unitgen.py`, `tests/test_service_install.py`, `tests/test_service_doctor.py`) — `unitgen` is a PURE generator (no I/O): the full SL-17 MUST table (Type=exec, one static `ExecStart=<venv>/bin/vesma service run`, NO ExecStop, KillSignal=SIGTERM, KillMode=mixed, TimeoutStopSec=90, Restart=on-failure, RestartSec=5s, StartLimit 300/5, journal) + the full SL-18 hardening block with `SystemCallFilter=@system-service` emitted COMMENTED with the loud Tier-B note and `MemoryDenyWriteExecute` deliberately ABSENT with the anti-silent-re-add comment; SL-17 contract constants (`TIMEOUT_STOP_SEC` etc.) are exported as the single source of truth for the supervisor wave; container detect (DISTROBOX_ENTER_ENV/CONTAINER_ID/.dockerenv/.containerenv) downgrades ONLY the filesystem allowlist `{ProtectSystem, ProtectHome, ReadOnlyPaths, ReadWritePaths, PrivateTmp}` loudly (`# vesma:downgraded=` machine-readable marker + install report) — any other directive = `UnitGenerationError`. `install` is the single installation writer: bundled pack manifests → `components.d/<name>.yaml` atomic tmp+rename (siblings untouched; stray files fail closed at `load_installation`), per-component data dirs + `config.schema.json` from `schema_inline`, one venv per python child (`venvs/metrics` 0700, EXACT `==` pins, PyPI-only — URL/range/wildcard rejected, `pip freeze` == lock verified right after install, `vesma==<engine version>` reconciled with metrics.yaml argv; in-process board has NO venv by design), idempotent reinstall; unit → `~/.config/systemd/user/vesma.service` atomic 0644 + best-effort non-fatal `daemon-reload`; `uninstall` (component or `--all`): best-effort `systemctl --user disable --now`, removes unit+manifests+env files+venvs, PRESERVES data dirs, refuses unknown components and uninstalling a component another depends on. `vesma doctor service` implements DR-01…DR-13 READ-ONLY (LY-14: every finding carries severity + a ready fix command with real paths; exit 1 on FAIL, 2 on warnings-only, matching the `vesma doctor` convention): DR-07 regenerates the unit and diffs byte-for-byte (drift → FAIL + `vesma service install`), DR-08 probes the contract socket path with the exact control-socket §4.4 `hello` frame (absent/stale socket = status, never an error), DR-13 parses the downgrade marker against the allowlist plus commented-without-marker tamper detection, DR-03 runs the REAL clean-env `PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1` subprocess. Draft-audit fixes kept: `pip freeze`/lock parsing partitioned on `==` (the `=`-partition bug made `vesma==X` parse as version `=X` and fail every real install), DR-01's `os.getuser` crash (→ `getpass.getuser`), site-packages checked against the contract's group/world-WRITABLE bar (readability is not a violation), the sanctioned container downgrade in DR-12 reported as WARN (a legitimate container install must not be permanently red; undocumented loss of coverage stays FAIL).
- **G0a/G0b usage-loop gates in the canon pack (wave 10, PR #493)** (`integrations/instructions/vesma-memory-ops.instructions.md`, `integrations/skills/vesma-core.md`, `integrations/skills/vesma-context-lifecycle.md`, `integrations/agents_md/vesma-always-on.md`; drift-guard pins in `tests/test_integration.py`) — usage-loop closure becomes canon for EVERY agent: **G0a** (assemble memory context before the first substantive answer; runs after the G1 recall, before the first model call; remember `usage_report.metrics_id`) and **G0b** (report usage after the model answered via `mnemos_usage_report` with the `<metrics_id>:<i>` composite ordinals derived from the assemble result's `blocks` list; an empty `block_ids_touched` is legitimate) join the gate table before G1; the post-model report leg is a new step 3 in the context lifecycle, subsequent steps renumbered; the always-on block mirrors the compressed pair. The `vesma integration setup` deployment reads the canon from the installed package, so this release is what carries the gates to live surfaces (`~/.copilot`, `~/.hermes`).

### Changed

- **Decision-provider conformance: contract `decision-provider/v1` `1.0.0-draft.2` (review-2) alignment — action classes on every degradation, fail-closed scanner, `-CONFIG` gate, per-answer telemetry (board card `svc-w5-dp-conformance`, wave W5; report `docs/project/reports/dp-conformance-2026-10-05.md`)** (`src/vesmaro/decision_provider.py`, `src/vesmaro/decision_jev.py`; tests `tests/test_decision_{provider,jev,cortex}.py` +15 contract tests) — every degradation warn now carries a machine-parseable `class=` field from the contractual set (`provider-class` / `verdict-class` / `call-class`) alongside the implementation-namespace code (`JEV-E-*`); the privacy gate is scanner fail-closed — an exception or 10s wall-clock timeout inside the secret detectors aborts the whole external call with `class=call-class`; the `no-federate` exclusion happens at state assembly (first line) before the scan (second line); the `jev` activation with a flag but no API key now warns `JEV-E-CONFIG class=provider-class` and stays on `deterministic` (was a raised exception); the wiring hands out `JevFailOpenProvider` so a single probe failure degrades THAT verdict (§3.8) instead of blocking the product path; every answered request logs a §3.9 telemetry line (primitive / implementation / confidence). DP-01…15 scorecard: 12 pass / 3 partial / 0 fail (was 7/7/1); partials (DP-01 wiring, DP-06 canon calibration binding, DP-12 live pin event) need artifacts outside this repo — see the report.

- **Release train: the container image is now a MANDATORY, synchronous artifact — the skip path is structurally gone (owner directive 2026-10-05, card `vesma-ghcr-5x-parity`; incident: ghcr.io carried no 5.3.0/5.4.0/5.5.0 images while PyPI carried the versions)** (`scripts/image-publish.sh` — new single image-phase implementation, subcommands `preflight` / `build` / `push` / `catch-up`, NO skip flag anywhere: `scripts/pypi-publish.sh --publish` orders it around the upload — preflight (builder present, `GHCR_TOKEN` present, version gates VG1–VG3: tag `vX.Y.Z` == `pyproject.toml` == `## [X.Y.Z]` section in `CHANGELOG.md`) and build+smoke (`vesma --version` inside the built container must print exactly the release version) run BEFORE any upload, so an unusable image environment blocks the whole train before anything is published anywhere; push — skopeo with explicit transports `containers-storage:` → `docker://` (field-proven on the 5.5.0 push: `podman push` to ghcr.io 403'd on the first blob-reuse check with the SAME authfile that worked through skopeo), direct `podman/docker push` fallback, fresh temp authfile pre-seeded `{}` and shredded on exit — plus an anonymous pull-flow verification (`tags/list` contains the version; digest `:VERSION` == digest `:latest` == pushed digest) run AFTER the uploads; a push/verify failure after PyPI succeeded ends the run with a loud `RELEASE INCOMPLETE` banner naming the exact catch-up command (`GHCR_TOKEN=<token> scripts/image-publish.sh catch-up`) and exits non-zero — a published PyPI version without its image is never reported green; `scripts/local-release.sh` (deprecated GitHub-Release fallback) lost `--no-image` (now a loud error) and delegates both image steps to the same module — one image-phase implementation total; `make local-release-no-image` abolished into a loud pointer; `Makefile`, runbooks `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/pypi-publish.md` (new section "Container image — mandatory half of the train": ordering, gates, `GHCR_TOKEN`, distrobox `VESMA_IMAGE_*` overrides) and `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/ci-cd.md` (stale pre-rebrand `ghcr.io/korrnals/vesma` registry reference corrected to `vesmaro`)).

### Fixed

- perf(codegraph): `graph_audit` gained `idx_graph_audit_search (action, reason, actor, session)` — the `has_search` lookup runs on EVERY `search_graph` call and was a full-table scan over the unbounded append-only audit trail; the index rides the idempotent schema init (existing stores pick it up on the next open — no schema-version bump) and the planner serves the lookup as a COVERING index, `session IS ?` included (card `vesma-graph-audit-search-index`, code-review SHOULD 2026-10-05).
- fix(release): `.containerignore` excluded `scripts/local-release.sh` while the wheel `force-include` enumeration requires it — every image build of a current tag died in hatchling with `FileNotFoundError: Forced include not found` (the file is a real tracked file since the #481 symlink cleanup; the context-exclusion list and the force-include list must stay disjoint). Proven on the v5.5.0 image build published 2026-10-05 as `ghcr.io/vesmaro/vesma:5.5.0` + `:latest` (digest `sha256:ba9de8aa29a9469bb934d7858c45181761435cc31ac48582c61a64573f8d99c0`, anonymous pull verified) (card `vesma-ghcr-5x-parity`).
- fix(codegraph): `search_graph` `total_matches` now counts the WHOLE answer set — when the hybrid literal fallback (W-H) answers, it reports the number of literal rows returned instead of a false `total_matches: 0` on a non-empty result (card `vesma-graph-roughness-repeat1`, vitals repeat #1).
- fix(codegraph): `trace_path` on an identical-qname collision (the same top-level symbol name in several files) now answers the ambiguity contract — a ranked candidate list with a path/line disambiguation hint — instead of silently tracing the first node (card `vesma-graph-roughness-repeat1`, vitals repeat #1).
- fix(api): tag-contract violations on `/ingest-url` and `/ingest-document` now return `422` with the contract error message instead of a raw `500` (#422/#432 class — same mapping discipline as `POST /memories`).
- fix(service): control-socket cascade fixes (PR #494, wave W3, cascade 2026-10-05) — a failed `logs follow` stream OPEN (unknown component / backend crash) no longer leaks the follow slot (one try/finally from acquire onward; previously two ghost follows locked the peer out with error 3 while `active_follows()` counted phantom subscriptions), the accept loop prunes finished connection threads (`_conns` grew without bound under steady polling), `StartFailedError.reason` is collapsed to `[a-z0-9_.-]{0,64}` else `"start-failed"` at the wire seam (§4.6 data hygiene), the client raises `ProtocolViolationError` (not a raw `ValueError`) on a non-integer error code and derives its follow read budget from `FOLLOW_IDLE_TIMEOUT_S` (1.25x margin); `_client_gone` docstring corrected (pending rogue bytes are not EOF); deterministic settle test for the §4.2 re-probe window (W6 backlog: connection cap + hello timeout on non-follow connections, follow pump send deadline, full run happy-path e2e, message-channel hygiene for backend exception text).

## [5.5.0] — 2026-10-05

### Added

- **`vesmaro.service` foundations package — component-manifest v1 loader, canonical layout, fail-closed env files (board card `svc-w1-foundations`, wave W1; contracts `specs/component-manifest/v1` + `specs/layout/v1` @ `1.0.0-draft.2`)** (`src/vesmaro/service/` — `errors.py` (CM §4 code registry), `manifest.py` (strict JSON-Schema 2020-12 load + normative §3 rules: shell-in-argv, placeholder allowlist, secret-in-vars, durations, restart clamps, fail-closed whole-installation load with dup/missing/cycle checks), vendored `schemas/component-manifest.schema.json` (specs @ d30e668, byte-identical copy; provenance in `manifest.SCHEMA_VENDORED_PROVENANCE`), `layout.py` (XDG §3.2 paths, explicit-chmod `ensure_dir`/`verify_dir`, §3.6 runtime fallback + WARN, §3.4 `resolve_component_paths`), `envfile.py` (fail-closed `ENV_FILE_UNSAFE` loader with ready fix commands), `placeholders.py` (4-token allowlist expansion, venv-less `{venv_bin}` refused), bundled pack manifests `components/board.yaml` (in-process, callback health) + `components/metrics.yaml` (child-process, HTTP health on the real port), minimal skeleton `board/__init__.py`; `src/vesmaro/metrics/exposer.py` — minimal `__main__` exposition server so the metrics argv points at a surface that actually runs; `pyproject.toml` — `jsonschema>=4.21` promoted to a declared core dep, imported directly by the manifest loader; tests `tests/test_service_manifest.py`, `tests/test_service_layout.py`, `tests/test_service_envfile.py`, `tests/test_service_placeholders.py`) — the reader side of the component contract, pure foundation: no CLI, no supervisor, no install flow (waves W2/W3 build on it). Both bundled manifests pass the full check set and the specs conformance runner; modes are umask-independent for the leaf AND every intermediate vesma-owned segment (0700/0750 verified under umask 077 and 000).
- **`vesma recall agent SLUG [QUERY]` — per-agent recall (M3) as a subcommand (board card `vesma-cli-architecture-rework`, wave W2; standing design rule: subcommand = function (WHAT), flag = configuration (HOW))** (`src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`, `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/install.md`, `PLAN.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`; tests `tests/test_cli.py` +10 new, 2 re-pinned) — `recall` is a group whose default command keeps the bare `vesma recall` behavior byte-identical, and per-agent recall moved to the canonical subcommand: without `QUERY` it returns the N most recent entries for the agent (created_at desc, the pre-W2 `--agent` semantics), with an optional `QUERY` it hybrid-searches scoped to the agent's entries (raw included) — the same data the MCP tool `mnemos_agent_recall` returns. `--project` and `--limit` are available on both surfaces.

- **REST twins for graph register/repoint and tags add/remove — the #454 K-class REST tail closed** (`src/vesmaro/api/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/http-api.md` EN/RU; tests `tests/test_api.py` +15 new) — the manager/service methods existed but had no HTTP surface; all four new routes mount under the canonical `/api/v1` namespace, never at the root: `POST /api/v1/graph/register` (twin of `mnemos_register_project`; source=agent, one-root-one-graph reuse, name collision → 403, PG7 attribution → 400), `POST /api/v1/graph/repoint` (twin of the `vesma graph repoint` CLI; ghost-only, live root → 403), `POST /api/v1/tags/add` (twin of `mnemos_tags action=add`; `dry_run=true` default) and `POST /api/v1/tags/remove` (wildcard prefix match). Error mapping follows the in-file fix patterns — graph refusals via `_graph_call` (503 disabled / 403 confinement / 413 limit / 400 rest), tags contract `ValueError`s (incl. `TagContractError`) via `_tags_call` → 422 with the same message — so the #422/#432 500-leak defect class is not carried into new code; per-memory tag-contract refusals stay in the report's `errors` list at 200 (MCP parity).

- **Deprecation headers on legacy root REST aliases — soft API-versioning migration (card `vesma-rest-deprecation-middleware`, ArchCom verdict accept(b) ratified 2026-10-04)** (`src/vesmaro/api/middleware.py` — new `DeprecationMiddleware`, outermost before CORS; docs `docs/en|ru/user/http-api.md`; tests `tests/test_deprecation_middleware.py` +150 lines) — the canonical API location is `/api/v1` and the 42 root-level routes (`/memories`, `/search`, `/context/*`, `/graph/*`, `/metrics`, …) stay as backward-compatibility aliases until 6.0 physically moves them; every response served via a legacy route template is now decorated with `Deprecation: true` and `Link: </api/v1<template>>; rel="suggested-version"`. Route templates are matched from `request.scope["route"]` only — no fallback to the raw concrete path, so unmatched 404s and auth-rejected 401s (rejected before routing) stay clean; a 405 still carries the matched template and is decorated. RFC 8594 `Sunset` is deliberately NOT sent: it requires an absolute HTTP-date and the 6.0 date is not fixed — it lands in the 6.0 line once the date exists. `/health` (liveness convention) and `/v1/*` (A2A Sessions API — its own versioned surface) are excluded from the alias set by design; the AuthMiddleware/VitalsVerb bypass lists are untouched.

- **CLI commands and REST routes as first-class graph nodes — sidecar schema v2 (card `vesma-graph-command-route-nodes`)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/node_sources.py` — new generic node-source seam, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/graph_surface_ext.py` — host wiring, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/indexer.py`, `src/vesmaro/storage/code_graph_store.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/project-graph.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`; tests `tests/test_codegraph_surface_nodes.py` +562 lines, `tests/test_codegraph_tools.py` updated) — a marker-detected vesma repo now grows `Command`/`Route` nodes (full invocation path / method+path, one-line help, option count, ≤16 compact params) bound to their implementing functions by `INVOKES`/`HANDLES` edges, so agents discover the surface with one bounded `search_graph` call (~185 tokens) instead of an ~18.7k-token `--help` dump. Data comes from the LIVE engine registries (the completion engine's typer/click tree, FastAPI `app.routes`) — no second parser; per-source failure isolation; contributions ride the same atomic publish; handler binding is by exact address via a path-scoped symbol map, unresolved handlers get no edge (unresolved-import honesty). Indexer fix: `decorated_definition` (any `@decorator`-carrying def/class — which is ALL CLI commands and REST endpoints) was invisible to the parser traversal; now indexed, decorator text never read (PG1 holds). Sidecar schema v2 passes the CHECKs with an in-place lossless v1→v2 migration via `graph_meta.schema_version` (standard SQLite table-rebuild, rows preserved, no forced reindex), the migration logs target version / previous stamp / carried-over row count on real runs (the no-log fast path is unchanged), `status` reports the per-kind `node_kinds` breakdown and `get_graph_schema` reports `schema_version 2`.

- **Vitals phase C — usage-loop analytics, sink write path and exposition (#461, ADR-0026)** (`src/vesmaro/metrics/usage.py` — new `UsageAnalyzer`, `src/vesmaro/metrics/sink.py`; tests `tests/test_usage_phase_c.py` +522 lines, `tests/test_vitals_phase_c.py` +263 lines) — three global, non-fatal aggregates over the `usage_reports` plane (`assemble_usage_rate`, `touched_share`, `wrong_tool_rate`); every read failure degrades to a fully-keyed loud NO-DATA dict with reasons — never an exception into a server path, never a silent zero. The kappa gate is structural: `kappa_calibration_pending()` stays True under the H-K0 ablation verdict, so `touched_share` is informational-only with `corridor_eligible=False` and the frozen pre-registration reason attached, in OK and degraded paths alike. `MetricsStore.record_usage` — the ONE client-supplied write into the plane — validates like hostile input: `block_ids_touched` a list of single-line non-empty strings ≤128 chars (≤256 entries before order-preserving dedup), `tokens_out` an int ≥0 (bools refused, cap 10**12), `wrong_tool_flag` strictly bool, hard range bounds and overflow classes degrade hostile ints to loud refusals; FK enforcement is ATOMIC (conditional `INSERT … SELECT … WHERE EXISTS` under one write lock — a check-then-write race against the nightly retention job can never strand an immortal orphan); `record_assemble` gains the same overflow hardening; all failures log a loud warning and roll the partial write back (TraceRecorder-grade non-fatality).

- **`mnemos_usage_report` — harness-authored usage-loop closure (vitals wave 9, #487)** (`src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/metrics/exposer.py`; tests `tests/test_usage_mcp.py` +389 lines / 15 new, pin updates in `tests/test_mcp_sdk2_port.py`, `tests/test_mcp_server.py`, `tests/test_f3_docs_as_memory.py`) — the phase-C usage-report write path is exposed as an MCP tool (manifest 39 → 40) so harnesses close the loop: after a turn built on an `mnemos_assemble_context` window, the harness reports which injected blocks it touched, the output token count and a wrong-tool flag (ADR-0026 §C; the server never self-reports usage). The tool is a thin boundary over the sink — every hostile-input rule (single-line ids ≤128 chars, ≤256 entries, `tokens_out` int ≥0 or None, strictly-bool flag, range-bound `metrics_id`, atomic FK refusal of unknown parents) stays in `MetricsStore.record_usage`, re-implemented nowhere, loosened nowhere; sink refusals surface as `{"error": …}` plus a loud server-log warning. Companion change: `record_assemble_vitals` now returns the assemble row id and the `mnemos_assemble_context` sync result carries the additive `usage_report.metrics_id` runtime key (born-final sidecar schema untouched; async handle envelopes carry no key and stay unrecorded — the phase-A rule). Manager wrapper `record_usage_vitals` keeps the non-fatal guest contract. The read half needs no new tool — the `mnemos_usage_*` aggregates were already exposed via `/api/v1/metrics` (scope discipline).

- **Docstrings for the hottest REST endpoints (card `vesma-docstrings-core-endpoints`)** (`src/vesmaro/api/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/http-api.md` EN/RU synced where they had gaps) — `GET /health`, `POST /memories` and `GET /memories` get OpenAPI-visible docstrings in the in-file house style (summary line, semantics, HTTP code mapping), verified to feed FastAPI `openapi()` descriptions; the http-api docs gain the missing `GET /memories` params (`agent`, `tags`, `since`/`until`, `offset`, the 422 note on unknown status, the quarantine-exclusion page under-fill), a corrected `POST /memories` errors table and an explicit `/health` unauthenticated-bypass note (stays at the root in 6.0).

### Changed

- **`vesma recall --agent` is a hidden deprecated alias (W2 alias cycle, design doc `docs/project/cli-architecture-rework.md` §2.4, §3)** — the flag form still works with identical behavior, but is hidden from `--help` and prints a one-line stderr hint (`[deprecated] \`vesma recall --agent SLUG\` is deprecated — use: vesma recall agent SLUG`; the W-C precedent from `vesma update` 5.4.0, stdout stays clean for pipes/JSON). Removal is not before 6.0. Options placed before the `agent` subcommand word are not silently dropped — a stderr note asks to pass them after it (same as `vesma doctor` / `vesma update`).

- **`vesma fts`, `vesma processor`, `vesma edge-stats` are real typer sub-apps (board card `vesma-cli-architecture-rework`, wave W1; standing design rule: subcommand = function (WHAT), flag = configuration (HOW))** (`src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_cli.py` +12 new, 1 re-pinned) — the former positional-action commands became subcommand groups: `vesma fts rebuild`, `vesma processor status|run|start|stop`, `vesma edge-stats stats|purge` (the `edge-stats` `--keep-last/--apply` options stay on `purge`, unchanged). The rework is textually compatible — the old positional spellings ARE the new subcommand spellings, so every existing invocation (runbooks `edge-stats-maintenance` and `migrate`, the doctor's `vesma processor start` hints) keeps working byte-identically, and no hidden-alias cycle was needed (design doc `docs/project/cli-architecture-rework.md` §2.1–2.3, §3.5; scripted-dependency grep over CI/Makefile/scripts/systemd units: 0 hits). Two deliberate behavior deltas: an unknown verb is now a typer usage error (exit 2 with the subcommand list; was a custom `Unknown action` message + exit 1), and the bare `vesma edge-stats` — which silently ran `stats` — now shows the subcommand help instead (spell the verb; the bare `fts`/`processor` forms, previously a missing-argument error, also show help). Sub-app help texts follow the W-C precedent (`vesma update` 5.4.0). Manager cleanup is now exception-safe on all paths (try/finally `mgr.close()`).

- **ruff format applied to drifted files — no semantic changes (card `vesma-ruff-format-drift-hygiene`)** — the formatter's canonical style is now the tree-wide baseline for the files that had drifted; `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on the whole tree.

### Fixed

- **Fresh-clone builds repaired: machine-local release symlink untracked, wheel scripts pinned by enumeration (#481)** (`.gitignore`; `pyproject.toml` — `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]`) — PR #179 committed `scripts/run-release-local.sh` as an absolute symlink into an external pipeline checkout; the directory force-include walked it, so on any machine without the target every build crashed (`hatchling os.stat → FileNotFoundError` — caught live by the k3s test-ring pilot: `uv sync` INFRA FAILURE on run1-a), and on machines where the target exists, source-tree wheel builds (`uv build --wheel`, `uv sync` editable) silently shipped the external 66 KB pipeline script inside `vesmaro/scripts/` of the artefact. The symlink is now untracked and kept repo-local via `.gitignore` (the owner's local release flow keeps working), and the wheel force-include enumerates exactly the sdist-visible scripts (15 files), making wheel content checkout- and path-independent: release `python -m build` (from sdist), dev `uv build --wheel` (from source tree) and editable `uv sync` now all ship the same set — previously from-source-tree wheels also carried `scripts/local-ci/run-all.sh` that release wheels never had (latent build-path divergence, converged here). Verified: fresh `git clone` + `uv build` produces wheel+sdist with no `run-release-local.sh` and a scripts file list identical to the owner-checkout build; `uv sync --reinstall-package vesma` (the exact operation that crashed the ring) rebuilds the editable with the symlink present on disk.
- **`scripts/pypi-publish.sh` works post-rebrand again: G4 smoke checks the `vesmaro` package** (`scripts/pypi-publish.sh`; docs `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/pypi-publish.md`, names note in `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/install.md`) — the mechanical rebrand sweep flipped the G4 metadata probe from `importlib.resources.files("vesmaro")` back to the nonexistent `vesma` import package (the PyPI distribution name `vesma` and the import package `vesmaro` are different names), so every pre-upload G4 gate would fail on a post-5.0.0 build; the `--full-smoke` step now exercises the shipped `vesma` CLI entry point instead of the deprecated `mnemos` alias, stale `mnemos-memory-server` header/normalization comments and `/tmp/mnemos-pypi-*` smoke-dir prefixes are cleaned. Verified end-to-end on the built 5.4.0 artifact set: `--dry-run --full-smoke` exits 0 without upload, and both smoke paths pass for real against the wheel (G4 `vesmaro` resources + full-deps `vesma --version`). The runbooks' name section was rewritten from the outdated «decided: `mnemos-memory-server`» to the verified channel reality (primary `vesma` 5.4.0, live mirror `vesma-memory-server` — the one `install.sh` probes, legacy `mnemos-memory-server` frozen at 5.2.0), and the install docs no longer describe the bare `pip install vesma` as a third-party project.

- **Tag-contract violations on `POST /memories` answer 422, not a raw 500 (#422/#432 class)** (`src/vesmaro/api/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/http-api.md` EN/RU; tests `tests/test_api.py` +1, falsification-checked against the unfixed tree) — the `TagContractError` (a missing required `mnemos:`/`project:`/`agent:` scope) escaped `create_memory` unhandled as a 500; the `validate_tag_contract` call is now wrapped in the in-file fix pattern: `ValueError` maps to HTTP 422 with the SAME error string — a contract violation is a client error, not a server fault. The docs' errors table moves the row to 422 and drops the known-defect note (a stale `vesma:` scope spelling in that row corrected to the real `mnemos:`); the happy-path create is untouched.

- **Ephemeral directories excluded from the graph index surface (worktree-in-root defect 2026-10-03)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/file_surface.py`, the indexer/staleness/autoindex walk paths, `src/vesmaro/config.py` — new `code_graph.exclude_globs` knob; tests `tests/test_codegraph_exclude_globs.py` +218 lines) — a git worktree checked out INSIDE a registered root (`git worktree add wt/<name>`) was walked like first-party sources: the indexed file count doubled (376→746) and secret fixtures inside the worktree copy were re-poisoned on every index run — any future worktree wave would have reproduced it. Two layers, the first non-liftable: `file_surface.DENY_DIRS` gains `wt` (a nested worktree is never first-party code; the denylist applies to the indexer walk, the staleness predicate and the W-H literal-fallback scan alike), and a new `code_graph.exclude_globs` knob (default `wt/.venv/venv/node_modules/dist/build/.tox/.git`; a bare name matches at any nesting depth, a `/`-bearing glob matches the repo-relative directory path; setting the field REPLACES the default list but can never lift `DENY_DIRS`) prunes directories on top. Wired through `index_full`, the incremental path (staleness check / `index_project`), beacon/staleness, autoindex and the literal scan, so a worktree landing in the root never reads as stale files and never auto-triggers a reindex. Env override: `VESMA_CODE_GRAPH__EXCLUDE_GLOBS` (JSON array).

- **Ghost graph projects are deletable behind a confirm+echo gate (card `vesma-graph-ghost-delete`)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, `src/vesmaro/storage/`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/api/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/graph_cmd.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/{mcp-tools,project-graph,http-api}.md`; tests `tests/test_codegraph_tools.py` +119 lines, `tests/test_cli.py` +25 lines) — a registration whose root vanished from disk (project moved/renamed) was undeletable: `delete_graph_project` resolved through `_resolve_root`, which refuses exactly the missing-root state, and the CLI had no delete twin. Deletion now resolves the project BY NAME/ID (the #450 repoint precedent): a LIVE registration keeps the v1 contract (index purge only), a GHOST is removed entirely — sidecar index AND the main-DB registration row (the projects table has no FKs, so the row delete cannot orphan rows) — behind the evidence gate: `confirm=true` plus `confirm_name` echoing the project name (mesh-RESTORE confirm precedent); gate-less attempts are audited as `delete-refused` (new `AUDIT_ACTIONS` entry). The MCP tool `mnemos_delete_graph_project`, the HTTP twin `DELETE /graph/projects/{project_id}` and the new CLI twin `vesma graph delete <project>` (`--force` + `--confirm-name`, register/repoint house style) all carry the same gate.

- **`install.sh` probes the channel it actually installs (card `vesma-install-sh-channel-mismatch`)** (`scripts/install.sh`; docs `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/install.md`, `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/pypi-publish.md`) — the installer probed the `vesma-memory-server` mirror for the latest version while installing the frozen `mnemos-memory-server` dist, so the detected version and the installed dist could belong to different channels; the version is now probed from the same channel that gets installed — canonical `vesma` — with the mirror kept as an explicit, logged fallback. The venv launcher linked into `~/.local/bin` is the canonical `vesma` CLI (the deprecated `mnemos` alias stays available inside the venv itself), and the container path accepts the canonical `VESMA_API__TOTP_MASTER_KEY` spelling (ADR-0031) alongside the two legacy aliases.

## [5.4.0] — 2026-10-03

### Added

- **Go language support in the project graph (#470, wave W-J)** (`pyproject.toml` — `tree-sitter-go>=0.25.0,<0.26` core dep, same tree-sitter org release line as the Python pack; `src/vesmaro/codegraph/languages.py` — `GO` LanguageSpec, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/indexer.py` — new `GoFileParser` + Go resolution passes; docs `docs/en|ru/user/project-graph.md` — new "Supported languages" section; tests `tests/test_codegraph_go.py` +18 new on a real Go fixture project `tests/data/codegraph_go_project/`) — Go repositories (vesma-mesh, vesma-agent) used to index to ~0 nodes (the language registry was Python-only since wave 1), leaving the graph blind for the ecosystem's Go services: no search, no outline, no trace. Now `.go` files index end-to-end through the SAME pipeline and invariants (PG1–PG7 untouched — the Go parser honors the same never-read comment/string traversal, secrets poisoning and fail-closed limits): top-level funcs → `Function`, methods with receivers → `Method` (qname `pkg.Type.Name`), struct/interface types → `Class`, aliases/other defined types → `Type`, all qnames prefixed with the dotted package directory (`pkg.util.Greeter.Greet`); proven `CALLS` for plain-identifier calls within a Go package (package scope makes the name unique — including cross-file) and `pkg.Ident(...)` through a resolved import (longest repo-directory suffix match; stdlib/external paths resolve to nothing, silently); honest heuristic `USES` for method calls (`obj.Method` — no receiver type inference) and type references (composite literals, `new`/`make` type arguments); `INHERITS` for struct/interface embedding; `IMPORTS` to the package's first sorted file's module; `TESTS` from `foo_test.go` to its own package. Interface satisfaction (implicit in Go), function-variable calls and generic instantiations are honestly skipped — documented in the docs' "Supported languages" section.
- **Hybrid `search_graph`: literal-content fallback + bare-tail `trace_path` disambiguation (wave W-H, owner directive 2026-10-03 «strengthen the graph where the benchmark showed losses»)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, new `src/vesmaro/codegraph/literal_scan.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py` — `code_graph.literal_fallback`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py` tool descriptions; docs `docs/en|ru/user/project-graph.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`, `config.example.yaml`; tests `tests/test_codegraph_hybrid.py` +18 new, `tests/test_codegraph_tools.py` 1 updated) — two benchmark losses fixed: (1) string literals were invisible — searching a literal (e.g. `mnemos_search_graph` as `name="…"` in `mcp_server.py`) returned 0 hits; now, ONLY on an empty symbol result, a bounded read-only literal scan of the REGISTERED root answers `match_kind: "literal"` rows (path/line/snippet, max 20) with a top-level `fallback_used: true` marker (absent otherwise — shape policy: never null/empty; symbol rows gain the additive `match_kind: "symbol"`). The scan reuses the indexer surface denylists (`.git`/`.venv`/`node_modules`/vendored/dotfiles/secret-bearing names never opened), never follows symlinks, skips binaries and >1 MiB files, and is hard-capped (file count / ~2 s wall clock — a capped scan is logged incomplete). SECURITY: every literal row passes the same PG4 secrets detector as snippet issuance — a finding DROPS the row, never issues raw content; poisoned paths (PG3) never issue content; a scan that cannot complete safely degrades to a no-fallback honest empty. The REST twin `POST /graph/search` inherits everything unchanged; the `code_graph.literal_fallback` knob (default ON) disables the leg. (2) `trace_path` refused a bare method tail (`update_fields`), forcing a 3-call dance; now an exact qname behaves byte-identically, a UNIQUE tail traces directly, an AMBIGUOUS tail answers a helpful NOT-error payload — ranked `candidate_list` (qname/kind/path/lines, max 10; `candidate_count` honest total), `candidates: true` marker and a re-run-with-the-qualified-name hint — and a missing symbol stays a clear not-found refusal.
- **Pack instruction `vesma-codegraph.instructions.md` — graph-first code search (W-G graph adoption)** (`integrations/instructions/vesma-codegraph.instructions.md`; tests `tests/test_integration.py` `TestInstructionPack` +5 new) — the pack now TEACHES the reflex the value report found missing: in a repo registered in the project graph, symbol discovery goes `vesma_search_graph` first and caller/neighbor tracing via `vesma_trace_path` after the qualified name is resolved, with `vesma_get_file_outline` before big reads and `vesma_get_code_snippet` for exact ranges; `vesma_check_graph_coverage` decides «indexed and clean» vs «missing/unindexed» before anyone concludes a symbol does not exist. Grep stays the tool for string literals (tool names, route paths, env vars, log strings) and unregistered repos. Every graph tool takes the `agent` attribution param — pass your own slug. Drift pins: the instruction ships by NAME, carries the pack safety contract + the full tool chain, and deploys through the real `IntegrationManager` to the copilot target.
- **`code_graph.secret_allowlist` example locked to the fixture pattern (machine-tuning fixation)** (`config.example.yaml`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/project-graph.md`) — the commented example now shows the canonical fake-secret-fixture pattern `tests/**` + `benchmarks/**` (as applied live on the owner machine 2026-10-03) and states the two hard rules explicitly: the PRODUCT default stays `[]` because PG3 is a security gate, not an inconvenience, and the PG4 issuance scan is NEVER waived by the allowlist. Docs get the same yaml example next to the `secret_allowlist` row.
- **`project_graph_status` hints: an all-fixtures poisoned set points at the allowlist** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py` — new `poisoned_fixture_hint()`; `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py` tool description; docs `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`; tests `tests/test_codegraph_tools.py` `TestPoisonedFixtureHint` +5 new) — the status payload gains an additive `hints` key: when EVERY poisoned path lives under a test-like tree (`tests/**` / `benchmarks/**`), it carries one line — «all poisoned files match test fixtures — consider `code_graph.secret_allowlist`» — so the adoption path tells the operator about the escape hatch instead of leaving a scary number. Any poisoned path OUTSIDE those trees suppresses the hint (real scrutiny is not coached); a clean or allowlisted-drained project reports `[]`. The REST twin (`/graph/status/{project_id}`) inherits the key unchanged.
- **`vesma update` becomes a subcommand family — `check` / `apply` / `timer install|uninstall|status` / `components` (board card `vesma-update-family-components`, standing design rule: flags do not replace subcommands)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_updates.py` +22 new, `tests/test_update_components.py` +18 new) — `vesma update check` is the report-only path (never prompts, never applies); `vesma update apply [--to VERSION] [--scope user] [-y] [--verbose]` is the apply path (reuses the #460 quiet-pip/summary/failure-tail machinery unchanged — invoking `apply` IS the confirmation, `-y` is an accepted no-op); `vesma update timer install|uninstall|status` manage/inspect the weekly systemd user timer (status: unit presence, enabled state, last trigger via `show --property`, `--json`; inside a box it also reports the HOST units). Plain `vesma update` keeps its 5.2.0 behavior (report + interactive apply prompt in a TTY).
- **Component inventory: `vesma update components [--json]`** (`src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`; tests `tests/test_update_components.py`) — one table of every updatable piece with its update path: pip dist (detected alias + version → `vesma update apply`), integration pack (version + aggregate stale/missing via the doctor's `IntegrationManager.verify` across detected targets — no logic fork → `vesma integration update`/`setup`), cortex bundle (name + weights revision from the shipped `vesma-cortex-v1/manifest.json` → ships with the wheel), embedder (model id + `config_fingerprint` fingerprint, with a loud `VINTAGE MISMATCH` when the vector store's stored `model_fingerprint` differs → `vesma reindex`), npm package (global version → `vesma update apply`), update timer (present/enabled + box note → `vesma update timer install`), prod venvs (ONE grouped row → MANUAL GATE, upgrade runbook), go binaries (report only → goreleaser). Local state only — zero network; honest `-` where a version is not cheaply readable.
- **pip alias family row + cached family latest** (`src/vesmaro/updates.py` — new `family_latest()`; `src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`) — the surfaces report collapses the `vesma-memory-server` / `vesma` alias loop into ONE row `pip: vesma (family)` (the canonical names are the same codebase; `mnemos-memory-server` is the deprecated legacy mirror, named in docs only): Installed = the detected dist+version, Latest = the family max over the aliases' published versions (cached in the shared `update-check.json` under a `family` key with the same 24h/1h TTL discipline; a fresh per-dist answer from `check_for_update` is reused so one CLI run costs at most one GET per alias), and the Note names the alias actually installed — `up to date (installed as vesma-memory-server — same codebase, alias package)`; nothing installed reads `not installed — pip install --user vesma`. An alias lagging behind no longer reads as "up to date".
- **#468 loud guard: `update timer install` refuses inside a distrobox/container** (`src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`) — detection now also honours the `DISTROBOX_ENTER_ENV`/`CONTAINER_ID` env variables (alongside the HOME `/.distrobox` layout and `/run/.containerenv`); a box install without `--force` exits 1 explaining the units would be dead and naming the host-side path (run on the HOST — it wires one ExecStart line per box — or see the upgrade runbook); `--force` on `timer install` is the explicit escape hatch for the intentional box-aware install into the HOST home; a container with no derivable host home refuses with the manual recipe even under `--force`. `timer status` notes whether the machine appears to be a box. The `--install-timer` deprecated alias carries the same guard.
- **`vesma doctor fix` and `vesma doctor paths` subcommands (board card `vesma-doctor-fixes-467`, standing design rule: flags do not replace subcommands)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_doctor_subcommands.py` +19 new, `tests/test_agent_wiring.py`, `tests/test_doctor_paths.py` updated) — the repair path and the paths overview are now SUBCOMMANDS: `vesma doctor fix [--dry-run] [--json]` runs the same fix pipeline the `--fix` flag used to (stale integration → `integration update`, unwired agents → `vesma integration setup`, missing MCP registration → MCP setup, re-check after fixing), and `vesma doctor paths [--json]` prints the paths overview without health checks. The old flag forms remain as HIDDEN deprecated aliases with identical behavior and a one-line stderr hint (`use: vesma doctor fix` / `use: vesma doctor paths`) — real stdout stays clean for piped `--json` scripts. `--json` stays a normal (visible) output-format flag on the default check run; options placed before the subcommand word produce an explicit stderr note instead of being silently dropped.
- **`vesma tags audit` — tag-contract conformance scan with an additive heal** (`src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_tags_audit.py` +15 new) — scans the SQLite store for entries violating the tag contract (the same rule as the doctor's Tag-contract check: at least one `project:*`, one `agent:*`, one `mnemos:*` tag) and flags entries whose tags JSON is unparseable.
