Default mode is a dry-run report (id, snippet, current tags, missing prefixes; `--limit N` caps the LISTING, the scan always covers the store; `--json` for both modes). `--apply` executes the owner-approved heal policy — ADD the missing prefixes only, never remove existing tags: missing `mnemos:*` → `mnemos:legacy`, missing `agent:*` → `agent:user`, missing `project:*` → the row's `project` column slug-normalized (lax mode: lowercase, spaces → hyphens) or `project:unsorted` when the column is empty; corrupt rows are healed to the three contract prefixes. Idempotent by construction (a second run reports 0 non-conformant); writes go through `update_fields` (plain UPDATE) so the FTS5 external-content index stays consistent.
- **The doctor's Integration check now names the concrete files** (`src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`; tests `tests/test_doctor_subcommands.py`) — missing/stale deployed files are listed as `target: path` lines inside the check detail (capped at the first 5 + "…and N more") instead of a bare count, so the report says WHERE to look.
- **#467 completion — the doctor's MCP check sees the legacy VS Code surface** (`src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`; tests `tests/test_doctor_mcp_check.py` +5) — the 5.3.0 registry-driven rewrite scans every `targets.yaml` target with an `mcp.config` (codex TOML, cursor, claude-code, windsurf, zcode, opencode, agents, pi) but missed the well-known `~/.config/Code/User/mcp.json` where the FIX path's fallback (`register_mcp()` legacy, mcp-setup.sh) registers targets without their own `mcp.config` — a VS Code-only deployment was permanently WARNed "not registered" after a successful fix. The check now also scans that file (only when it exists, so MCP-less registries stay n/a-green), honoring both key generations and the corrupt-config no-crash contract; report-only as before.

### Changed

- **`vesma update` flag forms are now hidden deprecated aliases of the subcommands (standing design rule: flags do not replace subcommands; migration from the flag spellings)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`) — `--check` → `vesma update check`, `--yes`/`-y` → `vesma update apply`, `--to` → `vesma update apply --to`, `--scope` → `vesma update apply --scope`, `--install-timer`/`--uninstall-timer` → `vesma update timer install`/`timer uninstall`. Every alias keeps working identically (scripts and the shipped systemd unit `ExecStart=vesma update --yes --scope=user` are a MANDATORY compatibility case) and prints one `[deprecated] … use: vesma update …` hint line to STDERR; stdout stays clean for pipes/JSON. Options placed before the subcommand word produce an explicit stderr note instead of being silently dropped. `--verbose` stays a visible flag (it still configures the apply output). The non-TTY hint text now names the canonical form (`apply with: vesma update apply`).

### Fixed

- **Cortex bundle swapped back to the recalled-safe B1 artifact (weights `beb0a65d…`) — B2 (weights `71f0572d…`) recalled by investigation verdict #480** (`src/vesmaro/models/vesma-cortex-v1/model.onnx` + `manifest.json`; tests `tests/test_decision_cortex.py` pins + smoke re-measured, `tests/test_update_components.py` comment) — the 5.4.0 wheel ships the B2 revision whose ranking inversion on identity pairs (a record graded BELOW the 0.5 duplicate cut against ITSELF, P≈0.011; investigation #480: feature-column-order desync, recalibration impossible) would otherwise be re-delivered by the next release. The bundle is replaced byte-exact with the B1 artifact (`model.onnx` sha256 `beb0a65d…`, 98112 bytes; corpus fingerprint `edd94073…`; trained 2026-10-01) — the vintage the prod cortex registry already runs; the `decision_provider` flag stays untouched (default path unchanged — pure artifact hygiene). Adversarial sanity suite (cortex `eval.sanity`, run against both the B1 source and the shipped bundle) passes all checks: self-pair P=1.0000 (≥0.9), near-boundary P=1.0000 (≥0.5), unrelated P=0.0000 (<0.5), monotonicity non-increasing over the fixed cosine ladder. The smoke test re-pins the measured B1 verdicts on real corpus records: self 0.999992 (≥ cut — the #480 inversion signature is gone), twin 7.54e-06 and family 0.016314 (both < cut — ordering monotone in cosine; the family band is sharper than the W5d-era surface, pinned as measured; retraining is a separate wave per #480). No `465d33e`/`71f0572d` traces remain in shipped manifests.
- **Test pins updated to the B2 artifact contract (weights sha256, manifest schema) — merge-gate miss of 465d33e, caught by the 5.4.0 release preflight** (`tests/test_decision_cortex.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`, `tests/test_update_components.py`) — the B2 bundle swap renamed the manifest weights key `weights_sha256` → `sha256` (adding `embedder_pin`/`corpus_fingerprint`) without touching its consumers: the 5 red preflight tests are re-pinned to the shipped artifact (weights `71f0572d…`, corpus `ebd9b17a…`, computed `sha256sum` over the shipped `model.onnx`); `vesma update components` cortex row reads the new key (with a pre-B2 fallback — it silently reported an empty `rev` since 465d33e) and its test now asserts the rev equals the shipped manifest hash instead of a vacuous prefix. **OPEN FINDING, not closed by this fix**: the B2 graph itself fails its adopted calibration surface — through the frozen 13-feature contract it grades every real-record pair below the 0.5 duplicate cut, including a record against itself (W5d graded the self-pair ~0.80), and per-dimension sensitivity shows `cos_target` nearly unweighted while `char4_containment`/`body_len_delta` dominate — the signature of a feature-column-order desync between the B2 export and the contract its own metadata claims. The smoke test now pins the measured B2 verdicts with a loud regression note; the artifact needs training-side investigation/recalibration before its `sens 0.989` adopt claim can be trusted. (Superseded by the B1 recall swap above — the artifact part; this entry stays as the historical record of the pin repair.)
- **Agent wiring check produces SKIP instead of permanent WARN on non-Copilot machines** (`src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/agent_wiring.py`; tests `tests/test_agent_wiring.py` +1 new) — new `CheckStatus.SKIP` (exit-code neutral, dim marker in the table, `"skip"` in JSON): when the `~/.copilot/agents` directory does not exist OR the integration registry sees no `copilot` harness (its detect paths — `~/.copilot/instructions` / `~/.copilot/skills` — absent), the check reports SKIP ("not applicable") instead of WARN; a leftover agents directory full of unwired files no longer produces a permanent "0/N wired" warning on machines that do not run the Copilot harness. When the Copilot harness IS detected, the check keeps its full PASS/WARN behavior.
- Deprecated `doctor --fix` / `--paths` flag aliases print a one-line deprecation hint to stderr (the flags keep working — see the Added entries above).

### Corrected 2026-10-03 — completion entries misfiled into the released [5.3.0] notes

These three shell-completion entries were erroneously filed under [5.3.0] although the work landed in the 5.4.0 wave (commits `b703deb` and `2fbecfc`, 2026-10-03 — after 5.3.0 was released on 2026-10-02). They are relocated here verbatim with this explicit correction marker; the [5.3.0] section carries the matching correction note. Released version sections are append/annotate-only — see the CHANGELOG rule in CONTRIBUTING.md.

- **Custom shell-completion engine with descriptions, plus a doctor check** (`src/vesmaro/cli/complete_cmd.py` new, `src/vesmaro/cli/completion.py` rewritten, `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_completion.py` +30 new, `tests/test_cli.py` updated) — new hidden command `vesma __complete WORDS... N` introspects the LIVE typer/click command tree (no hardcoded command tables, so CLI restructuring is reflected automatically) and emits one `value<TAB>description` candidate per line: top-level commands, nested subcommands at any depth, option names (long form), option values incl. the `--flag=` prefix form, and enum/choice values; exit code 0 always, empty output on no match/malformed input, no DB, no network. `vesma completion bash|zsh|fish` now installs Vesma's OWN per-shell scripts calling `__complete` instead of typer's generated ones (which carry no descriptions in any shell): zsh renders candidates via `_describe` with descriptions visible in the menu, fish renders `value\tdescription` pairs natively, bash completes values only (readline cannot render descriptions — documented honestly). Scripts bind the primary program name (`vesma`, derived from `sys.argv[0]` at install time) plus legacy aliases (`vesmaro` always, `mnemos` when found on PATH), so Tab works for every real binary name. `vesma doctor` gains a "Completion" check (PASS/WARN with the exact fix hint: script file exists + canonical rc source line + script binds the primary name). Typer's built-in top-level `--install-completion/--show-completion` options remain untouched (unification parked for 6.0).
- **A half-removed legacy completion block could leave an orphaned `fi` that silently killed the whole rc — the product is now immune (block-aware migration + `bash -n` validation; 2026-10-03 field incident on the owner's vscode-box)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/completion.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_completion.py` +14 new/updated) — a historical edit had stripped the `if [ -f ~/.mnemos/completion/mnemos.bash ]; then source …` lines from `~/.bashrc` but left the block's `fi` behind; bash aborts parsing the ENTIRE rc at an orphaned `fi`, so nothing below it ever ran (completion dead even with the canonical source line present) while `_is_installed()` — a line grep — reported "already installed", and the old line-based migration could itself create the same damage (removing a multi-line `if` opener left its `fi` orphaned). Now the legacy migration operates on WHOLE shell constructs: a matched `if …; then` opener removes the entire if/then(/else)/fi block (nesting-aware, down to the matching `fi`; a self-contained one-line `if …; then …; fi` stays a single-line removal; an opener whose block never closes removes only itself), and orphaned pure control lines (`fi`/`then`/`else`/`elif`/`do`/`done`/`esac` whose opener is gone — removed by us or by an older external edit) are cleaned up as well. Additionally, EVERY rc write is validated with `bash -n` (`zsh -n` when a zsh binary exists; fish needs no check; script-file writes excluded): the pre-edit content is kept in memory, a non-parsing result is restored byte-for-byte, the failing line number/text plus the shell's own error are printed, and the installer exits non-zero — Vesma can no longer write a broken rc. `vesma doctor`'s Completion check now also runs `bash -n` on the rc and WARNs with the exact failing line and the repair hint instead of PASSing an rc whose source line can never be reached.
- **Shell completion was bound to the wrong program name — Tab completion was dead for `vesma`** (`src/vesmaro/cli/completion.py`) — the installer registered `complete -F … vesmaro` (script `~/.mnemos/completion/vesmaro.bash`) while the binary users actually invoke is `vesma`; a stale pre-rebrand `source ~/.mnemos/completion/vesmaro.bash` line in user rc files then made `_is_installed()` report "already installed" (substring match), so the bug self-healed never. Now: scripts are written as `vesma.<shell>` and bound to all real binary names, `_is_installed()` matches ONLY the exact canonical source line, and every run of `vesma completion <shell>` migrates away ALL legacy rc forms (old `eval "$(… --show-completion …)"` lines, `# Added by` marker comments, `if [ -f ~/.mnemos/completion/mnemos.bash ]; then source …; fi` blocks, and `[ -f … ] && source …` one-liners referencing old script names), leaving exactly one canonical guarded source line per shell.

## [5.3.0] — 2026-10-02

> **Correction (2026-10-03):** the three shell-completion entries previously listed in this section — the custom completion engine (`b703deb`), the block-aware legacy-rc migration / orphaned-`fi` fix (`2fbecfc`), and the wrong-program-name fix (`b703deb`) — actually shipped in **5.4.0**, not in the released 5.3.0 (2026-10-02). They are relocated verbatim to the «Corrected 2026-10-03» subsection of [5.4.0] below; released history is annotated with this marker, not silently rewritten.

### Added

- **Native awareness heartbeat — wave 1 canary slice (#473; ADR-0035; board card `vesma-eye-wave1-canary`; the `canary` FLIP itself stays gated on wave-0 telemetry)** (`src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/hooks.py`, `src/vesmaro/heartbeat.py`, `src/vesmaro/api/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/awareness.py`, `src/vesmaro/metrics/{schema,sink}.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`; tests `tests/test_native_heartbeat.py`, `tests/test_awareness.py`) — (a) the optional `agent` argument (string, `maxLength: 64`, the `agent:<slug>` slug shape) added to the `mnemos_search` and `mnemos_recall_context` MCP tool schemas: the heartbeat's identity extraction used to see `None` on the funnel's most frequent calls, so shadow telemetry under-measured delivery; legacy no-agent calls keep working and stay identity-less (additive only — required lists and existing arguments untouched). (b) hooks `include_awareness` default is now MODE-LINKED behind the same `awareness.native_heartbeat_mode` flag: when the mode is `canary`/`on`, `mnemos_hooks` (MCP) and `POST /hooks/{action}` (REST) compose awareness BY DEFAULT (argument absent → compose; explicit boolean always wins; `off`/`shadow` stay byte-identical), and the C13 deny-list gains the awareness surfaces themselves — `mnemos_awareness` and `mnemos_hooks` never carry the native tail (cascade SEC-2: a hooks call under canary composes its own section; a native tail on top would be a double render), while still landing in the `tool_call` denominator. (c) cascade follow-ups: SEC-4 — the `awareness_events` kind CHECK now knows `conflict_hint_emitted`, with an introspection-gated create+copy+drop+rename migration (`migrate_awareness_events_kinds`, the main-store A0 edge-kinds pattern) so wave-0 sidecars migrate on their next open with zero rows lost; ARCH-2 — the delta high-water now spans the FULL window feed including delta-excluded (no-federate) rows, so an excluded write can no longer pin the cursor below itself and re-force the expensive probe→compose leg on every call up to the C14 rate cap; SEC-5 — the delta-stage hydration bound (≤200 rows, `DELTA_FEED_LIMIT`) is pinned by a regression test (the bound already held in the landed wave-0 code; the test makes it non-regressible). Defaults untouched: `native_heartbeat_mode` stays `off`; `off`/`shadow` behavior byte-identical (CI pins green); no VERSION bump. Protected contracts (`mnemos_*` tool names, `mnemos:` tags, `MNEMOS_*`/`VESMARO_*` env names, `~/.mnemos` paths) untouched.

### Changed

- **Canonical `VESMA_` env prefix introduced alongside the deprecated `VESMARO_` prefix (board card `vesma-env-vesma-prefix`; owner directive: the product end-state is `vesma`-branded, removal of `VESMARO_*`/`MNEMOS_*` deferred to 6.0 — ADR-0031 dual-period pattern, same as `VESMA_MCP_BRAND`)** (`src/vesmaro/config.py`, `src/vesmaro/decision_jev.py`, `src/vesmaro/updates.py`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/api/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`, `src/vesmaro/decision_provider.py`, `src/vesmaro/embeddings/__init__.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/{cli-reference,getting-started,mcp-tools,project-graph}.md`, `docs/en|ru/index.md`, admin runbooks; tests `tests/test_env_dual_prefix.py` +16 new, `tests/test_decision_jev.py`) — `VESMA_<SECTION>__<FIELD>` now resolves for every setting and takes precedence over its `VESMARO_` twin (per-field: config.yaml > `VESMA_` env > `VESMARO_` env > #139 short aliases, now with `VESMA_DATA_DIR`/`VESMA_VAULT__VAULT_PATH` twins > `VESMA_` .env > `VESMARO_` .env > defaults); `VESMARO_*` keeps working exactly as before when the `VESMA_` twin is absent, so existing deployments are unaffected. The config-file path env var, the update-check kill switch, auto-collect, the ORT thread cap, the export passphrase and the Jev OpenRouter key indirection gained the same dual read (`VESMA_CONFIG`, `VESMA_UPDATES_CHECK`, `VESMA_AUTO_COLLECT`, `VESMA_ORT_THREADS`, `VESMA_EXPORT_PASSPHRASE`, `VESMA_OPENROUTER_API_KEY` — the last is now the `decision_jev_api_key_env` DEFAULT, with a loud `code=DEPRECATED-ENV` warn when only the legacy twin is set; `vesma serve` propagates its `--host/--port` under the canonical name so the CLI override keeps beating ambient legacy env in the worker). One deliberate fragility fix on the way: `dotenv_filtering=only_existing` — previously ANY unknown key in a `.env` file (e.g. `FOO=bar`) crashed `Settings()` with `extra_forbidden`; unknown `.env` keys are now ignored, and `VESMA_*` entries in `.env` work under the same precedence. Docstrings/docs flipped to the canonical names with one deprecation note per surface (`VESMARO_*` = 5.0–5.2 spelling, accepted until 6.0; `MNEMOS_*` = 4.x, no longer read). Protected contracts untouched: no removals, no VERSION bump, console scripts / `mnemos_*` tool names / `mnemos:` tags / `~/.mnemos` paths unchanged. Tests: 16 new precedence/compat tests (`tests/test_env_dual_prefix.py`), jev fallback pins in `test_decision_jev.py`; full suite green (mesh-family files excluded on this box — generated `mnemos_core_api_pb2` unavailable), `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean.
- **Graph registration hardening (#464, 4 review items)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/audit.py`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/graph_cmd.py`; docs `docs/en/user/project-graph.md`, `config.example.yaml`) — (P2-1) registration IS a read-scope grant, so the agent call path (`mnemos_register_project`) is now gated by `code_graph.agent_registration` (default `true`, matching the code_graph default-on convention; `false` reserves registration to the operator — the `vesma graph register` CLI path, distinguished by the explicit `source="operator"` call-path parameter, is never gated); a gated attempt is refused AND audited. (P3-2) manual register/repoint refusals (bad root, name collision, root-taken cross-jump, not-registered) are audit-first-class like the auto path — exactly one `manual-register-refused` / `repoint-refused` row per refused attempt, reason sanitized to basenames (the 10173a2a-4 sidecar hygiene). (P3-3) `repoint` enforces its docstring: only a GHOST registration (old root gone on disk) may be re-pointed — a live old root is refused (move-root is not repoint), pathless registrations may still be attached. (P3-4) the `$HOME`/fs-root forbidden-root check runs on `os.path.realpath` — a symlink to `$HOME`/fs-root or a `..`-laden spelling no longer bypasses the string compare. (P3-1) the one-time «vesma server updated» notice is no longer burned by a failed dispatch: `last_reported_server_version` is re-stamped only AFTER the hint actually rode a response — a failed dispatch keeps the notice pending and the next dispatch delivers it (retry survives; at-most-once delivery and fail-open store behavior unchanged).

- `doctor`'s generic MCP check is now registry-driven and dual-key (#467): it scans every target `integrations/targets.yaml` declares with an `mcp.config` — the JSON surfaces in their declared shape (`mcpServers` / zcode `mcp.servers` / opencode direct `mcp`), the Codex TOML config via stdlib `tomllib` (`[mcp_servers.<key>]`), and the pi TypeScript bridge by presence — instead of the hardcoded 5-path candidate list, and it accepts BOTH server key generations (brand-primary `vesma`, legacy `mnemos`) during the dual period until 6.0: any `vesma` key → green, legacy-`mnemos`-only → green with a migrate note, nothing → the existing not-registered warning; report-only (per-target verdict + key generation in the detail, never writes). `vesma update --install-timer`/`--uninstall-timer` inside a distrobox now operate on the HOST units (#468) — the box previously wrote dead units into `<box-home>/.config/systemd/user/` that the host user manager never loads while reporting success; the units go to the host home (derived from the resolved `HOME` before `/.distrobox/`, no subprocess probing), the service accumulates one `distrobox-enter -n <box>` ExecStart line per box (idempotent), enabling tries `systemctl --user -M <user>@.host` and on failure prints the exact manual host-side commands incl. the `sudo loginctl enable-linger` hint (never fails the command, never runs sudo); uninstall is symmetric — only this box's ExecStart line is removed, the units are disabled+removed only when no box lines remain; non-box host behavior unchanged.

- Deploy image pins flipped `5.1.2` → `5.2.0` (7 pins in 6 files: docker-compose default, helm Chart example, podman kube manifest, quadlet unit, podman + deploy READMEs; `install.sh` pins nothing — it derives from `VERSION`). Reference manifests only — existing installs update via `vesma update` / `helm upgrade`.
- **Final rebrand sweep — live surfaces cleaned to `vesma` branding (protected contracts untouched; board card `vesma-rebrand-final-sweep`)** (178 paths: 152 modified + 26 renamed files, across `docs/`, `src/`, `integrations/`, `scripts/`, `contrib/`, `deploy/`, README/CONTRIBUTING/ARCHITECTURE/NOTICE/PLAN) — every remaining product self-naming `Mnemos`/`mnemos`/`Vesmaro`/`vesmaro` in prose, comments, docstrings, log/error strings, docs pages, examples and help text replaced with `vesma`/`Vesma` (or the obsolete mention removed). Shipped-artifact names renamed with their references in one commit: helm chart `deploy/helm/vesmaro/` → `deploy/helm/vesma/` (chart name + helper includes + example release/namespace names); podman kube manifest `mnemos-pod.yaml` → `vesma-pod.yaml`; systemd units `contrib/systemd/mnemos-{server,watcher,sync}.{service,timer}` → `vesma-*` (the dedicated peer-side SSH account/user `mnemos-sync` and key comments stay — peer config contract); node-install layout `/opt/vesmaro` → `/opt/vesma` (+ `/etc/vesmaro`, `/var/lib/vesmaro`, container templates, `--vesma-repo` flag) — existing node installs need one reinstall/adopt pass; banner asset `mnemos-banner.svg` → `vesma-banner.svg` (the deliberate Mnemosyne lore section stays). Code renames (not wire/storage load-bearing, full cross-ref sweep incl. tests): `MnemosSDK` → `VesmaSDK`, `MnemosConfig` → `VesmaConfig`, `MnemosCoreServicer` → `VesmaCoreServicer` (our servicer class; the wire service `MnemosCore` in `mnemos_core_api.proto` stays until 6.0), `MnemosMemoryProvider` → `VesmaMemoryProvider`; MCP `serverInfo.name` → `vesma`; default export filename `mnemos-export.*` → `vesma-export.*` (CLI default + HTTP Content-Disposition); MCP registration bin resolution now prefers the canonical `vesma` binary with the legacy `mnemos` entry point kept as fallback. `mcp-presets.md`/`adapter-template.md` teach the canonical `vesma` server key and command (drift guards retargeted). Docs: CLI examples swept to `vesma *` (canonical short hook; legacy `mnemos` entry point unchanged in `pyproject.toml`), stale `vesmaro/vesmaro` repo links retargeted to `vesmaro/vesma`, `NOTICE` updated, `PLAN.md` product naming cleaned with its historical fork steps kept verbatim. Deliberately UNTOUCHED (protected until 6.0, ADR-0031 class): `mnemos_*` MCP tool names, `mnemos:` tag namespace, `MNEMOS_`/`VESMARO_` env prefixes, `~/.mnemos` paths and `mnemos.db`, `mnemos-memory-server` dist, `mnemos_core_api` proto and the `MnemosCore` wire service, `mnemos.federation.v1` payload schema, the python import package `vesmaro` and the `import mnemos` shim, dual-pattern legacy recognition (`vesmaro-agent`/`mnemos-mesh` binary names, `mnemos-prod*` laptop units, `mnemos-integration` stamps, `MNEMOS1` magic, `project:mnemos` store slug), `deploy/helm` org paths under `ghcr.io/vesmaro`, and historical records (`docs/project/{adr,reports,sessions,decisions}`, `CHANGELOG` history, dev-plan wave records, `docs/experiments` run provenance). Follow-up resolved en passant: `doctor`'s generic MCP-registered check now recognizes the brand-primary `vesma` key (was legacy-only — the pre-existing gap noted in 5.2.0). Gates: residue grep over live surfaces returns zero rows under the protected-contract filter; full suite 4980 passed / 3 skipped; `ruff check` + `ruff format --check` clean on all touched Python files; `mypy --strict src/vesmaro src/mnemos` clean.

### Fixed

- Cortex provider tails from the PR #458 SHIP review (#472; review thread #459; three P3 hardening items) (`src/vesmaro/decision_provider.py`, `src/vesmaro/decision_jev.py`; tests `tests/test_decision_cortex.py` +4): the output scalar decode (`float(tensor[0])`) in `_run_graph` now sits under the `CortexInferError` guard — a `(1,)`-shaped tensor with an undecodable value degrades that verdict via the typed fail-open seam instead of escaping as a raw `TypeError` (defense-in-depth: the load-time smoke inference still rejects such graphs first); the `CortexPinError` recalibration warn in `resolve_decision_provider` now carries the machine-parseable `code=` token every neighbouring warn already had (a strict `code=`-prefix parser no longer misses exactly the recalibration line), pinned by the wiring test; and `validate_cortex_metadata_props` gained its negative test through the REAL `_load` path — a stubbed `ort.InferenceSession` with drifted `metadata_props` (`feature_set_sha256` mismatch, major-2 version) refuses construction with `CORTEX-E-META`, not only on the pure-function surface.

### Security

- Security dependency floors raised past the moved advisory DB (#476): `pyjwt>=2.15.0` (PYSEC-2026-4140..4152; 2.13.0 floor had covered PYSEC-2026-120/175-179), `urllib3>=2.8.0` (PYSEC-2026-4177); transitive `anyio` lifted to 4.15.1 via the lock (PYSEC-2026-4023/24/25). PYSEC-2026-4146 has no fixed pyjwt release yet — pinned ignore in `make security`, drop when the fix lands.

## [5.2.0] — 2026-10-02

### Added

- **Four native harness targets: codex, cursor, claude-code, windsurf (ADR-0033 contract phase H-2; board card `vesma-harness-targets-h2`; owner directive: any harness works with the vesma memory engine natively)** (`integrations/targets.yaml`, `src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/memory_status.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/integration-guide.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md`, `integrations/mcp-presets.md`, `integrations/engine-manifest.yaml`; tests in `tests/test_integration.py` + `tests/test_integration_agents_md.py`) — (1) **codex**: MCP registers in `~/.codex/config.toml` under `[mcp_servers.vesma]` via a NEW stdlib-only TOML-merge engine (ADR-0024 P-B.3) — a surgical text-level splice of the managed table region (header line → next table header) that preserves every user byte outside it, validates both the original and merged text with `tomllib` and refuses any merge that drifts outside `mcp_servers` (fail-closed: inline-table/dotted forms, broken files and child-table misfires are refused with a clear note, never half-written); the always-on behavioral pack injects as a stamped block into `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` (agents_md paired-block engine). (2) **cursor** (existing target extended): MCP merge into `~/.cursor/mcp.json` (top-level `mcpServers`, the shared JSON engine); the pre-existing stamped instructions copy into `~/.cursor/rules/` is kept unchanged, and NO `.mdc` is generated — Cursor's rule frontmatter dialect is its own, so the honest smaller surface wins (behavioural rule via the Rules UI, documented). (3) **claude-code**: user-scope MCP merge into `~/.claude.json` (additive — Claude Code's own state keys preserved as data) + the always-on block injected into the native global memory file `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. (4) **windsurf**: MCP-only target — additive merge into `~/.codeium/windsurf/mcp_config.json`; deliberately deploys NO files (the built-in `memories/` directory belongs to the harness per the ADR-0033 overlay semantics, and Windsurf rules are workspace/UI-managed with no stable global surface). Shared discipline across all four: static registry paths only, idempotent re-runs, `--dry-run`/`--home` supported, versioned stamps on every deployed file, uninstall removes only stamped files plus the evidence-checked `vesma` MCP entry (legacy `mnemos` key migrated/removed; foreign entries under our keys never touched), legacy-key TOML migration mirrors the JSON engine. `memory status` now reads the Codex TOML config too (server KEYS only, values never printed). Tests: `TestCodexTarget` (13 — schema, create/merge/env-preserve/legacy-migration/idempotency, corrupt-TOML and inline-form refusals, unregister evidence checks, full lifecycle, real-pack roundtrip), `TestH2JsonTargets` (8), `TestH2RegistryAndDetect` (2), `TestMemoryStatusCodex` (1), 4 shipped-registry schema guards in `test_integration_agents_md.py`; `tests/test_integration.py` 209 → 250 passed (+1 skipped unchanged). Known follow-up (pre-existing, out of scope): `doctor`'s generic MCP check still scans only its hardcoded JSON candidate list and only the legacy `mnemos` key, so it does not yet see the new configs (nor the brand-primary `vesma` key of older targets).
- **Cortex decision provider `vesma`: the `vesma-cortex-v1` ONNX bundle ships in the wheel + `VesmaProvider` behind a flag, fail-open (W3/W5d, #458)** (`src/vesmaro/models/vesma-cortex-v1/{model.onnx,manifest.json}` — new; `src/vesmaro/cortex/`; tests `tests/test_decision_cortex.py`, `tests/test_cortex_network_isolation.py`) — the ADOPT-calibrated artifact (98 KB, 13 frozen pair features) rides the wheel exactly like `vesma-embed-v1`, with a manifest that pins the embedder pin, corpus fingerprint, feature-set hash and graph contract — a weights-sha drift is a recalibration event (`CORTEX-E-PIN`, loud refusal), never a silent swap. `VesmaProvider` answers is-duplicate with the calibrated probability (not a step; the 0.5 application threshold lives in product code), runs a CPU ORT session (`VESMARO_ORT_THREADS`, default 4) with a startup smoke inference, and is guarded by a 5 MB self-containment gate plus an AST tripwire proving ZERO network imports in the provider module. Flag semantics: `decision_provider: vesma` opts in; the default stays `deterministic` — flipping it is an owner decision after field experience. Every failure path is fail-open: any `CORTEX-E-*` load/metadata/schema error degrades to the deterministic provider with a machine-parseable `code=` warn (ingest is never blocked); per-request INFER/SCHEMA failures degrade the single verdict to the deterministic step rule.
- **Native awareness heartbeat — wave 0 shadow (ADR-0035; #453/#465)** (`src/vesmaro/settings` awareness section, `src/vesmaro/awareness.py`, `src/vesmaro/heartbeat.py`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/storage/`; docs `docs/project/adr/0035-*`) — a new `awareness.native_heartbeat_mode: off|shadow|canary|on` knob (env `VESMARO_AWARENESS__NATIVE_HEARTBEAT_MODE`). Default `off` = the kill switch — byte-identical engine behaviour, CI-pinned; `shadow` composes and measures WITHOUT rendering anything; `canary`/`on` append one tail TextContent to capped tool responses. Admin contour only: nothing changes for any user until the mode is switched on. The engine rides a sub-millisecond `exists_since(project, since)` delta probe (zero row hydration) backed by the new composite `(project, created_at)` memories index, a session-free `awrh:` cursor namespace (deliberately distinct from legacy `awr:` — no parse-time aliasing), a per-(project, agent) rate cap (default 30/min, 0 disables), a ≤120-token envelope with the deterministic calm-line and ORDER-only relevance (the sort key never leaves the function — no score oracle), and a deny-list (`assemble_context`/export/import). Wave 0 also lands the measurement plane: an additive `awareness_events` table in the vitals sidecar (`CREATE IF NOT EXISTS` — legacy sidecars gain it on their next open; 90-day retention; kinds peer_write / delta_available / heartbeat_delivery / heartbeat_suppressed / tool_call; zero peer content — identity slugs plus a fail-closed meta allowlist; a refused meta drops the whole write loudly, never fatally), wired to every dispatched tool call. Waves 1–2 (opt-in depth surfaces, hooks integration) remain open on #453.
- **Code-graph project lifecycle: `vesma graph repoint` + `root_missing` (#450) and agent-side registration `mnemos_register_project` / `vesma graph register` (#454)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/project-graph.md`, `mcp-tools.md`) — a project whose root moved on disk was stuck (auto-index no-ops, explicit index refuses confinement, the only exit was nuclear delete): `vesma graph repoint <project> <new-root>` rewrites the root, purges the stale index, lifts the auto-path suspension and bumps the graph epoch — confinement-gated (existing directory, project-marker-or-`.git`, never `$HOME`/fs-root, one root per graph) and audited as `repoint` (sidecar rows carry basenames only). `mnemos_list_graph_projects` rows gain `root_missing` so ghosts are visible at a glance. Registration: the new `mnemos_register_project` MCP tool (CLI twin `vesma graph register`; tool-count pins 38 → 39) lets an agent fix «project not registered» refusals itself — same confinement gates, idempotent on the same root, attaches a root to existing pathless projects, refuses name collisions with a different root; explicit registration does not count against `auto_register_max_projects`, and unregistered-project refusals now name the registration path.
- **`code_graph.secret_allowlist` — an escape hatch for known-fake secret fixtures (#449)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/project-graph.md`, `config.example.yaml`) — repo-relative fnmatch globs; a matching file skips PG3 poison-marking at index time (still indexed normally — real parse verdict, no poisoned-node metadata), and a previously poisoned path that now matches is removed from the poisoned set on the next index run — every index call, publish or fresh, so a config-only change takes effect without waiting for a file change (reported in `IndexResult.unpoisoned`, audited `allowlist-unpoison`). The PG4 issuance scan is NEVER waived; default `[]` keeps PG3 byte-identical.
- **`redacted_patterns` on every aggregate awareness surface (#456)** — every issuance surface that reports a `redactions` count now also names WHICH detector patterns fired (per-pattern counts — names only, never values, never matched spans). The per-item channels already carried the field; the gap was the aggregates: `hooks.on_session_start` top-level totals, `awareness.project_delta` counts, `operational_picture` counts (goal-title leg included) and `compose_pre_llm_awareness` meta (`pre_flight` inherits via the shared dicts). Shape policy unchanged and pinned: the field is ABSENT on a clean issuance, never an empty dict.
- **One-time «vesma server updated: X → Y» notice (#456)** (`src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`) — a mid-session server upgrade was invisible to the sessions it serves; the dispatcher (the single point every tool call passes) now checks, once per process, the store meta `last_reported_server_version` against the running `__version__` and appends one non-blocking line to that response — exactly one notice per upgrade, never per call. A missing meta is a silent first-contact baseline; store errors skip the notice after one warning log (fail-open).

### Changed

- **Deploy manifests ship the canonical image** — compose, docker-compose, helm chart (values + ArtifactHub annotations), podman kube/quadlet examples, READMEs and `install.sh --container` now pull `ghcr.io/vesmaro/vesma:5.1.2` instead of the legacy `ghcr.io/vesmaro/vesmaro:4.3.0` pin (phase-g flip completed; the canonical image has been public since 2026-10-01). `install.sh` also detects the latest version from the canonical `vesma-memory-server` PyPI JSON and prints the canonical product name — container mode was effectively broken for any post-rebrand version before this. The `VESMARO_IMAGE_TAG` env name is kept for compatibility; the 4.3.0 legacy image remains published for historical pins.
- **Rebrand finished: the last user-visible `mnemos-*` pack artifacts renamed (loud rename per ADR-0033 / board card `vesma-naming-debt-rename`) + #448 P3 verify scoping** (`integrations/skills/mnemos-canon-write.md` → `vesma-canon-write.md`; `integrations/skills/mnemos-context-lifecycle.md` → `vesma-context-lifecycle.md`; `integrations/prompts/mnemos-memory.prompt.md` → `vesma-memory.prompt.md`, frontmatter `mode:` → `vesma-memory`; `integrations/extensions/mnemos-mcp.ts` → `vesma-mcp.ts`; `integrations/instructions/canon-records.instructions.md` → `vesma-canon-records.instructions.md`) — the #426/#431 record-canon artefacts that had kept their pinned pre-rebrand names now carry the `vesma-*` brand like the rest of the pack; zero old filenames remain under `integrations/`, while `mnemos_*` TOOL names and `mnemos:<subtype>` tag prefixes stay (server/storage contracts). Cross-references inside the renamed files retargeted to current pack siblings (`vesma-write`, `vesma-tag-contract`, `vesma-checkpoint`, `vesma-memory-ops.instructions.md` — two of the old pointers named files already removed in 5.1.2). Registry + docs aligned: the pi MCP config path in `targets.yaml` and the `package.json` pi extensions export now point at `vesma-mcp.ts`, the inert hermes plugin deploy path follows the docs (`~/.hermes/plugins/vesma/`), `docs/en|ru/user/integration-guide.md` pack trees updated, `integrations/mcp-presets.md` renamed-file paths fixed (and the documented bridge env var corrected to `MNEMOS_BIN` — the bridge never read `VESMARO_BIN`). Pi bridge content: default server binary is now `vesma` (was `mnemos`; the `MNEMOS_BIN` override is unchanged), a brand-primary `/vesma` reconnect command is registered alongside the legacy `/mnemos` alias (docs already promised `/vesma`), the standing-hint heading is rebranded, and the duplicate-hint check recognizes both marker generations. Pin tests retargeted to the new names with freeze notes pointing at this wave (`TestSkillPack` glob, `TestCanonPack`, `TestPiTarget`). Fixed (#448 P3): `verify`/`update` now scope extra-file scans and orphan removal to the target's WHOLE deploy map (`IntegrationManager._target_expected_dests`) — hermes maps both `instructions` and `skills` into the same `~/.hermes/skills/` directory, and the old per-kind scan flagged the sibling kind's freshly deployed files as stale orphans; worse, `update` deleted the entire shared directory right after deploy had re-written it (on a deployed hermes home `update` went 20 files → 0; now 20 → 20; a real-pack hermes roundtrip test pins the fix). CLI hint strings swept to `vesma integration *` (verify old-stamp hint, module docstrings). `tests/test_integration.py` 204 → 209 passed (+1 skipped unchanged); `tests/test_mcp_presets.py` drift guard green.
- **Repo-wide `ruff format` sweep (#462/#466)** — 4 drifted files reformatted (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/indexer.py`, `src/vesmaro/decision_jev.py`, `tests/test_decision_cortex.py`, `tests/test_proposals.py` — pure `ruff format` 0.15.x with the repo config, zero semantic changes) so the `ruff format --check .` gate is green again — it had been failing on main; all 356 files now pass the check.

### Fixed

- **#460 — `vesma update` UX: interactive confirm by default, `-y` alias, quiet pip, stale-Latest drift after a self-upgrade** (`src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py`, `src/vesmaro/updates.py`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`; tests `tests/test_updates.py` +14) — four defects from the owner's field test of 5.1.2. (1) Plain `vesma update` now shows the surfaces report and — in an interactive terminal, when a pip update is pending — asks `Apply update? [y/N]` and applies on yes; pipes/CI (non-TTY) keep the check-only behavior and print `apply with: vesma update --yes`; `--check` never applies or prompts. (2) `-y` is a full alias of `--yes` (both skip the prompt). (3) The pip firehose is gone: pip output is always captured — one summary line per surface (`pip: vesma-memory-server 5.1.1 → 5.1.2` or `already current`, detected by re-reading the installed version after the run); on failure the last ~15 captured lines are printed with a `re-run with --verbose` hint; the new `--verbose` flag restores the full pip output as before. (4) A fresh positive cache whose `latest` is OLDER than the installed version (written before a self-upgrade landed) is treated as stale and re-checked synchronously once (3s-capped; on failure the previous answer is served with `stale=True`); a fresh negative cache keeps its bounded no-refetch behavior; and when the installed version is still newer than the published latest, the report says `newer than published latest (local build?)` instead of `up to date`.
- **#451 — Unicode conflict-hint tokenizer (Cyrillic works)** (`src/vesmaro/awareness.py`; docs mcp-tools EN/RU) — the awareness goal tokenizer was ASCII-only (`[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_.\-]+`), so Cyrillic goals produced an empty token set and conflict hints could never fire for RU users. `_TOKEN_RE` is now Unicode `\w` with dotted tails kept in-token (`v4.0.0` stays ONE token) and hyphens as separators (`qa-vesma-5x` → `qa`/`vesma`/`5x`); a minimal RU stopword set (и в не на с по для из у к о как что это) rides the EN set, so shared service words alone never manufacture a conflict. A Cyrillic goal now tokenizes like a Latin one.
- **#452 — pre_flight picture dedup + graph-coverage `missing` verdict** (`src/vesmaro/awareness.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`; docs mcp-tools/http-api EN/RU) — (a) `mnemos_awareness` pre_flight carried the SAME operational picture 3× (top-level dict, presence dict, rendered text); the presence snapshot is now composed with `include_picture=False` — ONE canonical top-level picture (the `on_session_start` hook keeps its nested copy, its only channel). (b) `mnemos_check_graph_coverage` answered `unindexed` for paths that do not exist on disk; a distinct `missing` verdict (resolved against the project root) now separates ghosts from real-but-unindexed files — «clean ≠ proof» stays visible. (c) stale wrong-fact claims corrected: the `watch_start` docstring and the `GraphDisabledError` text said «default OFF» — both default ON (watch in config.py, code_graph per the 2026-09-28 owner decision); the texts now say so.
- **#455 — bare-slug task-search fallback** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/storage/`; docs mcp-tools EN/RU) — the tags filter is exact-membership, so `tags=["my-task"]` never matched rows tagged `task:my-task`: a developer typing the bare slug got a silent empty page. On an empty exact result, when a bare entry fits the task-slug alphabet AND the store tag census (cached) shows `task:<slug>` rows exist, the search retries ONCE with the exact minted tag — every other scope kept (runs BEFORE the project soft-fallback so a found in-scope row never widens across projects); results carry `task_tag_fallback` (mirroring `project_scope_fallback`) and `search_stats()` counts it. No behavior change for exact-tag queries; no `task:<slug>` rows → an honest empty page, no marker.

## [5.1.2] — 2026-10-02

### Added

- **Memory-switch protocol MS-0: engine manifest, formalized precedence, `vesma memory status` (ADR-0034, board card `vesma-memory-switch-protocol`)** (`integrations/engine-manifest.yaml` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/memory_status.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/util.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`, `integrations/targets.yaml`; docs EN+RU; tests in `tests/test_integration.py`) — the engine-side identity card for the memory-switch protocol ships as a PLAIN pack file `integrations/engine-manifest.yaml` (generalized from the Hermes `plugin.yaml`: name/description/author/license/capabilities, `mcp` transport block — stdio, brand-primary server key `vesma` with legacy `mnemos` recognized, `precedence_modes: [overlay+mirror]`, `attach_points` pointing at the targets registry); it rides the wheel via the existing `integrations/` force-include and is deliberately NOT deployed into harness directories — consumers load it via `load_engine_manifest()`, which validates required keys, rejects unknown precedence modes (fail-closed) and injects `version` from the installed package (single source of truth — the YAML never drifts). The target-profile layer gains an explicit `precedence:` field per target (default `overlay+mirror` — the only ENFORCED mode; `replace`/`off` are recognized-but-not-yet-enforced values documented as arriving with the spec-repo MS-1 wave, honest staging per the contract; any other value fails at parse time with `validate_precedence`). `integration verify` and `doctor` now show the active precedence per wired harness. New read-only CLI surface `vesma memory status` (`cli/memory_status.py`, registered as the `memory` sub-app): per detected harness — pack attachment state (stamps via the verify engine), MCP registration (vesma entry present or not), external memory engines seen in that harness's MCP config, built-in store markers (data dir, vault dir, `mnemos.db` — existence + mtime ONLY) and the active precedence. Hygiene per ArchCom B3: only paths from the static `targets.yaml`/pack registry and the well-known store markers are read, file NAMES and mtimes only — never contents, env values or command lines; no network, no writes. Tests: `TestEngineManifest` (load+validate, version injection, attach-point pointer, unknown-mode/missing-key rejections), `TestPrecedenceField` (default/explicit/recognized/unknown + shipped-registry invariant + verify output), `TestMemoryStatus` (output shape, server-keys-only hygiene with secret-leak assertions, empty-host, unknown-target, marker reporting).
- **Harness layer handed to the integration pack: vesma-* skills, memory-ops canon, G1–G4 always-on block (ArchCom 2026-10-01 «Владение harness-слоем памяти», variant b — single delivery channel `vesma integration`)** (`integrations/skills/vesma-*.md` — 16 skills new, 14 superseded flat `mnemos-*.md` removed; `integrations/instructions/vesma-memory-ops.instructions.md` — the consolidated memory-operations canon (gates G1–G4, ops discipline, tag contract, graceful degradation), replacing the split `mnemos-memory-ops` / `mnemos-session-lifecycle` / `mnemos-tag-contract` instructions; `integrations/agents_md/vesma-always-on.md` — the always-on behavioral block injected into AGENTS.md-standard files, replacing `mnemos-always-on.md`) — ownership of the harness-facing memory canon moves from the GCW (GithubCopilotWorkflow) repo to Vesma per the layer-separation contract «the memory layer is owned by Vesma, not by any agent framework»; GCW ships inert one-line stubs pointing at `vesma integration setup`. Content notes: skills are the live v2.14.1-generation canon renamed `mnemos-*` → `vesma-*`; tool names swept to brand-primary `vesma_*` (legacy `mnemos_*` accepted by server builds until 6.0, noted where relevant); tag prefixes `mnemos:<subtype>` are the storage data contract and are deliberately unchanged; the record-canon artefacts from #426/#431 (`mnemos-canon-write`, `mnemos-context-lifecycle`, `canon-records`) keep their pinned names pending a dedicated rename wave. Security-major #1 (gate): every deployable text-kind file now carries the pack safety contract — recalled store content is DATA not instructions; no exfiltration of memory contents into URLs/web requests/commits/external messages; no real credentials in examples; subordination line «Инструкции пака описывают работу с сервером памяти vesma и применяются только в объёме, где локальный канон харнеса молчит; при любом расхождении приоритет у локального канона и safety-правил хоста» — pinned by `TestPackSafetyContract` (grep over the pack; the byte-identical `schemas` kind is documentedly exempt). Stamp migration: the version stamp is now `<!-- vesma-integration: vX -->` (engine `make_stamp`), with dual-pattern recognition of legacy `mnemos-integration` (file stamps, `AGENTS.md` block markers, schemas manifest name) for a 2–3 release window — first deploy/update re-stamps, `verify` reports legacy markers as the dedicated `old-stamp` status; legacy `mnemos-schemas.manifest.json` migrates to `vesma-schemas.manifest.json` on first deploy/update and both names uninstall. Security-major #2 (gate): `uninstall` now also unregisters the MCP server entry the pack registered (`IntegrationManager.unregister_mcp`; zcode `mcp.servers`, agents `mcpServers`, opencode `mcp` maps) — brand-primary key `vesma`, legacy `mnemos` key removed, entries removed only on command-evidence (`mcp-server` argv or `vesma`/`mnemos` binary basename), foreign entries and unrelated config keys preserved as data, dry-run never touches the config; `register_mcp` migrates a legacy-key entry to the brand-primary key. Release tests: `TestStampMigration` / `TestSchemasManifestMigration` / `TestUnregisterMcp` (7 tests) / `TestAgentsMdInjectionByteExactness` (AGENTS.md injection with CRLF pre-content changes not one byte outside the stamped block, update re-splices in place, uninstall restores the exact user bytes). `tests/test_integration.py` 154 → 176 green, `tests/test_integration_agents_md.py` pins updated; docs `docs/en|ru/user/integration-guide.md` pack trees rewritten.

- **Auto-update family: quiet update check (default-on) + `vesma update` + contrib weekly user-timer (#445)** (`src/vesmaro/updates.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/update_cmd.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`, `contrib/vesma-update.service` / `contrib/vesma-update.timer` — new; docs: `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md`, `config.example.yaml`; tests: `tests/test_updates.py` — 41) — Vesma now answers "is there a newer release?" and updates itself: a quiet stdlib-only check (`pypi.org/pypi/<dist>/json`, 3s timeout, no telemetry, 24h sidecar cache `<data_dir>/update-check.json`, dist detected across the post-rebrand family with `vesma-memory-server` → `vesma` first-hit-wins) surfaces as the `update_available` object in `mnemos_stats`, a stderr hint on `vesma --version`, and one INFO line at `serve`/`mcp-server` start; offline machines are unaffected (stale answers served, 1h negative cache bounds the timeout cost, the check never raises); opt-out via `updates.check_enabled` (env `VESMARO_UPDATES__CHECK_ENABLED`) or the independent hard kill switch `VESMARO_UPDATES_CHECK=off`. `vesma update` without flags reports every update surface on the machine (pip dists, npm `@vesmaro/vesma`, host prod-venvs, Go binaries); `--yes --scope=user` upgrades the installed dist via `pip install --user --upgrade` (PEP 668 `--break-system-packages` only under an externally-managed interpreter), updates npm best-effort, and appends to `~/.local/share/vesma/update-history.json`; `--to <version>` is the rollback path; `--install-timer`/`--uninstall-timer` manage a weekly (`OnCalendar=weekly`, `Persistent=true`, `RandomizedDelaySec=1h`) systemd USER timer. Prod venvs, Go binaries and containers are NEVER auto-updated (report-only by design, documented in the unit headers and docs).

### Changed

- **`vesma integration setup` is now the full host deployment by default (UX inversion, owner ruling; board card `vesma-integration-setup-default-all`)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/util.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/doctor.py`, `integrations/targets.yaml`; docs `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/integration-guide.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/cli-reference.md`) — the plain `vesma integration setup` deploys the pack to **ALL detected harnesses on this host AND wires all agents** in ONE non-interactive, idempotent pass; the interactive wiring prompts are gone (the previous "prompt when TTY / skip in CI" default is retired). Flags are now the narrowing/custom variant: `--target <name>` is repeatable and deploys only the named harness(es) (`all` still accepted), `--no-wire-agents` skips wiring, `--select a,b` narrows wiring to named agents (works standalone); `--wire-agents` / `--all` remain accepted for backward compatibility (docs and scripts reference them) but are no-ops relative to the default — wiring is already default-on. `--dry-run`, `--precise`, `--home`, `--no-mcp` unchanged. Doctor hints and the verify wiring hint updated to the plain command. A failing target is now reported loudly per target and never blocks the remaining targets (see the #448 fix below).

- **Ecosystem rebrand completed** — all sibling repositories moved to `vesma-*` names in the `vesmaro` org (`vesmaro-agent` / `vesmaro-cortex` / `vesmaro-canon` → `vesma-agent` / `vesma-cortex` / `vesma-canon`; `mnemos-eyes` → `vesma-eyes`; `mnemos-mesh` → `vesma-mesh`; `mnemos-vitals` → `vesma-vitals`); models `mnema-*` → `vesma-*` hash-preserving; GHCR packages `vesmaro/vesma` and `vesmaro/vesma-eyes` live. Documentation updated to the new names.

- **Brand-primary MCP manifest** — with `VESMA_MCP_BRAND` set (e.g. `vesma`), `tools/list` now advertises each tool under its `vesma_*` name ONLY instead of appending brand aliases to the `mnemos_*` manifest (owner ruling 2026-10-01: the doubled 76-entry list confused clients). Legacy `mnemos_*` spellings leave the manifest but remain accepted on the call path and dispatch identically (dual-period contract until 6.0, ADR-0031).

### Fixed

- **#448 (point 1): multi-target one-pass deployment** (`src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/util.py`; regression tests `TestIssue448MultiTargetOnePass` in `tests/test_integration.py`) — on the first real host deployment of the pack, `integration setup` stopped after the FIRST target: a single unreadable destination file (non-UTF-8 bytes at a pack destination path — e.g. a user file that happens to carry a pack file's name) raised `UnicodeDecodeError` from `_deploy_file`'s unguarded `dest.read_text`, which propagated through the CLI target loop and killed the whole run, so every target AFTER the failing one silently never deployed. Root cause in one sentence: the deploy loop had no per-target failure isolation and no guard on the destination read. `_deploy_file`/`_verify_file` now treat an unreadable destination as the refuse-to-touch discipline already used by the `agents_md` kind (reported SKIPPED, file untouched, run continues), and the CLI `setup` loop isolates per-target exceptions — the failing target gets a loud `✗ Target <name>: …` line and a non-zero exit at the end, while the remaining targets still deploy. Regression tests: a poisoned second-target destination leaves the file untouched and still deploys targets one and three in the same pass; an injected crashing target exits 1 with the loud line while later targets deploy.

- **Version detection for pip installs** — `vesma --version` (and every consumer of `vesmaro.__version__`: CLI banner, `stats`, doctor) now resolves the installed distribution across the full post-rebrand family (`vesma` → `vesma-memory-server` → `vesmaro` → `mnemos-memory-server`). A clean `pip install vesma-memory-server` previously reported `mnemos 0.0.0+unknown` because the lookup chain missed the standalone server dist name. The `-V` banner now prints the canonical `vesma` brand (was `mnemos`); `tests/test_version.py` guard updated to the same chain.


## [5.1.1] — 2026-10-01 — the native auto-indexing ships for real

> **Shipping-integrity note.** The 5.1.0 artifacts were cut from a tree
> that did NOT contain the PG-0.5 code below (the wave landed on a branch
> the release merge never touched) — the 5.1.0 CHANGELOG advertised it in
> error. 5.1.1 ships the code; nothing else changed.

- **PG-0.5 — native auto-indexing of the project graph (ADR-0032 wave PG-0.5, owner directive 2026-09-29: indexation happens by itself — no explicit agent/harness call, no instruction, no skill)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/autoindex.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/watch.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/audit.py`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/hooks.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`; docs: `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/http-api.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, ADR-0032 status update) — every dispatched MCP tool call (ONE cut-in point at the top of `_dispatch`) and every `pre_llm_call` hook emits a non-blocking activity hint (`MemoryManager.codegraph_activity_hint`; bounded queue, hint never raises and never blocks the caller). The `AutoIndexer` consumer rides the GENERALIZED single scheduler thread (`GraphWatchScheduler.submit` one-shot jobs — the same cooperative thread now hosts the watch poll AND auto-index drains; never thread-per-hint) and per hint: marker-gated auto-registration (a project absent from the projects table whose cwd carries `.git` / `pyproject.toml` / `package.json` / `go.mod` / `Cargo.toml` gets registered with that root — attribution in the description, new audit action `auto-register`), then a background first index (audit reason `auto-first`) when the graph is empty or an incremental reindex (reason `auto-stale`) when the cheap mtime+size classification finds actual changes. Guardrails: per-project throttle `code_graph.auto_reindex_min_interval_sec` (default 300s, sidecar `last_auto_action` stamp, reserve-then-act so a failing auto index is not retried on every hint); PG7 attribution — the hinting agent is the actor, no agent means no auto action; PG7 fail-closed limits hold identically on the auto path (a breach aborts the whole index, audited, nothing partial); v1 limitation — a multi-path registration indexes `paths[0]` only. REST is deliberately NOT an auto surface (no cwd to gate a registration on); the manual tools (`mnemos_index_project`, `watch_start`) are unchanged; `code_graph.auto_index: false` (env `VESMARO_CODE_GRAPH__AUTO_INDEX=false`) turns the auto path off entirely. Tests: `tests/test_codegraph_autoindex.py` — 10 native e2e tests at the manager+dispatcher+hook level (first-contact register+index+beacon, MCP-dispatcher and pre_llm_call-hook cut-ins, stale reindex after the throttle window, marker-less cwd ignored, flag-off no-op, two-rapid-hints→one-task throttle, hint-never-joins scheduler + drain coalescing, limit-breach fail-closed with the audit cause). Fix-slice (PR #443 review, two P2 holds, 2026-09-29): **P2-1 flood of auto-registrations** — auto-registration now searches the existing projects for one whose `paths` contain the resolved root FIRST (`CodeGraphService.find_project_by_root`, the one-method scan; normpath-normalized on both sides); a hit means the hint's graph operations run under the EXISTING project id/name (audit `auto-register-reused`) — no duplicate `projects` row, no second full indexation of the same tree — and a global cap `code_graph.auto_register_max_projects` (default 64, counted through the `auto-registered by` description marker; env `VESMARO_CODE_GRAPH__AUTO_REGISTER_MAX_PROJECTS`) makes any further auto-registration a SILENT skip with an `auto-register-capped` audit row, never an error to the caller (root-reuse and operator registrations never count). **P2-2 server-cwd hazard** — the marker gate now requires a packaging MANIFEST (`pyproject.toml` / `setup.py` / `package.json` / `go.mod` / `Cargo.toml`; lockfiles do not count): a bare `.git` no longer triggers the auto path (the dotfiles-`$HOME` case), and `$HOME`/the filesystem root are refused even with a manifest present; plus a failed FIRST auto index (e.g. an `IndexLimitError`) writes the sidecar stamp `auto_suspended:{len}:{project}=1` — further hints skip the tree with NO disk walk until a successful manual `index_project` publish (the watch poll rides the same method and clears it too) or a `delete_graph_project` lifts the flag (an `auto-stale` run over a valid index never suspends). Tests: +5 (two names on one marker-root → one row/one graph/`auto-register-reused`; `.git`-only directory ignored; `$HOME`-mock cwd refused; cap=1 → second root capped-audited while root-reuse passes; limit-failed first → `auto_suspended` proven by a walk-count spy until a manual index lifts it).
## [5.1.0] — 2026-09-30

### Added


- **vesmaro-canon v1.0.0 enforcement — server-minted checkpoint envelope + always-five-sections render (#416)** (`src/vesmaro/models.py`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`, `src/vesmaro/awareness.py`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/api/main.py`, `tests/test_checkpoint_canon_envelope.py`) — every checkpoint now mints the ratified canon envelope `metadata.canon` (`schema_version`/`type`/`status`/`language`/`session_ref`; the `canon` key joined `CHECKPOINT_STAMP_KEYS`, so client-forged copies are stripped on every generic path), and the render ALWAYS emits the five EN sections in `CHECKPOINT_FIELDS` order — an empty field gets a deterministic per-language placeholder line (ArchCom option A: no more skipped sections). Section H2 titles come from an explicit map pinned by a drift test to the canon schemas' `x-canon-sections` (never `str.title()`). New `language` parameter (`ru`/`en`, no heuristics): config `mnemos.checkpoint_language` (default `ru`, env `VESMARO_MNEMOS__CHECKPOINT_LANGUAGE`) with a per-call override. Dedup hash inputs untouched.
- **Write-path canon validator, warn by default (#418)** (`src/vesmaro/canon_validate.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/sdk.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`, `tests/test_canon_warn_validator.py`) — records carrying `metadata.canon` are validated at the write path against the ratified canon: six stable machine-parseable codes (`CANON-E-ENVELOPE/-SECTION/-STATUS/-TITLE/-LANGUAGE/-DATE`) logged as one `canon_violation:` line per violation AND attached as `metadata.canon_warnings`; the write NEVER blocks in warn mode. New config `mnemos.canon_mode` (`off|warn|strict`, default `warn`): strict rejects CREATE-path violations only (`CanonViolationError`) — update paths stay warn-only even in strict, and records WITHOUT an envelope are out of canon scope in both modes (legacy never fails retroactively, canon §10).
- **Dedup guards + envelope-aware checkpoint reads (#419)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/awareness.py`, `tests/test_checkpoint_canon_envelope.py`) — a dedup hit returns the existing record without the canon gate; the trivial-reject (all five fields empty) stays BEFORE the render; the dedup hash is regression-pinned to `[project, agent, *CHECKPOINT_FIELDS]` (render/config changes cannot move it). `checkpoint_goal_title` and the awareness summaries read the envelope when present (frozen section-title map, per-language placeholder from the declared language) and keep the first-line heuristic for pre-canon rows.
- **Canon integration pack: instructions + skill + vendored schemas kind (#426, #431)** (`integrations/instructions/canon-records.instructions.md` — new, `integrations/skills/mnemos-canon-write.md` — new, `integrations/schemas/` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `integrations/targets.yaml`, `tests/test_integration.py`) — any harness connected to the memory server now receives the ratified record standards natively: the `canon-records` instruction and `mnemos-canon-write` skill ship in the integration pack (stamped deploy/verify/uninstall round-trip tested), and a new `schemas` artefact kind deploys the five canon JSON Schemas byte-identical to pin tag `canon-v1.0.0` with a sidecar manifest (version/provenance/sha256) — deployed JSON stays valid for external validators. Uninstall ownership = inline stamp or pack byte-identity (a co-located manifest is never proof); deploy verifies pack bytes against the pin and fails loud on divergence.
- **Decision-provider seam + deterministic baseline (ADR-0004, #439)** (`src/vesmaro/decision_provider.py` — new, `tests/test_decision_provider.py` — new, `tests/data/w4b_corpus_snapshot.json`) — the ratified decision-provider interface as code: typed primitives `Choice`/`Score`/`Noul`, a `CanonRecordView` prepared-state boundary (envelope + body + title only; `embedding_text()` byte-mirrors the engine's embedding composition), typed refusals instead of fabrication, and `DeterministicProvider` formalizing the existing heuristics as-is (quality placeholder 0.5/0.5, is-duplicate 0.92 threshold, goal-overlap via the awareness public signal). ZERO production call sites — this is the calibrated seam future providers (local mnema router, the optional external Jev adapter) plug into; the acceptance baseline (21-record corpus + pre-registered rule) lives in vesmaro-canon `docs/experiments/provider-calibration.md`.

- **W3 part 3 — agent data gate on the core leg: per-request token re-validation + effective-scope narrowing on `ListMemories`/`ReadMemory`/`WriteMemory` (ADR-0018 variant (c), ADR-0018-T §3 criterion 5, T3/T4/T7)** (`src/vesparo/mesh_server.py`, `src/vesparo/agent_tokens.py`, `tests/test_mesh_server.py`) — the non-deployable gap this closes: the Go AgentGateway (mnemos-mesh `feat/w3-gateway-leg`) validates the bearer via `ValidateAgentToken` and then translates agent RPCs to core-leg data calls stamped `authorization: Bearer <token>` (end-to-end) + `x-mnemos-agent-id: <verdict.agent_id>` + `x-mnemos-peer-id: <node>` — but core did not interpret those metadata on the data path, so a compromised mesh node could skip the gate by dialing the data RPCs directly. ADDITIVE presence-switch: a request WITHOUT `x-mnemos-agent-id` takes the unchanged federation path (UDS 0700/0600 / mesh-CA mTLS trust, exactly as before); WITH the key, `MnemosCoreServicer._agent_data_gate` re-validates the bearer per request (defense-in-depth, TM §3: signature, version, lifetime, `aud` node binding — checked against the CALLING node's `x-mnemos-peer-id`, the same value the gateway binds at issuance (single-hop topology, T1) — jti denylist/grace) via the existing `validate_agent_token`; identity derives ONLY from the verdict (`AgentIdentity`), and a claimed `x-mnemos-agent-id` that does not equal the verdict's `sub` is refused (spoof, T3/T4). Fail-closed mapping: missing/non-Bearer authorization or any token rejection → `UNAUTHENTICATED` (coarse reason category in details, never token values); identity mismatch → `PERMISSION_DENIED`; store/key init failure → `INTERNAL`. Effective scope = token scope ∩ transport-peer ACL (TM §3), resolved only in mnemos: the transport-peer ACL is the EXISTING fail-closed `PeerConfig.allowed_projects` gate (ADR-0016 per-peer model — no new ACL engine; the agent rides the same core leg, so it can never exceed what that leg permits), and the token's `project:<slug>` grants only NARROW it (`_narrow_by_agent_scope` at each RPC's existing ACL checkpoint; new `token_project_grants()` helper in `agent_tokens.py` — the scope grammar's home; `scope_allows_write`/`token_project_grants` widened to `Sequence[str]`, behaviour unchanged). Class grant per method: `WriteMemory` requires `rw` (a `read` token is `PERMISSION_DENIED` regardless of the peer ACL; narrowing flows through `import_compact_record(agent_scope=…)` so the S2 lazy-fetch leg is untouched); List/Read stay open to `read`/`rw` under the SAME project filtering as the plain path — no filter bypass. T7 logging: `agent_id`/`jti`/coarse reason (+ a dedicated line when token grants narrow the effective set to empty) — never token values, never record content. Tests: +13 over the real Unix-socket server (no-agent-metadata legacy path unchanged; read token lists+reads but write-refused with nothing persisted; rw token writes `EXHAUSTIVE`+persisted; garbage / expired / revoked / cross-node `aud` / missing bearer / Basic scheme → `UNAUTHENTICATED`; spoofed agent id → `PERMISSION_DENIED`; and the TM §3 narrowing matrix on a two-project fixture where the peer alone allows both — `project:` grants narrow an unscoped list to the granted project, deny a non-granted scoped list, and refuse a write into a non-granted project). No proto change (metadata-only contract, as ADR-0018-T §2 specifies). The cross-repo W3 part 3 E2E (Go gateway ↔ this gate) remains open.

- **W3 part 2 enabler — additive `MnemosCore.ValidateAgentToken` RPC: the core-leg token-validation hook (ADR-0018-T §3 + §8 launch condition 2)** (`federation/proto/mnemos_core_api.proto`, `src/vesmaro/mesh_server.py`, `tests/test_mesh_server.py`) — part 1 exported `validate_agent_token` as an in-process function only; the Go mesh's AgentGateway (part 2) needs to reach it over the core contract on EVERY agent request, so the RPC this PR adds is the canonical way the mesh asks mnemos "is this bearer token valid for my node". Wire shape (additive, no renumber): `ValidateAgentTokenRequest{gateway_node_id=1}` → `ValidateAgentTokenResponse{valid=1, agent_id=2, scope=3, jti=4, aud=5, exp_ok=6, revoked=7, reason=8}` — a faithful mirror of `AgentTokenVerdict` so the gateway can rate-limit per agent and log a precise reject reason to its `gateway_query` category WITHOUT becoming an ACL authority (criterion 5: scope→ACL resolution stays mnemos-side per data RPC). TOKEN PLACEMENT (TM §2 end-to-end rule): the token rides in gRPC `authorization` metadata (`Bearer <token>`), NEVER in the request message — a message field could reach logs (T7); the core leg is already authenticated (UDS isolation / mesh-CA mTLS), so only the mesh can reach this RPC. Servicer `MnemosCoreServicer.ValidateAgentToken` is a thin fail-closed wrapper over `validate_agent_token`: missing/empty `authorization`, non-Bearer scheme, or empty `gateway_node_id` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (a malformed RPC, not a token verdict); a store/key initialisation failure → `INTERNAL` (never a fabricated verdict, never a crash of the serving thread); anything else → the honest per-check verdict. Store + signing key initialise LAZILY on first validated RPC under a double-checked lock (`_token_validation_deps`) — a mnemos host that never serves agent RPCs never mints a key and never opens the registry ("first use" semantics, TM §3); the key path and the shared-DB `agent_tokens` registry are the same ones the part-1 CLI uses. Tests: +11 over the real Unix-socket server (full-verdict propagation for a valid rw token, read-scope propagation, revoked → `revoked`/`revoked=True`, `aud` cross-node mismatch, expired (`iat`-shifted mint), signed-but-unregistered jti → `unknown_jti` fail-closed, garbage → `malformed`/`bad_signature` without crash, missing metadata / non-Bearer scheme / empty node id → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, first-use key minted 0600). `buf breaking` still not runnable locally (buf absent) — the delta is service/RPC/message-additive only, no renumber, green by construction. The W3 part-2 consumer (mesh AgentGateway listener + per-agent token bucket + `gateway_query` retention) lands in mnemos-mesh; part 3 (cross-repo E2E) remains open.

- **W3-v1 AgentGateway contract + agent-token infrastructure, part 1 (ADR-0018 variant (c), ADR-0018-T threat model, W3 scope per amendment 3)** (`federation/proto/agent_gateway.proto` — new, `federation/proto/mnemos_core_api.proto`, `scripts/gen-proto.sh`, `src/vesmaro/agent_tokens.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/agent_token_cmd.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/cli/main.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`, `src/vesmaro/mesh_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/_mesh_gen.py`, `tests/test_agent_tokens.py` — new, `tests/test_mesh_server.py`) — the canonical proto bytes for the dedicated agent leg (TM §2 variant (ii)): new `mnemos.gateway.v1.AgentGateway` service in a THIRD proto file (one file per leg — `federation.proto` peer leg, `mnemos_core_api.proto` core leg, `agent_gateway.proto` agent leg; Agent-prefixed message names because all three files share Go package `mnemosmesh` and would collide otherwise), RPC set strictly per the TM W3-v1 scope `ListMemories + ReadMemory + WriteMemory class`: `AgentListMemories` (`AgentListMemoriesRequest{projects=1, limit=2, cursor=3}` → `AgentListMemoriesResponse{records=1, has_more=2, cursor=3, trigger_code=4}` — deliberately narrower than the core list: no `include_no_federate`, no `since`, NO free-text query — Search is a later additive phase per amendment 3, so the sketch-name `AgentSearch` was not used), `AgentReadMemory` (`AgentReadRequest{record_id=1, project=2}` → `AgentReadResponse{record=1, trigger_code=2}`), `AgentWriteMemory` (reuses `mnemos.core.v1.WriteMemoryRequest/Response` verbatim — relay-without-remarshalling, the `SyncMetadata` pattern); the token travels in gRPC `authorization` metadata END-TO-END and never in message fields (file-header rule, TM §2/§3); core-leg delta: additive `MnemosCore.ReadMemory` (`ReadMemoryRequest{record_id=1, project=2}` → `ReadMemoryResponse{record=1, trigger_code=2}`) — the TM names ReadMemory as "not yet in mnemos_core_api.proto", and the mesh cannot forward a by-id agent read without it. Core implementation `MnemosCoreServicer.ReadMemory` mirrors the ListMemories gate matrix on a single record: peer identity + TLS pin, `fed:<agent>:<memory.id>` tail-parse with raw-id fallback, unknown → `NOT_FOUND`, `mnemos:no-federate` → `NOT_FOUND` (no id oracle), untagged / denied-project / mismatched narrowing → `PERMISSION_DENIED`, moderation refuse → `OK` + `trigger_code=REFUSED`, `revision` stamped 0 (= "no revision info", documented proto default — the by-id path does not walk the rowid list). Token scheme (TM §3) in `agent_tokens.py`: custom three-segment envelope `base64url(header).base64url(payload).base64url(Ed25519-sig)`, claims `{v, iss, sub, aud, scope, iat, exp, jti}`; **Ed25519 over HMAC** because `cryptography>=50` is already a direct dep and asymmetric signing keeps "the signing key never leaves mnemos" true for any future external verifier (HMAC would need the shared secret shipped to every verifier); key = PKCS8 PEM under `<data_dir>/agent-token-signing.key` (override `federation.agent_token_key_path`), minted on first use via `O_EXCL`+`fsync` at 0600, reload-stable; TTL mandatory max 24 h default 8 h; `aud` node binding enforced per request; denylist by `jti` and by `agent_id` (revoke-all), checked per request; rotate honours the old `jti` only for the grace window (`grace_until`, default 5 min) then fails closed; unknown-`jti` fails closed (minting record authoritative). Storage: additive `agent_tokens` table (jti PK, agent_id, aud, scope, iat, exp, revoked_at, grace_until) — plaintexts NEVER stored, only issuance metadata (same posture as `auth_tokens`); `AgentTokenStore` mirrors the AuthStore concurrency contract (RLock + WAL + `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`). Issuance UX (TM §5 launch condition): `vesmaro agent-token issue --agent <id> --node <gw-node> [--scope read|rw|project:<slug>,…] [--ttl-hours N≤24]` (token printed ONCE, `soft_wrap` so the ~330-char envelope survives copy-paste), `rotate --agent [--grace-minutes 5]` (inherits scope/aud/lifetime of the latest live token), `revoke --agent | --jti`, `list` (metadata + live/grace/expired/revoked status; no token values exist to leak). `validate_agent_token(token, gateway_node_id, …) → AgentTokenVerdict{valid, agent_id, scope, jti, aud, exp_ok, revoked, reason}` exported + tested for the W3 part-2 gateway wiring (launch condition TM §8 — validation lands BEFORE the mesh forwards the first agent request); `iss` deliberately not compared: a valid signature already proves minting by this core. `_mesh_gen` re-exports `gateway_pb2`/`gateway_pb2_grpc` LAZILY (PEP 562) so a stale generated dir cannot break `import vesmaro` (the known gen-proto trap); `scripts/gen-proto.sh` regenerates the third file atomically. Tests: 26 new envelope/CLI tests (round-trip; tampered sig/payload → `bad_signature` incl. a sub-spoof forge; expired/not-yet-valid; aud mismatch; revoked jti + idempotent re-revoke; revoke-all; grace both-valid→old-fails-closed; unknown-jti; malformed battery; v=2 rejection; scope grammar incl. `*`/blank/class-dup refusals; TTL ceiling; key 0600/reload-stable/wrong-type PEM refusal; CLI e2e issue→validate→rotate→revoke with the plaintext printed exactly once and never in `list`) + 8 ReadMemory servicer tests over the real Unix-socket server (fed-id fetch, raw-id fetch, unknown/empty id, no-federate id-oracle, denied project, narrowing mismatch, moderation-refuse trigger). `buf breaking` still not runnable locally (buf absent, same as the ADR-0020 entry) — the delta is service/RPC/message-additive only, no renumber, green by construction. Part 2 (gateway listener in the mesh + the token gate on the forwarded core leg) and part 3 (E2E) remain open.

- **Subscription cursor contract, core side — opaque core-owned resume cursors + storage-revision reserve (ADR-0020 / ADR-0021 Q10.6, archcom 2026-09-20, mnemos-mesh #27)** (`federation/proto/mnemos_core_api.proto`, `federation/proto/federation.proto`, `federation/proto/README.md`, `src/vesmaro/mesh_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/storage/sqlite_store.py`, `tests/test_mesh_server.py`, `tests/test_contract_sync.py`) — additive-only proto delta (the canonical bytes the Go mesh copies): `ListMemoriesResponse.cursor = 4` (opaque checkpoint minted by core, non-empty iff the page is non-empty; empty = legacy fresh-subscribe already handled by mesh v1.1.1), `ListMemoriesRequest.resume_cursor = 8` (takes priority over `since`; malformed/foreign → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, never a silent empty page), `CompactRecord.revision = 12` (storage revision = SQLite rowid at export, monotonic per record, `0` = unknown; reserved BEFORE S2 per ADR-0021 ruling Q10.6 because LWW-by-timestamp accrues conflict debt from the first sync; deliberate exception to the mirror-compact.py rule — storage provenance, not envelope content), plus the peer-leg cursor transport ratified with ADR-0020: `PullRequest.cursor = 6` (replays the opaque token byte-for-byte; empty = full pass, preserving the #34 one-shot disaster-recovery semantics) and `PullResponse.cursor = 6` (the responding core's checkpoint; empty = pre-cursor peer → the client degrades to a full pass) — unblocking mesh-side incremental pull `--resume`. Core implementation: cursor = `base64url(json{"v":1,"rowid":N})` (CORE-PRIVATE, version-tagged, do not change without bumping `v`); the parser is strict-by-contract — rowid outside `[1, 2^63-1]` (SQLite cannot bind larger; uncaught it would surface as `UNKNOWN`, not `INVALID_ARGUMENT`) and tokens longer than 128 chars are rejected as `CursorError` → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`; a new `SQLiteStore.list_all_for_mesh` walks the ACL-intersected project set in ONE `rowid ASC` query (`after_rowid` resume bound, legacy `since` still honoured when no cursor is sent; scope stability on resume is the caller's duty — widening the scope after a checkpoint skips older rowids, inherent to rowid cursors), so every page boundary is a project-global checkpoint — resuming yields exactly the undelivered remainder across projects, and the pre-cursor per-project DESC pagination trap (a mid-walk DESC page checkpointing past undelivered older rows) is closed by construction. Known accepted limitation (Phase 0): SQLite can reuse a deleted max rowid, so deleting the row a cursor points at could let one newer insert slip past a resume; closing it needs a monotonic-sequence migration (DBA/archcom decision), not a quick fix. `SyncMetadata` was audited against ADR-0021 Q10.2/Q10.3: the `SubscribeStream.record` oneof (`compact`/`metadata`) already provides the record-vs-metadata-only variant and the poll-first `MetadataSync*` stays index-only by design — no change.
