Tests: +8 over real gRPC Unix socket + real SQLite (mint non-empty + stable on repeat, empty page → empty cursor, resume → exactly the 3 new records and empty thereafter, `resume_cursor` priority over a future `since`, 6 garbage-cursor shapes → `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (non-base64, non-JSON, wrong version, non-int rowid, rowid > 2^63-1, oversized token), legacy `since` filtering unchanged, limit-2 cursor-walk partitions 7 records across ≥3 pages with no dupes/gaps, `revision` > 0 and strictly increasing per page). `buf breaking` not runnable locally (buf absent) — the delta is field-additive only (4/8/12, no renumber), green by construction under the README's breaking-detector policy.

- **PG-0 «Foundation» — the project code graph: sidecar store, tree-sitter indexer, 10 MCP tools + `/graph/` REST namespace, token contract, recall beacon, watch poll, security invariants PG1–PG7 (ADR-0032 §3, ArchCom 2026-09-28)** (`src/vesparo/storage/code_graph_store.py`, `src/vesparo/codegraph/` — new package: `languages.py`, `file_surface.py`, `indexer.py`, `incremental.py`, `audit.py`, `service.py`, `watch.py`; `src/vesparo/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesparo/api/main.py`, `src/vesparo/manager.py`, `src/vesparo/config.py`, `src/vesparo/assemble.py`, `pyproject.toml`; docs: `docs/en/user/mcp-tools.md`, `docs/ru/user/mcp-tools.md`, `docs/en/user/http-api.md`, `docs/ru/user/http-api.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `config.example.yaml`) — a MEMORY-grade symbol graph over REGISTERED project roots, stored in the sidecar `code_graph.db` (never the main DB, never the vault): nodes (`Project`/`File`/`Module`/`Class`/`Function`/`Method`/`Type`) and edges (`CONTAINS_FILE`/`DEFINES`/`IMPORTS`/`CALLS`/`INHERITS`/`TESTS`/`USES` — an unproven call is never `CALLS`, the provenance-honest fallback is `USES`) persisted as `code_graph.<data-dir>`-adjacent schema v1 with CHECK-enforced kinds, atomic whole-project publish, and a per-project poisoned set. **PG1 zero bytes of source:** only names, qnames, line ranges and signature SHAPES (no defaults, no docstrings, no literals — enforced at the parser level); **PG2 root confinement:** only projects registered in the projects table are indexable, and every path argument is normalised + refused if it escapes the registered root; **PG3 default surface:** denylist first (dotfiles, lockfiles, vendored dirs…), then an extension allowlist — a file that hits the secrets detector at index time joins the POISONED set and is refused at snippet issuance FOREVER (only `mnemos_delete_graph_project` clears it); **PG4 scan at issue:** snippets are read from disk at request time through mtime+size+sha256 freshness against the indexed record — divergence yields a staleness marker, never content — and the range is secret-scanned at issuance with ANY hit refusing fail-closed; no snippet cache exists; **PG5 born-no-federate:** the sidecar is export-blind — graph artifacts never ride memory export/federation, peers re-index locally; **PG7 limits and audit:** `index_max_files` (20000) / `index_max_source_mb` (500) are fail-closed — a breach aborts the WHOLE index and the previous graph survives, no partial publish — and every call (read or write) is audited per agent (`agent` required everywhere, `attribution-required` otherwise). Tool surface (master flag `code_graph.enabled`, ON BY DEFAULT per the owner decision of 2026-09-28 — see the follow-up entry below): `mnemos_index_project`, `mnemos_project_graph_status`, `mnemos_search_graph`, `mnemos_trace_path` (BFS depth ≤ 2, fanout cap 32, total-work cap 512 — the ADR-0030 walk discipline), `mnemos_get_file_outline`, `mnemos_get_code_snippet`, `mnemos_check_graph_coverage` (indexed/stale/parse-error/unindexed/poisoned — «clean ≠ proof» stays visible), `mnemos_get_graph_schema`, `mnemos_list_graph_projects`, `mnemos_delete_graph_project` — each with a `/graph/*` REST twin mapping refusals honestly (`503` disabled / `403` confinement / `413` limit / `400` bad-request). Token contract (§3.4) on every windowed output: `max_output_tokens` 128–1M (default 3200), budget in BYTES = tokens × 4 (a deterministic 4 UTF-8 bytes/token ceiling), rows never split (whole-row / whole-line drops), strictly advancing cursor, a zero-fit budget refused (`GraphBudgetError`) instead of looping. Discovery: the recall beacon v1 — ONE ≤200-byte tail line after the `assemble_context` budget stage (outside the budget, never pinned) reporting freshness + the `mnemos_search_graph` call hint (flag `code_graph.beacon`, default on when enabled). Indexing: tree-sitter (`tree-sitter` + `tree-sitter-python`, promoted to core dependencies by the same default-on decision), incremental by mtime+size with a per-project serialization lock (concurrent index → `in-progress`), staleness classification with zero byte reads on the cheap path. Tests: PGT acceptance suite (zero-bytes e2e, export-blind sidecar, poisoned-forever, self-repo acceptance) + tool-surface/token-contract/PG-mechanics + indexer/store suites. Docs: MCP/REST references (EN+RU) with the operator gate and token contract; ARCHITECTURE.md «Project graph» section; `code_graph:` block in `config.example.yaml`.


- **Graphs ON by default (owner decision 2026-09-28 «graphs on by default») — both graph families flip to default-on, tree-sitter promoted to a core dependency** (`src/vesmaro/config.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/languages.py`, tests, docs EN+RU) — the owner exercised the reserved default-on decision: `graph_auto_mint` / `graph_walk` / `feedback_apply` (ADR-0030 A0/A1 legs) and `code_graph.enabled` / `code_graph.watch` (ADR-0032) now default to `true`; the `[code-graph]` optional extra is retired in the same unreleased wave and `tree-sitter` + `tree-sitter-python` move into core `dependencies` (a default-enabled flag over a missing optional package would be a silent degradation — graphs are first-class, ecosystem components build on them). Nothing self-starts: the project graph still waits for an explicit `index_project` (and the watch poll for an explicit `watch_start` registration); minting pays one hybrid search per write; the walk rides its reserved quota with the 0.9121 guard floor. Operators can hide either family with one flag (`code_graph.enabled: false` / `VESMARO_MNEMOS__GRAPH_WALK=false` etc.). Leg-isolation goldens now pin the graph legs off explicitly; default-pin tests assert the new defaults and the flag-off contracts stay covered. Verified: full suite 4575 passed / 0 failed; bench s1 gate PASS (recall@5 = 0.9484).

- **PG-0.5 — native auto-indexing of the project graph (ADR-0032 wave PG-0.5, owner directive 2026-09-29: indexation happens by itself — no explicit agent/harness call, no instruction, no skill)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/autoindex.py` — new, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/watch.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/audit.py`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesmaro/hooks.py`, `src/vesmaro/config.py`; docs: `docs/en|ru/user/mcp-tools.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/http-api.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, ADR-0032 status update) — every dispatched MCP tool call (ONE cut-in point at the top of `_dispatch`) and every `pre_llm_call` hook emits a non-blocking activity hint (`MemoryManager.codegraph_activity_hint`; bounded queue, hint never raises and never blocks the caller). The `AutoIndexer` consumer rides the generalized single scheduler thread (`GraphWatchScheduler.submit` one-shot jobs — the same cooperative thread hosts the watch poll AND auto-index drains; never thread-per-hint) and per hint: marker-gated auto-registration (a project absent from the projects table whose cwd carries a packaging MANIFEST — `pyproject.toml` / `setup.py` / `package.json` / `go.mod` / `Cargo.toml`; a bare `.git` does NOT trigger, `$HOME` and the filesystem root are always refused — gets registered with that root, attribution in the description, new audit action `auto-register`), then a background first index (audit reason `auto-first`) when the graph is empty or an incremental reindex (reason `auto-stale`) when the cheap mtime+size classification finds actual changes. **One root = one graph** (review fix): a name hint whose resolved root already belongs to an existing project REUSES it (audit `auto-register-reused`); a global cap `code_graph.auto_register_max_projects` (default 64) makes further auto-registrations a silent skip with an `auto-register-capped` audit row. **No retry tax** (review fix): a failed first auto index writes `auto_suspended` — further hints skip the tree with NO disk walk until a successful manual/watch publish or `delete_graph_project`. Guardrails: per-project throttle `code_graph.auto_reindex_min_interval_sec` (default 300s, sidecar `last_auto_action` stamp, reserve-then-act); PG7 attribution — the hinting agent is the actor, no agent means no auto action (v1 boundary: `mnemos_recall_context` carries no `agent`, so the native path fires from `pre_llm_call`, `add`/`save`/`assemble`/`context_rewrite`, or explicit graph tools); PG7 fail-closed limits hold identically on the auto path; v1 limitation — a multi-path registration indexes `paths[0]` only. REST is deliberately NOT an auto surface; manual tools unchanged; `code_graph.auto_index: false` turns the auto path off. The recall beacon then appears BY ITSELF — zero harness-side wiring. Tests: `tests/test_codegraph_autoindex.py` (15: native e2e via the real MCP dispatcher, stale reindex, marker-less cwd ignored, `$HOME` mock refused, flag-off no-op, throttle coalescing, limit fail-closed, one-root-one-graph reuse, cap, suspension walk-spy).

### Changed

- **`mnemos_watch_start`/`watch_stop`/`watch_status` — new semantics: the project-graph watch poll (ADR-0032 §3.2), the legacy directory-watcher form removed** (`src/vesparo/mcp_server.py`, `src/vesparo/api/main.py`, `src/vesparo/manager.py`, `src/vesparo/codegraph/watch.py` — new) — behaviour change, not an addition: the M8-era `mnemos_watch_start` was an UNIMPLEMENTED STUB that reported false success (`✅ Watcher started on [...]`) while nothing was watching; the stub is gone. The tools now register a project in the IN-PROCESS watch poll: `project_id` + `agent` are REQUIRED (PG7 attribution), `session` optional; the poll checks the project's graph files by mtime+size on the adaptive interval (`watch_base_interval_sec` + 1s per 500 indexed files, `watch_max_interval_sec` cap — one cooperative thread for ALL registrations, no daemon) and reindexes on ACTUAL changes with audit reason `watch`. The legacy form (`paths=` / `scan=` / `include_rules=`) is REFUSED with `code: "bad-request"` and an explicit explanation — those arguments never did anything. Prerequisites fail honestly: `code_graph.enabled` or `code_graph.watch` off → `code: "disabled"`, no index yet → bad-request (the poll reindexes; it never seeds a first index), registration cap (`watch_max_registrations`, default 8) reached → bad-request, missing `agent` → `code: "attribution-required"`. `watch_stop(project_id?)` is idempotent and drops one or ALL registrations; `watch_status` reports `running`/`watch_enabled`/`cap`/`registrations[]` with the last poll outcome per project (`runs`/`reindexes`/`last_result`/`last_error`). Registrations live for the process lifetime (a restart drops them — re-register). REST twins (`POST /watch/start`, `POST /watch/stop?project_id=`, `GET /watch/status`) mirror the shapes; consumers of the old `paths`/`files_queued` status fields must migrate.

### Fixed

- **Project-graph review tails P3 (trackers 10173a2a + 57ae9a66)** (`src/vesmaro/codegraph/indexer.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/service.py`, `src/vesmaro/codegraph/watch.py`, `src/vesmaro/storage/code_graph_store.py`, `src/vesmaro/manager.py`, tests) — six review findings closed: signature defaults are dropped WHOLE including bare NAMES in default position (`f(x=SOME_NAME)` used to leak the name — rendered `f(x=…)` now, literals/calls alike); `manager.close()` closes the lazily built graph service (sidecar store + audit connections no longer live on GC; the closer never builds one); the watch stop/register race is closed by an exit-commits-under-the-lock protocol (a registration in the thread-exit window is always polled — never silently orphaned); the limit-breach audit reason carries the root BASENAME, not the absolute path (the raised exception keeps the full root for the log); `search_graph.total_matches` is the honest COUNT of the predicate instead of the top-limit slice size (60 matches / limit 10 no longer reports 10); and `watch.close()` is final — a late registration after a long job outlived the 5s join is refused instead of resurrecting the poll thread.

- **Cascade integrity fixes (#441)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/canon_validate.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/import_.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `src/vesmaro/models.py`) — `metadata.canon_warnings` is gate-owned: client copies are stripped on create and update, honest warnings are CLEARED by a fixing edit (they used to stick forever), and a forged list can no longer impersonate the validator. Client-authored canon envelopes for `task`/`decision`/`report` now PERSIST through generic writes (canon §2 — the W2-S1 blanket strip over-reached; `checkpoint` stays server-minted), so strict mode is meaningful for three of four types. The strict-mode canon gate runs BEFORE the vault write (a rejected record no longer leaves an Obsidian artifact). JSON import strips canon-line client keys by default (`--trusted-restore` for operator-asserted backups). Schemas-uninstall ownership no longer trusts a co-located manifest; schemas deploy fails loud when pack bytes diverge from the pin.
- **Worktree-independent canon pins (#436)** (`tests/test_integration.py`) — the vendored-schemas drift pin resolves the canon sibling via the engine repo's git-common-dir (correct from any linked worktree; the frozen fallback leg now compares sha256 hexdigest instead of decoded hash bytes) with a regression test pinning worktree-independence — fixes the red main of issue #433.

- **Canon-line engine integrity A1 — `canon_warnings` single-writer lifecycle (cascade review SEC P2-1 + QA P1-1)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `tests/test_canon_warn_validator.py`) — the key had three unowned write paths and one missing clear path: a client could FORGE `metadata.canon_warnings` on a generic create (not in the stamp strip set), DELETE honest warnings via an update metadata replacement (the client dict won wholesale), and a FIXING edit never cleared the key (the gate early-returned on an empty fresh violation list, leaving stale warnings stuck forever). `_canon_gate` is now the SINGLE writer: client copies are stripped on both the create and update paths together with the forged stamps, the key is never merged back like internal lifecycle keys, and a fresh EMPTY violation list REMOVES a stale key — only an actual content/metadata fix clears warnings. The `INTERNAL_METADATA_KEYS` docstring block documents the gate-owned status.

- **Canon-line engine integrity A2 — client task/decision/report envelopes persist (cascade review SEC P2-2, TL ruling: engine returns to ratified canon §2)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `src/vesmaro/models.py`, `src/vesmaro/canon_validate.py`, `tests/test_canon_warn_validator.py`) — ADR-0003 obligation 3 was read over-broad in W2-S1: canon v1.0.0 §2 defines CLIENT-authored envelopes for the non-checkpoint types (task/decision/report carry `owner_slug`/`priority`/`size`, `reversible`, `period` — extras the server cannot know), and the unconditional `canon` strip on generic creates/updates broke that contract (W3a pack docs, ADR-0004 fixtures and the neighbor intake contract all assume persistence). The strip is now TYPE-CONDITIONAL at the call sites: checkpoint-type canon strips as before (`save_checkpoint` stays the only minter), malformed canon (non-dict, unknown type) strips whole with the forged-stamp warning, and client envelopes persist — flowing into the canon gate, so warn/strict modes are now MEANINGFUL for the three client types (a violating task envelope rejects the create in strict mode). The update MERGE-BACK is equally conditional on the row's own envelope: a minted checkpoint row stays a checkpoint row, while a task-envelope row's corrected replacement stands. `models.py` documents: «ADR-0003 obligation 3 read over-broad in W2-S1; canon §2 ratified client envelopes for non-checkpoint types — corrected by cascade review SEC P2-2».

- **Canon-line engine integrity A3 — the canon gate runs BEFORE the vault write (cascade review SEC P3-1)** (`src/vesmaro/manager.py`, `tests/test_canon_warn_validator.py`) — `manager.add()` ran `_canon_gate(create_path=True)` AFTER the Obsidian vault markdown write, so a strict-mode reject raised `CanonViolationError` with the refused content already persisted as a vault file no SQLite row ever pointed at (and row deletion could never clean it up). The gate now sits above BOTH persistence sinks (vault + SQLite), following the doc-grouping gate's ordering precedent; the gate-attached warnings also land in the vault render together with the stored row.

- **Canon-line engine integrity A4 — JSON import strips canon-line server-minted keys + `--trusted-restore` (cascade review SEC P3-5)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/import_.py`, `src/vesmaro/cli/import_cmd.py`, `tests/test_export_import.py`) — `_import_json` saved export rows with their metadata verbatim: a crafted export landed checkpoint stamps, a checkpoint-type canon envelope and gate-owned `canon_warnings` as if the server had minted them (a forged `checkpoint_dedup_key` could then satisfy a later genuine `save_checkpoint` dedup). Imported rows now strip `CHECKPOINT_STAMP_KEYS` + `canon_warnings` + checkpoint-TYPE canon by default (untrusted input; one warning line per affected row), client task/decision/report envelopes persist (canon §2 — peer data), and the new `--trusted-restore` flag (documented in the command help) bypasses the strip when the operator asserts a trusted self-backup. The canon type predicate lives once in `canon_validate.canon_envelope_is_client_authored`, shared by the manager strips and the import strip.

- **Canon-line engine integrity A5 — schemas uninstall ownership by pack byte-identity only (cascade review SEC P3-3)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `tests/test_integration.py`) — `_uninstall_schemas` accepted two ownership proofs a co-writer of the deploy directory could forge for files they placed there: a checksum listed in the on-disk `mnemos-schemas.manifest.json` (a plain file sitting NEXT to the schemas — forging one naming a foreign file made uninstall delete it) and file-NAME overlap with the pack (names matched, not bytes). For `*.schema.json`, ownership is now provable ONLY by an inline stamp or BYTE-IDENTITY with a current pack schema (same name AND sha256); manifest checksums never prove ownership of files they sit next to. Drifted/older-version/foreign copies survive uninstall as user files (the safe direction; `update` restores genuine drifted deployments in place).

- **Canon-line engine integrity A6 — schemas deploy verifies the canon pin before writing (cascade review SEC P3-4)** (`src/vesmaro/cli/integration.py`, `tests/test_integration.py`) — `_deploy_schemas` wrote pack checksums straight into the manifest without checking them: a tampered pack file (edited on disk, unpinned addition, stale pin entry) deployed wrong bytes while the manifest claimed the clean canon pin, silently defeating the ADR-0003 pin protocol. Every pack schema checksum is now verified against `SCHEMAS_SOURCE_PIN["sha256"]` in both directions BEFORE any write; any mismatch raises `ValueError` naming the offending files per class (drifted / unpinned / stale_pin) and nothing deploys.

- **W3 review nits — `INTERNAL` mapping for mid-validation store failures at both `validate_agent_token` call sites + a `bad_signature` data-path test (PR #398 review, P3-1/P3-2)** (`src/vesmaro/mesh_server.py`, `tests/test_mesh_server.py`) — a runtime crash of the validator itself (e.g. a locked/corrupt SQLite hit during the jti denylist lookup inside `validate_agent_token`) escaped the servicer as gRPC `UNKNOWN` with the raw exception repr in the wire details (leaking store paths to the mesh caller); both call sites — `_agent_data_gate` (the W3 part 3 per-request data gate) and the `ValidateAgentToken` RPC (part 2) — now wrap the call and map any such failure to `INTERNAL` with a FIXED details string ("agent token validation failed" / "token validation failed"), the cause going to `logger.error` only (mirroring the existing `_token_validation_deps` init-failure mapping). New test `test_bad_signature_unauthenticated` on the DATA path: a structurally valid envelope signed by a FOREIGN Ed25519 key (freshly generated in the test; jti genuinely registered, agent id / `aud` / scope genuine and matching the request metadata, so the signature is the ONLY failing check) with `x-mnemos-agent-id` present → `ListMemories` → `UNAUTHENTICATED` with the coarse `bad_signature` reason in details.

## [5.0.0] — 2026-10-01 — VESMA rebrand
> Retroactive note: v5.0.0 also carried the pre-rebrand engine waves already on main — canon engine-integrity fixes (#441), the date-detector rework (#437/#442), and the external Jev adapter (W4c, `8bc139b`, default-off).


### Changed (breaking)
- **Product name: Mnemos → Vesma.** PyPI: naked `vesma` (canonical install) + full legacy-compatible name `vesma-memory-server`; npm: naked `vesma` + scoped `@vesma/*`; GH org stays `vesmaro`, repo renamed `vesmaro/vesma` in the release window.
- **CLI:** canonical command `vesma` (aliases `vesmaro`, legacy `mnemos` — deprecated, retire no earlier than 6.0 per dual-import contract ADR-0031).
- **MCP brand env:** `VESMA_MCP_BRAND` is canonical; `VESMARO_MCP_BRAND` kept as deprecated alias (dual-read); `mnemos_*` tool names and `mnemos:` tag namespace stay live wire contracts until 6.0.
- **Version chain:** `vesma` → `vesmaro` → `mnemos-memory-server` (importlib lookup order, both in `vesmaro.__init__` and the version guard).

### Changed
- src legend: 54 files (docstrings/comments/help, no code-channel changes); tests legend: 8 files; docs EN/RU: 91 files (README h1/badges/banner-alt; env tables use the `VESMARO_*` code truth with a deprecated `MNEMOS_*` note block; EN/RU parity 28↔28).
- dev-plan: Vesma 5.0.0 wave row (commit `3697e45`).

### Fixed
- stray tracked `.venv` symlink removed from the tree (regression surfaced by PR #441) and ignored.
- version drift guard (#204): VERSION==pyproject==metadata enforced synchronously.

## [4.3.0] - 2026-09-16

### Changed

- **Search v2 query semantics — FTS v2 builder, project soft fallback, graph leg, embedding_id stamp (issue #313, ADR-0029)** (`src/mnemos/storage/sqlite_store.py`, `src/mnemos/manager.py`, `src/mnemos/models.py`, `src/mnemos/storage/vector_store.py`, `src/mnemos/cli/main.py`, `tests/{test_search_v2_golden,test_search_v2_scope_fallback,test_search_v2_graph_leg,test_embedding_id_backfill}.py` — new, `tests/test_security.py`) — the M15.2 whole-input quoted phrase (the injection hardening) made multi-token queries adjacency phrases (`"GWS конвейер"` → 0 live hits vs 4 for the per-token AND) and disabled prefix matching (`конвейер` 49 rows vs `конвейер*` 77 — inflected RU/EN forms invisible). The v2 builder keeps the hardening and removes the side effects: every token is individually quoted with a builder-owned prefix star (`"tok"*`), AND-joined (cap 8 terms), with a de-hyphenated OR-alternative per hyphenated token (`("release-trigger"* OR "release"*)` — unicode61 splits on hyphens, so the bare identifier can never match the split index); AND-empty multi-token queries retry ONCE with the OR join (bm25-ranked, logged). Scope drift (`release-pipeline` vs `releases-pipeline`) no longer silently zeroes scoped searches: the A9 pre-RRF predicate is unchanged, a NEW OUTER retry without the scope surfaces the rows TAGGED (`SearchResult.project_scope_fallback`, backward-compatible field) and audited (`search_stats()["project_scope_fallback_total"]`, new counter distinct from cross-project; explicit `status=` drill-downs are NOT retried — the same status policy holds on the retry, no junk resurfacing). New graph leg v1: after RRF fusion the top-`limit` fused ids expand 1 hop along `memory_edges` (`supersedes`, both directions — `get_incoming_edges` added), edge rows not already fused are appended with the deterministic decay `(1-alpha)/(rrf_k + 2*anchor_rank)` (same FTS weight at a strictly deeper position — never outranks its anchor; the naive `1/(rrf_k+2*rank)` DOES at alpha 0.5 and was caught by the golden decay test), carry `via_graph=True`, pass the SAME status/quarantine (ADR-0019 §5 — an edge is never a side door)/refined-only (§4) gates, capped at `limit` extra rows. `memories.embedding_id` (NULL for 1644/1644 live rows — a diagnostic trap; the vector leg resolves by id and worked) is now stamped by `upsert_embedding` on every write and closable for existing rows via `mnemos backfill-embedding-ids` (idempotent id-join against vectors.db, dry-run default, `--apply` to write; rows whose vector is gone stay NULL — the column never lies); the production-DB backfill run is deliberately NOT part of this slice. Snowball stemming is deliberately deferred (FTS rebuild migration) — prefix terms are the zero-migration morphology fix; recorded as the follow-up in ADR-0029. Tests: golden suite re-derives every live-probe defect as a semantic assertion (multi-token far-apart AND, inflected prefix, both hyphen spellings, drift-tagged fallback with counter, superseded sibling via graph, injection battery on the builder output, single-token no-regression proven as a prefix superset of the old exact phrase), plus the M15.2 escaping tests updated to the v2 shape and a builder-level injection class (output always executes; no operator syntax; stars are builder-owned).

- **`search.hybrid_alpha` default 0.7 → 0.5 — balanced RRF fusion (quick win, issue #300)** (`src/mnemos/config.py`, `config.example.yaml` + `config.container.yaml`, docs EN+RU (incl. cli-reference env defaults), e3 run manifests) — one constant: the RRF fusion weight now balances the FTS/vector legs. At alpha 0.7 the vector leg structurally subordinated any FTS-only match (an FTS-rank-1 hit scored 0.3/61 < a pure-vector rank-1 at 0.7/61); at alpha 0.5 they tie, so FTS-rank-1 matches stop drowning by construction — no special-case code. Measured on both embedder regimes (probe, issue #300): governance top-5 68→73/96 nano (+5.2pp, the full B1 recoverable set) / 68→69/96 lexical (+1.04pp); knowledge recall@5 +3.9pp nano / +3.7pp lexical; G-neg top-5 composition byte-identical (zero displacement). Per-call `hybrid_alpha=` overrides (manager/SDK/MCP) unchanged. Event-driven S1/S1m re-baseline per ADR-0020 (composition-algorithm change): recall@5 0.8637→0.902967 (S1 hybrid) / 0.863002→0.902269 (S1m nano); model fingerprint unchanged — fusion, not embedder. e3 run manifests now pin `retrieval.hybrid_alpha` in the content-addressed core (probe finding 6) so a future default re-tune is visible to e3 content-addressing; recorded runner-1 runs stay valid as history (version-gated verify).

- **Search v2 short-token guard — degenerate tokens no longer collapse bm25 idf (issue #314, closes via #317; ADR-0029)** (`src/mnemos/storage/sqlite_store.py`, `tests/test_search_v2_golden.py`, `tests/test_security.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/{s1.json,BASELINE.md}`, `docs/project/adr/0029-search-v2-query-semantics.md`) — 1-char tokens and RU/EN stopwords (`_FTS_STOPWORDS` frozenset, exact lowercase entries) matched nearly every row, collapsing bm25 idf to 0 and degrading any AND query containing one to LIMIT rows ordered by id-tiebreak noise. The guard runs between tokenisation and `_FTS_TERM_CAP`: 1-char tokens are dropped; stopwords are dropped unless the token is fully uppercase (acronym exemption — IT, QA, DB, CI, ML, GWS, and AND-as-literal survive; Cyrillic `isupper()` acronyms included); identifier-shaped tokens (v2, x1, p0) survive structurally (>=2 chars, never alpha stopwords — no digit-scanning code); an all-degenerate query keeps its original tokens (never-empty fallback — the builder must not introduce silent zeros); dropped tokens consume no term-cap budget. Injection safety untouched: tokens still flow through the same per-token quoting, no new user text reaches MATCH raw. S1/S1m baselines re-recorded per ADR-0020 (semantics change is permanent, the measured shift is real): reference recall@5 0.9366 → 0.9409, recall@10 0.9503 → 0.9642 (planted appearances 202 → 221); S1m recall@5 0.9275 → 0.9484; corpus/model fingerprints unchanged. Tests: golden suite section 8 (13 cases — builder pins, acronym/digit survival, never-empty whole-list fallback, cap interplay, manager-level recall) plus the `test_security` quoted-only pin updated to the guarded token list.

## [4.2.0] - 2026-09-13

### Added

- **Round-3 embedder weights + fingerprint-aware re-embed migration (ADR-0021)** (`src/mnemos/models/mnema-embed-v1/`, `src/mnemos/manager.py`, `src/mnemos/embeddings/__init__.py`, `src/mnemos/storage/sqlite_store.py`, `src/mnemos/cli/doctor.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/{s1.json,BASELINE.md}`, `tests/{test_embeddings_mnema,test_pipeline_state,test_doctor_vector_vintage}.py`, docs EN+RU) — the bundled `mnema-embed-v1` artifact is swapped to the round-3 export (epoch-3 checkpoint, teacher **Qwen/Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B**, `weights_sha256 3b752e06…`, int8 29.6 MB, MRL dims 64/128/256/384, opset 15, dataset fingerprint `b27f8cf0…`; corpus ~100k text→teacher pairs incl. 8086 real store entries, RU 41.9%; manifest schema-merged — the training-side export uses `embed_dim` and carries no `name`, the bundle contract keeps `name`/`dimensions` and gains the full provenance tail). **P0 migration gap closed**: `_vector_metadata` previously stamped only `content_hash`, so after a weights swap the unchanged content made old-geometry vectors look fresh forever — mixed embedding spaces with silent vector-search degradation. Every upsert now also stamps `model_fingerprint`; `heal_stale_embeddings` re-embeds any vector whose fingerprint is missing (pre-swap vintage) or differs from the current embedder, through the single write point `upsert_embedding`, keeping the batch/limit budget — and the sweeper now PAGES through the whole refined set (previously it re-read the same `created_at DESC` head window every cycle, so a uniform-vintage migration could never drain past the first `limit` rows). Provider identity contract: `EmbeddingProvider.fingerprint` (concrete default = class name) with `nano:sha256:<weights>` for the bundled model, `ollama:<model>` / `onnxhub:<model>@<revision>` / `st:<model>` coarse switch-detection identities for the external providers (documented limitation: no weights hash, so silent upstream weight refreshes under the same name are not detected), plus a doctor-side twin `config_fingerprint(cfg)` that computes the identity WITHOUT loading a provider session and a test pinning twin==instance. `mnemos doctor`'s Vector store check now counts vintage-mismatched rows (WARN + the `mnemos reindex` / background-heal recommendation; diagnostics only). S1 re-baselined in the same PR per ADR-0021: deterministic corridors unchanged (recall@5 0.8637), S1m honestly re-recorded on the round-3 weights — recall@5 0.87452 → 0.863002 (−0.0115, inside CI95 ±0.0439), with the eval-jig student recall@5 gain 0.428 → 0.4485 (191 judged queries, teacher const 0.6071) as the adoption evidence. Docs: EN/RU architecture overview carries the round-3 lineage + vintage-tracking note; the migrate runbook (EN/RU) explains the gradual background re-embed on upgrade. Tests: +12 (fingerprint pin/twin/legacy-degradation/coarse formats, metadata stamp, heal on mismatch/missing/no-op, limit-drain, quarantine skip, doctor vintage verdicts incl. corrupt metadata + JSON surface).
- **`mnemos doctor`: pending-refinement queue diagnostics (ADR-0019 Phase D)** (`src/mnemos/cli/doctor.py`, `tests/test_doctor_pending_refine.py`, `README.md`) — new "Pending refine" health check: counts rows with `pipeline_state='pending'` (the B2a refine intake, which the B1 backfill filled with bypass-era heritage) and WARNs with the `mnemos processor start` recommendation when the queue is non-empty — CLI-only deployments have no background daemon, so the queue never drains on its own. Diagnostics only: the doctor deliberately does not run the processor (it is a server-side service). A missing `pipeline_state` column (pre-ADR-0019 schema) or a missing DB is a PASS with an explanatory detail. The README's Auto-pipeline feature row now documents the pending state and the processor command. Tests: 7 new cases (missing DB, zero-pending, 1/3 pending WARN + recommendation, pre-ADR-0019 schema, JSON output wiring).

### Changed

- **Hermes bypass removed — `publish_on_write` neutralized (ADR-0019 Phase D)** (`src/mnemos/adapters/hermes.py`, `tests/test_hermes_adapter.py`) — the adapter no longer publishes on its own: `_maybe_publish` (the `publish_on_write` → `publish(skip_quality_check=True)` path) is deleted and every write verb (`add_memory` / `sync_turn` / `mirror_memory_write` / `session_end` / `save_checkpoint`) now goes out WITHOUT an explicit `status`, so the server's `mnemos.visibility` policy owns the initial visibility through the fail-closed ingest gate (ADR-0019 §2 B2b): `immediate` (default) publishes clean content at once with `pipeline_state=pending` (refused content is stored RAW, zero-loss); `curated` holds the row RAW + pending until the refine cycle gates the refined projection. `publish_on_write=False` therefore no longer means "raw forever": it is a no-op compatibility knob (still accepted because the Hermes shim passes it; an INFO line says so when flipped) — under `immediate` every entry is visible regardless of the knob, under `curated` the row is RAW + `pipeline_state=pending` and the refine daemon completes its visibility. Per-adapter visibility flags were explicitly rejected by the ADR-0019 committee (server-level default); the first-class `publish` surface and the REST `POST /publish/{id}?skip_quality_check=true` endpoint are unchanged — only the adapter's automatic use of the bypass is gone. Tests: the old bypass tests are replaced by the new-semantics pins (immediate + knob off → visible; curated + knob off/on → RAW+pending, visible after `refine_pending`; injection write → refused at `path=ingest`, RAW + `pipeline_state=None`, audited) plus a removal-contract guard (`_maybe_publish` absent, no `skip_quality_check`/`manager.publish` in the adapter source).

### Fixed

- **Security: fresh pip-audit advisories closed, `make verify` gate green again (#267)** (`pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`) — newly published PYSEC advisories against four pinned packages made every `make security` / CI pip-audit run red. Floors raised to the minimal fixed versions: `aiohttp>=3.14.1,<4.0` → `>=3.14.3,<4.0` (PYSEC-2026-3546/3547 fixed in 3.14.2, PYSEC-2026-3545 in 3.14.3 — one floor covers all three) and `cryptography>=48.0.1` → `>=50.0.0` (PYSEC-2026-3552; the major bump is verified compatible — the codebase only uses stable primitives: Fernet, AESGCM, PBKDF2HMAC, x509/rsa/hashes). The other two advisories hit venv tooling no direct pin controlled inside the audited dev environment: `pip>=26.2` (PYSEC-2026-3721; enters transitively via pip-audit → pip-api) and `setuptools>=83.0.0` (PYSEC-2026-3447; via grpcio-tools' runtime dependency) are now floored in the `[dev]` extra — the exact profile `make security` and CI audit. `uv.lock` freshly regenerated on a dedicated branch (no parallel-track drift carried in): the four fixes, the previously missing `types-PyYAML` dev pin added, and the setuptools-linked training-extra subtree re-resolved to latest (torch 2.12.1→2.14.0, triton, cuda-toolkit, nvidia-cudnn/nccl — lock-only collateral; training stays outside the runtime per ADR-0021 and is installed by no gate). No new `--ignore-vuln` crutches: the pre-existing documented `CVE-2026-45829` ignore (chromadb legacy) is untouched.
- **Federation/import rows now pass the Phase A danger gate (#166)** (`src/mnemos/cli/import_.py`, `src/mnemos/cli/sync.py`, `tests/test_federation_import_gate.py`) — `run_sync_import` (compact federation payload) and `_import_json` (JSON export merge/restore, including `--overwrite`) wrote peer-status rows directly through `sqlite.save()`, so a PUBLISHED record arriving from peering never met the ADR-0019 publication gate. Both paths now route every imported row through the new `gate_imported_memory` helper — the SAME single gate point the server uses (`MemoryManager._publish_gate_detection`, i.e. `danger_detectors.detect` over the served projection and the title, fail-closed, audited as `publish gate: … path=federation-import`): a positive danger signal or scanner error stores the row RAW + `pipeline_state=NULL` (zero-loss, invisible, no embed — and outside the refine intake so danger-class content cannot auto-refine back into visibility); a clean row keeps the peer's status and a NULL `pipeline_state` is stamped `pending` so the local refine queue picks it up (non-NULL peer states — `refined`, `quarantined` — are never clobbered; a peer's quarantine verdict survives import). An `--overwrite` refusal also evicts the formerly-published row's stale vector embed (N1 demotion hygiene, mirroring `MemoryManager.update`), and each refusal appends an operator-facing warning to the import result. Tests: 9 new cases (federated PUBLISHED with secret/injection-in-title/scanner-error → RAW+NULL+invisible+un-embedded+audited; clean → PUBLISHED+pending+embedded; JSON-import twin; overwrite demotion with embed eviction; quarantine preservation; clean RAW row joins the queue) — the mutation "remove the gate call" fails them first.

## [4.1.0] - 2026-09-06

### Added

- **Universal behavioral pack: always-on memory instructions for every target + new `opencode` target (#231)** (`src/mnemos/cli/integration.py`, `src/mnemos/cli/doctor.py`, `src/mnemos/cli/util.py`, `integrations/targets.yaml`, `integrations/agents_md/mnemos-always-on.md`, `integrations/extensions/mnemos-mcp.ts`, `tests/test_integration_agents_md.py`, `tests/test_mcp_presets.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `VERSION`) — the behavioral pack (always-on instructions) no longer stops at the Copilot family. A new `agents_md` deployment kind injects a compact always-on digest of the three instruction files (recall at session start; checkpoint before compaction and session end; tag contract on every write; memory ops are PRIORITY tools — recall before re-deriving, save before session end) as a stamped, additive block (`<!-- mnemos:integration:vX BEGIN … END -->`) into each harness's native standing-instructions file: `~/.agents/AGENTS.md` for the `agents` target (the one AGENTS.md-standard channel — Claude Code included, one mechanism instead of a diverging CLAUDE.md writer), `~/.zcode/AGENTS.md` for ZCode (read natively as user instructions), and `~/.config/opencode/AGENTS.md` for the NEW `opencode` target (OpenCode reads it natively). The block engine never touches user content: deploy appends around it (repairing a missing trailing newline so user text never glues onto the markers), update replaces only the stamped region in place, uninstall strips only the block (deleting the file only when deploy itself had created it), verify reports missing/stale/version-drift/content-drift. The `pi` target receives the same pack as a standing system-prompt hint injected by the shipped bridge extension's `before_agent_start` hook (Pi has no AGENTS.md surface; the extension already owns tool registration). The new `opencode` target deploys nested skills to `~/.config/opencode/skills/`, injects AGENTS.md, and registers MCP via an additive merge into `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json` under the top-level `mcp` key with OpenCode's native local-server entry (`{"type": "local", "command": ["mnemos", "mcp-server"], "enabled": true}`) — other servers, unknown keys and user-tuned `environment` values are preserved. `doctor` reports the new artifacts (per-target verify now covers blocks; OpenCode MCP config added to the MCP-server check). Tests: 37 new cases in `tests/test_integration_agents_md.py` (block engine, never-clobber lifecycle mutations, opencode end-to-end incl. MCP merge idempotency, schema guards over the real `targets.yaml`); the mcp-presets drift guard now validates the OpenCode local preset against its native contract (and fixes the stale 3-block count that #230's preset addition had silently broken). Fully backward compatible: copilot/cursor/hermes/generic-copilot/pi deploy maps unchanged. Version 4.0.0 → 4.1.0 (pyproject + `VERSION` in lockstep per #204): the pi bridge is existing deployed content whose change requires a stamp bump, so all targets cleanly update to v4.1.0. Docs pages are owned by the parallel docs wave and will document the capability after merge.
- **Bundled `mnema-embed-v1` as the default embedding model (NM-1c+1d, ADR-0021)** — 29.6 MB int8 ONNX, 384d, fully local search out of the box (no downloads, no API keys). `chromadb` removed from runtime dependencies (−3 CVEs, −250 MB install); `onnxruntime`/`tokenizers`/`huggingface_hub` promoted to direct core deps. Migration: `provider: chromadb` → warning + `mnema-embed-v1` fallback. S1m re-baseline: recall@5 0.929 → 0.875 (−5.5 pp accepted v1 trade for full autonomy; `provider: onnx` remains the quality escape hatch). Training corpus enriched 646 → 5996 unique texts (28 synthetic families, RU 49%).
- **BF-4 — full S2 nightly + one-page owner report + 192-query corpus (ADR-0020 §5–§8, epic #169)** (`benchmarks/stands/s2_timing/run.py`, `benchmarks/report_page.py`, `benchmarks/corpus/queries.py`, `benchmarks/reports/{latest.md,latest-prev.json}`, `benchmarks/reports/.gitignore`, `tests/test_benchmarks_bf4.py`, `Makefile`, `benchmarks/README.md`) — the final benchmark wave. **S2 nightly**: `--repeats N` (N ≥ 3) runs N full workload passes; the max−min spread of the per-repeat p50/p95 IS the measured noise band (the machine measures its own noise first). `analyze_nightly` (pure, unit-tested) turns band+baseline into per-verb statuses: a band wider than the corridor width (25%) → **NOISE**, de-escalated to report + ticket per ADR-0020 §5 with **exit 0** — never a block; a tight band whose median exceeds `baseline × 1.25` → **REGRESSION**, exit 1 (the nightly gate role); per-verb statuses are independent (a noisy verb de-escalates only its own comparison). `--record-nightly` is the ONLY writer of `benchmarks/baselines/s2.json` — the S2 baseline is born on the nightly quiet machine, never a developer laptop — and needs `--force` to overwrite (event-driven re-baseline only); a `workload_fingerprint` (sha256 of the stand module) fails the nightly gate on any workload edit until a same-PR re-record. **Owner report** (`make bench-report`): `report_page.py` renders `benchmarks/reports/latest.md` from ALL `baselines/*.json` (bytes, not memory) plus the freshest per-stand run report when it is NEWER than its baseline (anything older was superseded by a re-record and is ignored — no phantom corridor failures from stale runs): traffic light per family F1–F7 (green = corridor holds / invariant meets requirement; yellow = skip / noise / baseline-not-born; red = gate failure or invariant `ok:false` in any consumed JSON, and the generator exits 1 so cron-only runs alarm), 1–3 key-number lines per family with Δ to baseline, invariants as separate `= 1.0000 / = 0` lines assigned to their registry family (injection-acceptance → F2, duplicates + retraction leaks → F4, checkpoint integrity → F5, quarantine-exclusion → F6, cross-principal leak + render-neutrality → F7), and ↗/→/↘ trend arrows against the previous wave's snapshot (`latest-prev.json`, rewritten each generation; committed alongside the page — per-run JSONs stay gitignored). **Corpus growth 48 → 192**: 144 new judged queries in four self-describing families (`-ph` exact phrases lifted verbatim from corpus content, `-pr` paraphrases that ride the vector leg, `-tp` topics, `-xr` cross-record themes including six global cross-project queries), honest-small judgments only — every judged slug is verified admissible by test (a judgment against a `raw` entry would be permanently unwinnable). **Nightly contour wiring** (review N4 on #206): `make bench-s2-nightly` presets `MNEMOS_BENCH_S1M_REQUIRED=1` for the whole target and runs the S1 gate leg before S2, so the flag has a real consumer — the nightly contour is the only place where required-S1m semantics is mandatory, while local `make verify` stays soft; `S2_REPEATS` / `S2_NIGHTLY_FLAGS` make-variables parameterize the cron entry. Acceptance: `tests/test_benchmarks_bf4.py` (24 tests — corpus schema 192/unique-qids/admissible-judgments/family counts, live fingerprint match of the re-recorded baselines, the full analyze_nightly truth table incl. NOISE-exit-0 and per-verb independence, `--record-nightly` R<3 refusal, report traffic-light semantics on synthetic JSON incl. breached-corridor → red + exit 1 and stale-report ignoring, Makefile source pins incl. verify staying S1-only).
- **BF-3 — S3 long-lived-session stand (ADR-0020 §2, epic #169; ADR-0021 NM-2 prerequisite)** (`benchmarks/stands/s3_session/{scenario,run}.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/s3.json`, `tests/test_benchmarks_s3.py`, `Makefile`, `benchmarks/README.md`) — stand S3 answers the coherence question "does memory keep serving an agent hundreds of turns into a session" (families F5 + the F3/F4 cuts ADR-0020 assigns to S3). The session is DATA: `scenario.py` builds a fixed turn list from `random.Random(seed)` (same seed → byte-identical ops; logical time only, no wall-clock metric, BLAKE2b lexical embedder), one long-lived manager then replays fact writes (unique markers), past-fact searches (exact phrase / paraphrase), budgeted `assemble_context` (`pre_llm_call` shape), periodic `on_context_rewrite` events (CCR marker mint asserted) and checkpoint → new session → `recall_context` round-trips. Metrics: fact-retention@N,k (histogram {10,50,100,200}, k=5, greedy age-balanced bucketing with strict age feasibility — no fallback pollution of far bins), recall-drift-over-session (same early-fact sample at ~1/3 vs end; delta < 0 = degradation), checkpoint-return-integrity (binary invariant = 1.000: after every round-trip every fact so far is re-probed by search), sufficiency@task (required facts must land in the ASSEMBLED block, not merely in search), context-growth-factor (fixed budget + saturating 12-anchor query at ~10% vs the final turn — the F3 stop-signal; a non-saturating cluster measured 2.56 = budget filling, not composition purity), stage-discard-profile (from assemble stage telemetry; informational in baseline v1). Gate: the checkpoint invariant blocks always; retention/drift/sufficiency carry `baseline − max(0.02; 95% CI)` floors, growth a +0.02 ceiling; scenario-fingerprint or seed/turns mismatch demands a same-PR `--record` (corridors only compare identical sessions). Nightly class per ADR-0020: `make bench-s3` / `bench-s3-record` are NOT in `make verify`; the suite carries a compact determinism smoke + gate mutation tests (invariant breach, corridor breach, bucket starvation, growth ceiling, axis substitution). First baseline (240 turns, seed 42, < 1 s): fact-retention 1.000 overall and per bucket (n=14/15/15/9), drift delta +0.000, checkpoint-integrity 1.000 (3 round-trips, 120 fact probes, 0 misses), sufficiency@task 0.933 (14/15), growth-factor 1.000, rewrite events 16/16 markers minted. The stand's settings disable the context-rewrite wall-clock quotas (a multi-hour logical session compressed into seconds vs a per-real-minute quota — rationale pinned by a source test). S3 unblocks the ADR-0021 nano-refiner gate (NM-3 ships only while fact-retention@N,k holds here).
- **BF-2 — S4 availability-probe stand + S2-timing smoke (ADR-0020, epic #169)** (`benchmarks/stands/s4_availability/{fixture,probes,run,store_copy}.py`, `benchmarks/stands/s2_timing/run.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/s4.json`, `tests/test_benchmarks_bf2_tails.py`, `Makefile`) — stand S4 answers "is ALL memory correct and available at any moment" (owner family F6): a fixture store (published/refined/failed/quarantined/raw populations) is COPIED via the SQLite online-backup API (never file-copy — WAL-unsafe) into an isolated root; probes are strictly READ-ONLY (search both legs, get, list_recent, assemble on budget, marker parsing), idempotent, and audit-marked `actor=benchmark`; the read-only invariant is verified by a store checksum taken before and after the probe pass — any write trips the gate. Metrics: probe-pass-rate, memory-completeness = retrievable-admissible / admissible (quarantine is NOT in the denominator — its unavailability is correct behavior), paired invariant quarantine-exclusion = 1.000 (the availability gate applies only while it holds, per ADR-0020), embed-staleness (content_hash mismatch), audit-marking check. First baseline recorded: probe-pass-rate=1.0, completeness=1.0, quarantine-exclusion=1.0, read-only=ok. Stand S2 (timing): wall-clock wrappers around add/search/assemble/refine_single on a tmp store with a fixed ~1e3-operation load; smoke mode R=1 is informational by design (ADR-0020: S2 never blocks locally; full R-mode is a `--repeats N` stub for the nightly machine; S2 results go to reports/, NOT baselines — the timing baseline is a nightly-machine artifact). First smoke numbers (informational): add p50=0.72ms, search p50=0.97ms, assemble p50=1.87ms, refine_single p50=0.05ms. Make targets `bench-s4` / `bench-s2-smoke` (both outside make verify per the gate policy; S4 is a nightly-gate candidate, wiring in BF-4). Plus the review hardening tails: s1m root-isolation test (asserts the s1m store root differs from the reference root and no cross-dimension vectors mix — closes reviewer finding F1 on PR #206), pyproject mcp-floor guard test (parses the extra and fails on a floor regression to 1.x — closes F1 on PR #205), MNEMOS_BENCH_S1M_REQUIRED documented in benchmarks/README (CI wiring lands with BF-4 nightly contour).
- **#122 — PyPI distribution name decided: `mnemos-memory-server`** (`pyproject.toml`, `src/mnemos/__init__.py`, `tests/test_version.py`, `README.md`, `README.ru.md`, `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/pypi-publish.md`) — the name on PyPI (`mnemos` and `mnemos-memory` are taken by same-domain AI-memory projects) was the last undecided owner call blocking the first publish; the account was created 2026-09-01 and the recommendation `mnemos-memory-server` was accepted. Only the installable name changed: the import package stays `mnemos`, the CLI stays `mnemos`, the MCP server name stays `mnemos`. The `all` extra self-reference now points at the new distribution name; `__version__` resolves via the new distribution metadata (with an explanatory comment); the version test checks the same. README pip-wheel markers now reference the normalized wheel filename `mnemos_memory_server-…` and are repointed to the NEXT release (v3.2.0): tag v3.1.0 was cut with the old name and its npm channel (`pi-mnemos@3.1.0`) is already published, so the tag is NOT re-cut — no wheel asset matching the new name exists for v3.1.0, and `scripts/sync-readme-version.sh` keeps the marker correct from v3.2.0 on. The Hermes install note and the integration guides (EN/RU) name the new `pip install` line, `scripts/install.sh` builds the wheel URL from the normalized filename, and the `doctor` MCP-transport remediation strings install via the new distribution name. The pypi-publish runbook (EN+RU) records the decision: the name matrix stays as decision history, the G0 gate stays as a hard safety net, and the publish procedure is unchanged (`scripts/pypi-publish.sh` adapts to the name from `pyproject.toml` automatically, wheel-filename normalization included).
- **#185 — MCP SDK 2.x port: `mcp_server.py` off the removed 1.x decorator API + loud `doctor` transport check** (`src/mnemos/mcp_server.py`, `src/mnemos/cli/doctor.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_mcp_sdk2_port.py`) — `pyproject` declared `mcp[cli]>=1.0` with no upper bound while `uv.lock` pinned 1.28.1: pip consumers resolved `mcp 2.1.1`, where `Server` removed the 1.x runtime decorators (`@server.list_tools()`/`@server.call_tool()` → `AttributeError`), and the stdio transport died **silently** — the CLI kept working, so nothing surfaced the breakage. Direction B (owner decision, not sitting on 1.x): the module now speaks the 2.x low-level `Server` constructor-registration API — `Server("mnemos", version=__version__, on_list_tools=_on_list_tools, on_call_tool=_on_call_tool)` with thin adapters translating between the SDK request/response models (`PaginatedRequestParams`→`ListToolsResult`, `CallToolRequestParams`→`CallToolResult`) and the pre-port plain callables, which stay importable with their old signatures (`list_tools()`, `call_tool(name, arguments)`) for the test suite. All 26 `Tool(...)` entries moved to the canonical 2.x `input_schema=` kwarg (runtime accepts both via pydantic `populate_by_name`; the wire alias `inputSchema` is serialization-only, so the model-visible contract is unchanged — 26 names/schemas byte-stable). The floor protects the port: `mcp[cli]>=2.0,<3.0` — a pip consumer can never again resolve a 1.x that no longer matches the code. Direction C (loud diagnostics, interim guard superseded by the floor): new `mnemos doctor` check **"MCP transport"** imports `mnemos.mcp_server` (the full `mcp` SDK import surface) and reports the exact failure with remediation — `ImportError` → "MCP transport broken: …; install with the .[mcp] extra (mcp>=2.0,<3.0)", `AttributeError` (the 1.x-on-2.x class) names the SDK-version mismatch; healthy path shows "imports OK (SDK 2.1.1, 26 tools listed)". The mypy override comment in `pyproject.toml` updated (the untyped-decorators rationale is gone; the override stays for remaining untyped SDK call sites). Acceptance: `tests/test_mcp_sdk2_port.py` (14 tests — doctor healthy/broken-import/AttributeError/unexpected-error fail-loud rows, check registration, `--json` carries the row, in-memory SDK-2.x handshake probe `initialize`+`tools/list` → 26 tools via `create_client_server_memory_streams` (no subprocess; skips in the stub env, runs green against the real SDK), constructor-registration wiring, `input_schema` manifest attributes, adapter round-trips incl. `arguments=None` → `{}`); conftest stubs updated to the 2.x constructor contract. Manual probe on real SDK 2.1.1: stdio `initialize` → `tools/list` → **26 tools**, `doctor` green ("MCP transport … SDK 2.1.1, 26 tools listed").
- **NM-0 — S1m model-quality contour + `model_fingerprint` fail-loud (ADR-0021, epic #197)** (`benchmarks/stands/s1_quality/model_contour.py`, `run.py`, `harness.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/{generate_md.py,s1.json,BASELINE.md}`, `tests/test_benchmarks_s1.py`) — closes the QA blocker "S1 does not gate the production embedder": the stand measured the retrieval pipeline on the deterministic BLAKE2b reference while the REAL embedder (chromadb's all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ONNX) was recorded nowhere and never checked, so a silent weights substitution passed the gate. S1m runs the SAME judged corpus and golden queries through the production embedder in the same deterministic pass (second fresh golden manager via the new `embedder=` parameter of `build_golden_manager` — identical ingest/ranking path, no parallel machinery) and reports recall@k, precision@k (k∈{5,10}), MRR, nDCG@k in a dedicated `s1m` section of `s1.json`. Corridor is SELF-comparison only (`its own baseline - max(0.02; 95% CI)`) — never against the BLAKE2b numbers, which stay the single source for pipeline corridors per ADR-0021 (the reference measures retrieval mechanics, the model semantic quality). New top-level `model_fingerprint` field `{provider, model, weights_sha256, opset}` pins the production embedder: the sha256 hashes the REAL local ONNX artifact, so an on-disk weights swap changes it even when every provider string stays identical; API/lazy providers record identifier-only; `opset` is read via the optional `onnx` checker and participates only when both sides read it (readability is an environment property, not a model property). **Fail-loud**: a gate run whose live fingerprint differs from the recorded one is RED — "production embedder changed (old=X new=Y) — explicit re-baseline required (--record), same PR per ADR-0021"; a pre-NM-0 baseline without a fingerprint is the documented migration (first `--record` pins it — nothing recorded can silently diverge yet), and a pinned fingerprint plus an unverifiable environment fails when `MNEMOS_BENCH_S1M_REQUIRED=1` is set. **Skip semantics**: when the production provider cannot be built in the run environment (no cached weights, no network, missing optional dependency) the `s1m` section reports `{"status": "skipped", "reason": …}` — GREEN in the default local posture, RED under `MNEMOS_BENCH_S1M_REQUIRED=1` (CI); the BLAKE2b reference measurement in the same pass is never disturbed. `BASELINE.md`/`generate_md.py` render the new fields (S1m section, fingerprint line, model corridors in the gate table); `benchmarks/README.md` documents the contour and the new re-baseline trigger. Baseline honestly re-recorded with the live production embedder (first production fingerprint pinned: chromadb all-MiniLM-L6-v2, sha256 `4f148ba8…`, dim 384; measured recall@5=0.9787, recall@10=0.9858, MRR=1.0, nDCG@10=0.9852 over 47 judged queries; the leg runs in its own store root `root/s1m` — sharing one `vectors.db` with the reference leg would mix 256-dim BLAKE2b and 384-dim production vectors, fail `np.stack` on every vector search and silently degrade the model contour to FTS-only, a defect caught and fixed during NM-0 self-review). Acceptance: `tests/test_benchmarks_s1.py` grows to 13 tests — fingerprint schema (null-or-object, 4 fields, 64-hex sha256), fingerprint-equivalence semantics (strict on weights/provider/model, lenient on unreadable opset), the **silent-weights-swap mutation → gate RED with the explicit re-baseline message**, same-fingerprint corridor breach RED, pre-NM-0 migration PASS, skip green/red under the required flag, hermetic stub-math for recall/precision/MRR/nDCG (probe queries excluded from denominators).

### Changed

- **MCP SDK moved into core dependencies — the base install now serves agent harnesses out of the box (ADR-0023, committee 2026-09-06; #234)** (`pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `src/mnemos/cli/doctor.py`, `tests/test_mcp_core_isolation.py` — new, `tests/test_mcp_sdk2_port.py`, `tests/conftest.py`, `Containerfile`, `Dockerfile`, docs EN+RU sweep) — `mcp>=2.0,<3.0` (bare — the upstream `[cli]` extra adds only already-core `typer` + `python-dotenv`) joined `project.dependencies`; the `mcp` extra remains as an EMPTY compatibility alias so legacy `pip install "mnemos-memory-server[mcp]"` commands keep resolving. Before this change the base install could not run `mnemos mcp-server` at all — the primary agent-harness surface — and every fresh base install got a guaranteed `mnemos doctor` FAIL on MCP transport. Security verdict (committee): stdio adds no network perimeter (the authenticated HTTP API was already core), net-new transitive deps ~1 MiB (post-resolve: `httpx2`/`httpcore2`/`truststore`/`mcp-types`, `jsonschema`, `sse-starlette`), wheel size unchanged. Binding conditions implemented: import isolation guard (`tests/test_mcp_core_isolation.py` — AST scan pins the SDK to `mnemos.mcp_server` + a subprocess test proves the CLI import path never loads it + the dist-metadata core-requirement check) and the pip-audit/SBOM gate now covers the unified profile (the extra split is gone). `doctor` remediation strings no longer tell users to install an extra — they name the reinstall; `Containerfile`/`Dockerfile` install the plain package; `uv.lock` regenerated (the stale `mcp 1.28.1` pin predated the `>=2.0` floor and broke `uv sync --locked`); docs sweep EN+RU (~15 mentions) flips every install command to the plain package name and the variants tables to the one-package story. Plus a mechanical `ruff format` pass over 13 files with pre-existing format drift so `make verify` is green for the 4.1.0 cut.
- **README quick start simplified to three commands + ADR-0023/0024 recorded (architectural committee 2026-09-06)** (`README.md`, `README.ru.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md`, `scripts/sync-readme-version.sh`, `docs/project/adr/0023-mcp-core-dependency.md` — new, `docs/project/adr/0024-unified-harness-connection.md` — new, `docs/project/adr/README.md`) — the root README's quick start is now three one-command steps (install the full server via pip → `mnemos integration setup` connects the harness AND deploys the behavioral pack → `mnemos doctor` + a two-line try-it demo), with every install variant, footnote, and the container/wheel details delegated to the extended getting-started guide (linked in the same block); the `<!-- version:pip|image|tags -->` release-marker blocks moved from the READMEs into the two getting-started guides and `sync-readme-version.sh` now covers them. The committee (Tech Lead chair + Product Architect + Analytics + Security + System Engineer) ACCEPTED two proposals: **ADR-0023** — MCP SDK moves into core dependencies as bare `mcp>=2.0,<3.0` (extra `mcp = []` stays as an empty compat alias; implementation lands in 4.1.0; binding: lazy-import guard test, unified pip-audit/SBOM); **ADR-0024** — all harness connection consolidates into `mnemos connect [harness]` over the existing `targets.yaml` registry in three phases (4.1.0 alias + detect-all listing; 4.2 cursor/windsurf/claude-code targets + doctor on the registry; 4.3 Codex TOML-merge + legacy deprecation), with binding security controls (static shipped registry only, `--home`-rooted path constants, pack diff-review, "memory content is data, not instructions"). Team-local protocol + contract in `~/.gcw/architectural-committee/2026-09-06-*`; mnemos decision ids `8bc9cb7f` / `b7933de7` / `961ee793`.
- **User-first README refactor + install-variant clarity + CONTRIBUTING split (EN+RU, follow-up to the #230 docs wave)** (`README.md`, `README.ru.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md` — new, `CONTRIBUTING.ru.md` — new, `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md`) — the README is restructured around a four-step Quick Start (install → first memory → connect the harness → deploy the behavioral pack + `mnemos doctor`), with the contributor tail (git workflow, quality-gate description, "reproduce the green state") moved out into a new `CONTRIBUTING.md` / `CONTRIBUTING.ru.md` pair whose gate table now states the REAL `make verify` composition (ruff format+lint, `mypy --strict`, pytest 2300+, bandit+pip-audit, bench-s1, `mnemos doctor`, version guard — the old footer undercounted it) and documents the docs conventions (docs reflect code; EN/RU synchronous; `<!-- version:… -->` markers preserved). The install story is now a variants table that explains pip extras explicitly: the base package already stores and searches offline (bundled `mnema-embed-v1`), `[mcp]` is the optional dependency group adding the MCP SDK that `mnemos mcp-server` runs on — recommended because MCP is how every harness connects — alongside `uv tool` / `pipx` isolated installs, the CLI/REST-only base install, and the LLM-provider extras; the `pip install mnemos`-is-an-unrelated-project warning stays attached. Quick Start step 3 is a five-row connect table (VS Code / Claude Code / Cursor+Windsurf / OpenCode / everything else → presets), step 4 states the behavioral-pack coverage honestly (full pack for `copilot`/`generic-copilot`/`cursor`/`hermes`; skills+MCP for `zcode`/`agents`/`pi`; always-on instructions for the latter group tracked in #231). Getting Started's install section mirrors the same variants table; README `<!-- version:pip|image|tags -->` marker blocks preserved in both languages; the docs table gains the CONTRIBUTING row.
- **Docs-vs-code accuracy pass: every operational doc reconciled against the source contracts (owner directive «документация должна отражать код»)** (`docs/**` en+ru, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `scripts/smoke-federation.sh`) — a fact-sheet audit against the Typer CLI registrations, `config.py` settings/env forms, `mcp_server.py` tool registry, and the FastAPI routes fixed, among others: the nonexistent `mnemos migrate-from-ai-brain` command → the real `mnemos migrate from-ai-brain`; global-flag placement (`mnemos --verbose serve`; `--verbose` never parses after the subcommand); env vars the app never read (`MNEMOS_LOG_LEVEL`, `MNEMOS_STRICT_TAG_CONTRACT`) → canonical `MNEMOS_LOGGING__LEVEL` / `MNEMOS_MNEMOS__STRICT_TAG_CONTRACT`; legacy-alias marking for `MNEMOS_DATA_DIR` / `MNEMOS_VAULT__VAULT_PATH`; REST paths that never existed (`/api/v1/...` workflow prefix, `PUT/DELETE /memories/{id}`, `GET /search`) → the real routes; ARCHITECTURE.md §3 rebuilt to the real 26-tool MCP surface, §11 module tree rebuilt from an actual `ls src/mnemos` + docstrings (the old tree described modules that do not exist), §12 Cache Center honestly restated as shipped under the CCR / CacheAligner names, §5 DLQ verbs corrected to the HTTP routes; six nonexistent CLI verbs removed from the ARCHITECTURE CLI surface. **`docs/en/admin/federation-testing.md` + `scripts/smoke-federation.sh` were rewritten against the real config contract**: store isolation uses per-instance `config.yaml` selected via `MNEMOS_CONFIG` (the previously documented `MNEMOS_HOME` env var is not read anywhere in `src/` — both the doc's smoke steps and the script silently shared the default store); CLI forms corrected (`mnemos add` positional content + comma `--tags`, `sync import` positional source, `search` positional query, no `mnemos delete` verb — cleanup via `DELETE /memories/{memory_id}/workflow` or the project-ACL argument); the trigger-code list now matches `src/mnemos/trigger_codes.py` (`RATE_LIMITED` / `TRUNCATED` do not exist; rate limiting is HTTP 429). The fixed smoke script was verified by a live run: `SMOKE PASS` (isolated A→B export → import → search → idempotent re-import). **Reference completeness**: cli-reference gained the 9 missing subcommand sections (`workflow`, `fts`, `processor`, `reindex`, `filter`, `completion`, `auth`, `scanner`, `logs`) plus `doctor --json/--fix/--dry-run` and `serve --log-file` (now 22/22 subcommands, EN+RU); mcp-tools gained the 4 missing tool sections (`mnemos_tags`, `mnemos_tags_rename`, `mnemos_filter`, `mnemos_reprocess` — now 26/26, EN+RU); http-api gained `POST /tags/rename` and the `/memories/{memory_id}/workflow` GET/POST/DELETE section (EN+RU). ~80 broken anchors/links fixed repo-wide; `federation.md` intro de-staled (the mediated-pull path is live, not "not yet wired").
- **RU mirrors completed for the whole admin set (EN/RU documentation synchronous, owner requirement)** (`docs/ru/admin/federation.md`, `docs/ru/admin/federation-testing.md`, `docs/ru/admin/ssh-sync-hardening.md` — new) — the three EN admin pages that had no Russian counterpart are now mirrored 1:1 (headings, tables, mermaid, code fences byte-identical; terminology anchored to the existing corpus precedent: «peer», «mediated pull», «bearer-токен», «смок-тест» stay Latin per `docs/ru/user/sync.md` / `security.md` usage).
- **Docs overhaul around the PyPI distribution + `install.sh` switched to PyPI (post-v4.0.0 documentation pass)** (`README.md`, `README.ru.md`, `docs/en|ru/index.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/getting-started.md`, `docs/en|ru/features.md`, `docs/en|ru/user/integration-guide.md`, `docs/en|ru/admin/runbooks/install.md`, `integrations/mcp-presets.md`, `docs/README.md`, `scripts/install.sh`) — the user-facing docs now teach the registry install as the one-command path: `pip install "mnemos-memory-server[mcp]"` (or `uv tool install` / `pipx install` for an isolated `mnemos` on `PATH`), with the explicit warning that bare `pip install mnemos` resolves to an UNRELATED PyPI project; `scripts/install.sh` now resolves the latest version from the PyPI JSON API and installs `mnemos-memory-server[extras]==VERSION` instead of a GitHub-Releases wheel URL (flags and venv/launcher behavior unchanged). The harness-connection story is consolidated: `integrations/mcp-presets.md` retitled "Connect Mnemos to any harness" with a three-level path table (native target → one-line preset → adapter template), a NEW OpenCode preset (`~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`, `type: "local"`, command array), a manual VS Code `mcp.json` block, and `~/.agents`/ZCode/Hermes sections; the integration guide's per-target list and deploy-map table now match `integrations/targets.yaml` exactly (the previously documented `vscode-copilot` / `claude-code` target names do not exist — `copilot`/`generic-copilot` + the `agents` standard are the real ones). Getting Started rewritten PyPI-first (bundled `mnema-embed-v1`, offline; stale source-only install, v2.1.0 wheel references and the 2026-06 timestamps removed), the docs landing (`docs/index.md`) gained an install + connect-harness section, and the feature map moves packaging (PyPI / npm / GHCR) and the S1–S4 benchmark stands out of "partial"/"planned" into shipped. README quick start is pip-first with the installer script demoted into an install-options block (release automation `<!-- version:… -->` markers preserved), PyPI + npm badges added, the integrations matrix covers nine harnesses, and the footer test count updated to 2300+. All six RU mirrors synced; `mcp-presets.md` prerequisite updated the same way.
- **License: MIT → Apache-2.0 (owner decision, 2026-09-03)** (`LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, `README.md`, `README.ru.md`) — the open mnemos family (server, pi-mnemos npm channel, bundled model artifacts) ships under Apache-2.0 from this release. Why Apache over MIT (analytics verdict): (1) it is the niche norm of the agent-memory/vector peer group (mem0, letta, cognee, chroma, qdrant are all Apache-2.0) and carries pre-approval on corporate OSS policy lists where MIT's patent silence invites an extra legal question; (2) the explicit irrevocable patent grant + retaliation clause (§3) fits an ML-adjacent project embedding into third-party commercial agent stacks; (3) the trademark carve-out (§6) and the NOTICE slot protect the future ecosystem branding (mnemos-eyes) and document the mnema-embed-v1 provenance (distilled in-project from Apache-2.0 teacher paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2; no third-party weights bundled). Monetization is unaffected: the copyright holder is not bound by the license, so the planned closed-source GUI and paid offerings remain fully available. Historical note: the v4.0.0 tag was originally cut under MIT and then re-cut under Apache-2.0. No installable artifacts (PyPI, npm, wheels, container image) were ever published for the MIT-tagged v4.0.0 (PyPI had 3.2.0, npm had 3.2.0), so no packaged MIT 4.0.0 distribution existed; the MIT tag and its GitHub auto-generated source archives were publicly reachable, and those snapshots remain MIT (per-version fixity). All versions ≤ 3.2.0 remain MIT forever (per-version license fixity). Monetization roadmap recorded in ADR-0022: closed GUI product + sponsorships now; FSL (or commercial relicensing) only if trigger conditions fire (≥10k stars, ≥25k monthly pip downloads, mnemos-eyes GA − 1 month, or first external contributor of standing → CLA first).
- **NM-1c+1d — bundled `mnema-embed-v1` as the default embedder; chromadb removed from the runtime (ADR-0021, epic #197)** (`src/mnemos/embeddings/__init__.py`, `src/mnemos/config.py`, `src/mnemos/models/mnema-embed-v1/`, `pyproject.toml`, `config.{example,container}.yaml`, `Makefile`, `benchmarks/stands/s1_quality/model_contour.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/{s1.json,BASELINE.md}`, `tests/test_embeddings_mnema.py`, `tests/test_benchmarks_s1.py`, docs sweep en+ru) — the shipped default embedding is now the fully local search stack: the bundled `mnema-embed-v1` artifact (384d, int8 ONNX, RU+EN, ~30 MB inside the wheel — zero downloads, zero network; final artifact name approved by the owner, previously staged as `nano-embed-v1`; the provider config key stays the short `nano`). **chromadb is gone from the runtime**: −3 CVEs (incl. the long-ignored CVE-2026-45829) and −250 MB of install weight; `onnxruntime`/`tokenizers`/`huggingface_hub` became direct deps, external providers (`onnx`, `ollama`, `sentence-transformers`) remain available. Legacy configs degrade loudly instead of crashing: `provider=chromadb|chroma|default` migrates to `nano` with a deprecation warning, and the FULL legacy default pair (`chromadb` + `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, review #221 F1) now degrades the model to the bundled artifact too — previously it died with `FileNotFoundError` on the MiniLM spec; the S1m fingerprint mirrors the effective embedder. The S1m model contour was honestly re-baseline'd for the embedder switch (ADR-0020 event-driven trigger): recall@5=0.9293, recall@10=0.9424 on the 192-query corpus — the −5.5pp recall trade vs the chromadb/MiniLM reference is the accepted cost of the fully local, CVE-free default (quality-first operators can switch to `onnx`). Supporting the distillation, the training corpus was enriched 646 → 5996 entries (NM-1a/b). Housekeeping: `make update-chromadb` removed (nothing to upgrade), `config.example.yaml`/`config.container.yaml` ship `provider: nano` with the alternatives documented, and the en/ru docs sweep replaces current-architecture chromadb mentions with the bundled-model reality (historical ADR/CHANGELOG context preserved).
- **BF-4 re-baseline — judged corpus 48 → 192 (ADR-0020 trigger "corpus growth ×4")** (`benchmarks/corpus/queries.py`, `benchmarks/baselines/{s1,s3,s4}.json`, `benchmarks/baselines/BASELINE.md`, `tests/golden/test_golden_baseline.py`) — the corpus growth is an ADR-0020 event-driven re-baseline trigger, so all deterministic baselines were honestly re-recorded in the same change. The reference contour on the harder corpus measures precision@5=0.2293 (was 0.2979), precision@10=0.1199 (was 0.1489), recall@5=0.8637 (was 0.9858), recall@10=0.8944 (was 0.9858) over 191 judged queries — the drop is the honest cost of the new query families (paraphrases with no verbatim overlap; processed-entry targets that the vector-weighted RRF structurally buries — recorded as an open finding), not a pipeline regression: the same code measured both waves. S1m production contour on the same corpus: recall@5=0.9293, recall@10=0.9424, MRR=0.9466, nDCG@10=0.9249. The McNemar-power argument is now demonstrated by measurement: the interim sign test over hybrid-vs-FTS full-hit pairs moved from a handful of discordant pairs at 48 queries to b=46/c=9 discordant pairs at 191 (p_two_sided=4.3e-07, hybrid wins 163 vs 126 full hits) — the deferred McNemar activation threshold is reached. The golden D5 tripwire floors were re-derived per their documented rule (recorded baseline − 0.02): P5 0.21 / P10 0.10 / R5 0.84 / R10 0.87; the rewrite pair and A9 delta floors are unchanged (scenario-fixed). S3/S4 numbers are unchanged by the corpus growth (their scenarios do not consume the judged queries; S4's fingerprint pins the queries module and was re-recorded with it).

### Fixed

- **#170 — lease-reclaim for stuck `processing` rows** (`src/mnemos/pipeline/refine.py`, `src/mnemos/storage/sqlite_store.py`, `src/mnemos/manager.py`) — rows claimed by `claim_for_refinement` (CAS pending→processing) and left in `processing` after a worker crash were never reclaimed. Fix: `REFINE_LEASE_TIMEOUT_SEC = 600` (single-worker, covers any stub digest with margin; configurable); `claim_for_refinement` now stamps `updated_at` as the lease clock start (without it, a long-queued row would be reclaimed from under a live worker); `reclaim_stale_processing()` per-row CAS (`WHERE id AND pipeline_state='processing' AND updated_at < cutoff`) in the sweeper loop, WARNING audit `pipeline: id=… outcome=lease-reclaimed age=…s`; retry budget untouched (not a failure). Concurrent-double-reclaim exactly-once (threading.Barrier test). Acceptance: `TestLeaseReclaim` (7 tests, mutation: lease-CAS removal → 5 failures).
- **#171 — danger gate on admissible content edits** (`src/mnemos/manager.py`) — `update()` edit-branch was gated only when `status == PUBLISHED`; dirty content edit of a `PROCESSED` row (also admissible) stayed visible. Fix: edit-trigger extended to all admissible statuses (`is_context_admissible`); dirty edit → demotion to RAW with the NEW content gated (B1 invariant: demotions never write `pipeline_state`, pinned). Flip-branch deliberately stays PUBLISHED-only — full extension breaks the knowledge-pipeline rewrite contract (caught by full suite, pinned by `test_flip_to_processed_is_not_the_gate_seam`). Clean edit of admissible row requeues `pipeline_state=pending` (from=refined/none). Acceptance: `TestN1ProcessedEditGate` (8 tests, mutation: PUBLISHED-only trigger → 2 failures).
- **#193 — same-transaction `clean_content` reset on content change** (`src/mnemos/manager.py`) — `update()` replaced content but left stale `clean_content`; served projection (`effective_content = clean_content or content`) lagged until re-filter. Fix: `content_changed` → `memory.clean_content = None` in the same `save()` transaction; effective_content serves new text immediately. Deliberate non-refilter: `apply_context_filter` builds from `raw_content or content` and would rebuild the OLD projection for immutable-source rows (carded as #202). Acceptance: `TestUpdateResetsCleanContent` (3 tests) + BF-1 scenario `filter_projection_stale_after_update` now `False` (s1.json re-recorded honestly; #193 tripwire).

## [3.1.0] - 2026-09-01

MINOR — two additive, non-breaking changes: the npm/π distribution channel (new package surface, no core changes) and an integration stamp-placement fix (self-heal after frontmatter). No API breaks; no migration required.

### Added

- **npm/π distribution channel** — `mnemos-pi` package published under three names (`mnemos-pi`, `@korrlabs/mnemospi`, `@korrlabs/mnemos-pi`) via OIDC trusted publishing (no long-lived npm token). `package.json` declares the π extension entry points; `scripts/sync-version.mjs` keeps `package.json` version synced from `pyproject.toml` (single source of truth); `scripts/publish-all.mjs` publishes all three names idempotently; `.github/workflows/publish-npm.yml` triggers on `v*` tags.
- **LICENSE** — MIT license file added for npm packaging.

### Fixed

- **Integration stamp placement** — the self-heal stamp is now placed after frontmatter in integration templates (`integrations/instructions/*`, `integrations/skills/*`), closing the skill-loading bug where frontmatter parsing broke on stamp-injected files. `src/mnemos/cli/integration.py` refactored for correct stamp insertion; `tests/test_integration.py` extended with coverage for the stamp-after-frontmatter case.

## [3.0.0] - 2026-08-31

The publication-engine major: the ADR-0019 optimistic-publication engine is a landmark engine shift and now carries the MAJOR it earns — immediate visibility as the honest server default (`mnemos.visibility immediate|curated`), fail-closed ingest/publish danger gates with audit, the async `pipeline_state` refine engine with transactional swap, reason-neutral retraction, terminal quarantine. Versioning per owner policy v2 (directive `5a5ac447`, superseding the `b0a1cf40` major clause): majors mark engine landmarks, plan completions arrive as minors; the earlier `v3.0.0 → 2.15.0` rollback was SemVer-incorrect (owner concurred) and 2.15.0 stays published as the historical minor. For the detailed engine content records see [2.15.0] below.

### Added
